# CITAR 0.1.6, Phase 4: the Rust helper, CPU sharing, proof of work (design)

**Status.** Design, 2026-10-14, for Phase 4 of `ops/plan-0.1.6.md` §7, written against `v0.1.6` at `1d5ca71` (Phase 3 complete and merged at `733345f`, with the CI fix `914abb8` and the side task "a closed connection ends a helper request quietly" on top). Sections are numbered P4.x, so they cannot be confused with DESIGN.md's Phase 1 (§1-§12), Phase 2 (P2.x) or `design/phase3-features.md` (P3.x). Appendix E is the work breakdown: fifteen packages, run strictly one after another, one agent at a time on this laptop (the owner's instruction, kept from Phase 3: "overall faster and had fewer problems"). Each package appends an "As built in 4-xx" note to the section it implements, as Phase 3's did, and records its wall-clock start and end.

**Revised the same day in the design's fix round** (P4.12, the review log): the hub now carries exactly one protocol at every merge (it stays v1 until the package that deletes the Python worker switches it to v2 only, P4.11); jobs have their own compatibility key and typed, budget-free specs (P4.4); verification decides replication only after a result is in, uses only the server and administrator-marked verifiers, and withdraws everything unverified a caught machine ever gave (P4.5.8); the protocol acknowledges results and expires offers (P4.2.5); the update hand-off, the services' exit codes, the Windows service's account and the local CPU switch were settled (P4.3).

This file lives in `design/`, outside `docs/`, so mkdocs and the wiki generator never see it. It names code paths in backticks rather than linking them, so `scripts/check_links.py` needs no change.

**Sources:**
- `ops/plan-0.1.6.md` §1 (R1, R4, C1-C4 and the trust-tier defaults under the table), §2 (the defects list), §4.5-4.8 (the faulty-helper QA), §6.6 (the ledger), §6.7 (protocol v2, the helper binary, CPU jobs and limits, proof of work), §7 Phase 4 (five parts and the exit) and Phase 5 (what packaging builds).
- `ops/review-0.1.6/pool-queue-workers.json` (the helper, the hub, packaging, the CPU-sharing and proof-of-work questions and their suggested defaults), `jobs-bench-lab-reports.json` (the lab as the job unit, verification, the Windows-only runner), `build-ci-tests.json`. Every finding used here was checked again against `1d5ca71`; the ones that no longer hold are in P4.1.5.
- `design/phase3-features.md`: P3.1.3 (Phase 4's hooks), P3.3.3 (draining), P3.3.4 (generation time bounded by observation, `flags.overreports`), P3.3.10, P3.4.2-P3.4.3 (`compute_events.verification`, the lab's CPU events), P3.8 and the as-built note of 3-18 (capabilities, modes, the last scan, `machine_settings`), P3.9 (the cross-cutting rules this design keeps), and 3-19's "Left for later".
- `crates/citar-engine/DESIGN.md` §2.1 (the `citar-helper` row), §4.10 (the digest and the chain), §9.6 (the determinism matrix), P2.2 (the crate graph, the build id), P2.4.1 ("a Python-free lab job is Phase 4's").
- `ops/p3-notes.md` (the CI and timing conventions: `perf_counter` for durations, `TZ=UTC0`, the Python 3.12 venv `C:/dev/venv/py312-check`, CI slack x3 for speed budgets, `fsutil.newest_first`, never sleep-then-assert) and `ops/phase1-common.md` (the build environment).
- The code each part touches (P4.0 cites it line by line), and a read-only probe of this laptop's WSL (Ubuntu 24.04, systemd 255 as PID 1, `systemctl --user` reachable for `claude`, cgroup v2 with `cpu memory pids` delegated to the user manager, NAT networking, Rust 1.98.1 in `~/.cargo/bin`, Python 3.12.3).

## P4.0 Facts the design leans on (checked against the tree at `1d5ca71`)

**The helper today is the Python worker** (`citar/worker/`, about 950 lines):
- **Protocol v1** (`citar/worker/protocol.py`): JSON text frames with `t`, `PROTOCOL_VERSION = 1` (`:39`) matched exactly by the server; worker frames `hello catalog done chunk error pong status`, server frames `welcome request cancel ping shutdown` (`:42-55`); refusals `busy closed unknown_model stopping` (`:59`); an 8 MB frame cap (`:61`). A request carries the whole message list, tools, `tool_mode`, `params`, a timeout and the activity (`:133-144`); `Done` carries text, thinking, parsed tool calls, `stop_reason`, `malformed`, usage and `duration` (`:148-158`). `CHUNK` and `STATUS` are never sent.
- **The agent** (`citar/worker/agent.py`): parses tool calls itself by reusing `OpenAIConversation` with `system=""` and the server's message list pasted in (`:301-341`), so a `json` tool mode's protocol prompt, which only `OpenAIConversation.__init__` appends (`openai_provider.py:44-47`), never reaches the model: the plan's "JSON tool mode is broken through helpers" still holds. Server `params` are whitelisted to `temperature max_tokens reasoning_effort top_p seed` (`:55`) and a configured model list is an enforced allowlist (`:234-243`). `CANCEL` is only logged (`:414-415`). Models are discovered once per connection (`:160-190`) and no catalog frame is ever sent. The hardware scan runs synchronously inside the async session before connecting (`:192-220, 348-353`). Reconnects back off from 2 s to 60 s with 30% jitter (`:47-50, 423-456`); a refused token stops it for good (`:434-440`).
- **Configuration** (`citar/worker/__main__.py`): `--config FILE`, else `helper.json` in the per-user base (`paths._user_base()`: `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CITAR`, `~/Library/Application Support/CITAR`, `$XDG_DATA_HOME/citar` or `~/.local/share/citar`; `CITAR_DATA_DIR` overrides; `:74-77`), a first-run prompt on a TTY that offers to save `helper.json` (`:80-104`), `CITAR_WORKER_*` environment variables, and flags `--server --token --config --base-url --provider --api-key-env --max-concurrent --name --model --quiet --insecure --no-hardware --hardware FILE -v` (`:156-186`); quiet hours as `22:00-06:00` or `Sat 00:00-09:00`, split at midnight (`:38-71`). `citar setup --worker` writes `worker.json` (mode 0600) under the configuration directory and, as root, a system unit `/etc/systemd/system/citar-worker.service` (`citar/wizard/worker.py:150-198`).

**The hub** (`citar/server/workers.py`, the `/ws/worker` route in `citar/server/app.py:2560-2650`):
- One `WorkerConnection` per machine, the newest replacing the older (`workers.py:258-272`); a request bridges from a game thread to the event loop and waits on a `threading.Event` (`:373-413`); `cancel` sends `CANCEL` and ends the caller's wait at once (`:215-229`); frames for resolved ids go to the scheduler as late frames (`:194-213`); `drop` on revoke, disable, delete or suspension sends `shutdown` and closes (`:326-356`); the ping loop checks every connection's token, machine and owner again each round (`:434-468`).
- **Defects found here.** (1) The `worker_auth` rate limit (`ratelimit.py:57`, 20 in 300 s) is recorded only after a failed lookup and its `RateLimited` is swallowed (`app.py:2658-2671`): it never refuses anything, and every attempt costs a token lookup. (2) `request_ip` takes the last `X-Forwarded-For` entry whatever `CITAR_TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS` and `behind_proxy` say (`app.py:2652-2655`), where the HTTP routes use `ratelimit.client_ip` (`ratelimit.py:174-193`): a client with no proxy in front can choose its own address. (3) `WorkerToken.last_ip` (`models.py:519`) is never written. (4) The version check is exact and comes before authentication (`app.py:2580-2586`), which is right for a refusal that names the version.
- `record_connection` keeps each report as the machine's last report and applies it by its modes (`machine_settings.record_report`, 3-18; `workers.py:554-574`).

**The server's model calls** (`citar/agents/providers/worker_provider.py`): a `WorkerConversation` ships messages and unpacks the helper's parsed `Done` (`:92-161`); `close()` asks the hub to cancel (`:176-184`); the provider's tool-call parsing (native calls, `repair_tool_calls`, XML, GLM, plain text including Gemma 4's own syntax, the JSON protocol) is `openai_provider.py:83-336`.

**The scheduler** (`citar/pool/scheduler.py`) already has every hook P3.3.10 promised: capacity and swaps from `servers.machine_settings` (`:229-244`), the helper's state pushed by the hub (`helper_state`, `:247-262`), every request carrying its own id, and **draining** for a cancelled v1 request until its late frame or its timeout plus 15 s (`:46-53, 1025-1040`); the over-report rule (`:128`, `flag_overreports`).

**Machine settings** (`citar/machine_settings.py`, 3-18): `capabilities.cpu = {enabled, threads, cpu_percent, memory_mb, work}` with `work` a subset of `CPU_WORK = ("lab_game", "ranking_game", "reference_game")` (`:54`), 0 meaning "left to the helper" (`machine_settings.js:108-110`); hardware, models and power modes with the last report kept; `servers.machine_settings(id)` returns slots, swaps and the CPU limits "Phase 4's welcome and settings frames will carry" (`servers.py:773-792`). `servers.trust_tier` and `users.trust_tier` exist, admin-set (`models.py:99, 190-191, 496`); `servers.flags.overreports` is set by the scheduler.

**The ledger** (`citar/ledger.py`, 3-10): `compute_events.verification` is one of `unverified verified pending` (`models.py:720`); model work is `unverified`; `ingest_lab` reads the lab's results files and writes one `cpu` event per game **on the host**, `verified`, whoever played it (`ledger.py:1132-1236`).

**The lab** (`citar/lab.py`): experiments are files (`saves/lab/queue`, `results/NAME.jsonl`, `done/`); `game_spec(exp, i)` is the job unit (`:277-297`); `play()` drives `engine_api.run_game` and builds the result line in Python from the events it hears (`:314-406`); the runner is a separate process (`python -m citar.lab run`) that starts one Python subprocess per game (`:554-670`), adopts orphans through PowerShell and kills them with `taskkill`, Windows only (`:496-538`), honours the host's restricted hours (`:424-430`) and retries a crash three times. Results feed the ratings (`citar/bots/ratings.py`, Bradley-Terry over every result, cached on the files' signature, `:290-335`). The plan's `ranking_game` and `reference_game` have no runner: the dialog names them (`machine_settings.js:17-18`) and nothing else does.

**The engine side is ready for proof of work:**
- **The digest and the chain** exist (DESIGN.md §4.10; `crates/citar-engine/src/save/chain.rs`): `Digest = blake3("CITAR-DIGEST" ‖ CANON_V1 ‖ RulesetId ‖ canon(State))`; `chain_0 = blake3("CITAR-CHAIN" ‖ spec_hash)`, `chain_t = blake3(chain_{t-1} ‖ turn_le ‖ digest_t)`, taken at the end of each round, opt-in through `Game::set_chain` (`game/turn/driver.rs:163-172`), about 3 ms a round at gargantuan.
- **The build id** (P2.2.1): `citar_bot::build_id(rules)`, 12 hex digits over the engine's and the bot's content codes and the ruleset's id (`crates/citar-bot/src/lib.rs:245-260`); the same on every target, since it hashes sources, data and locked dependency versions.
- **The determinism matrix** already runs every golden set on five targets, macOS x64 included (`macos-15-intel`; `.github/workflows/determinism.yml:83-91`); the `bot` set holds each round's digest of bot games, so a target that plays differently shows there.
- **The runner** (`citar-sim`): `Runner` and `run_game` (P2.4.1), the baseline writer's `play_one(cx, spec) -> BaselineLine` (`baseline/play.rs:212`); citar-sim and citar-store are libraries "so the helper can link them" (DESIGN.md P2.1.2).
- DESIGN.md §2.1 reserves `citar-helper | bin | 4 | Links the engine and the bot`; P2.4.1: "A Python-free lab job is Phase 4's, when the helper needs one and its result format is redesigned with signatures"; §7.5: "Denormals flushed in the host process: citar-py gets a canary; if it fails, results are marked as not verifiable for proof of work".
- `cargo xtask check` holds each workspace crate to its row of the crate graph (`xtask/src/check/graph.rs:18-28`), the engine to its dependency allow-list (`deps.rs:17-29`), the shipped crates away from `test-ops` and `legacy`, and `crates/citar-py/src` free of `unsafe`; the workspace denies `unsafe_code` (`Cargo.toml`, `[workspace.lints.rust]`).

**Hardware and power:** `citar/hwinfo.py` (standard library, psutil when present; `collect()` `:354-395`, the `citar-hardware` format, `COLLECTOR_VERSION = 1`, `guess_power` `:327-344`), the no-Python collectors `citar/data/collectors/collect_hardware.{ps1,sh}`, and the host's `usage.PowerSampler` (`usage.py:405`), which writes one-minute power rows per machine that costing reads. Helpers send no power.

**Packaging:** the release workflow fails at its first step for any version up to 0.1.6 ("Release packaging is Phase 5", `release.yml:31-56`); its `build-helper` job bundles `citar.worker` with PyInstaller into four unversioned assets (`release.yml:186-243`); `citar/web/js/helper.js` links the same four from `releases/latest/download/<name>` and tells Intel Mac owners to use pipx (`:10-19, 67`); `pyproject.toml` has the `worker` extra and the `citar-worker` script (`:62, 93`); `install.sh --worker` and `install.ps1 -Worker` install the Python worker; the Homebrew formula installs the whole game (R4 says the tap ships only the helper; Phase 5).

**Found by the design's review** (checked against `1d5ca71`):
- **The build id does not cover everything a job computes.** `citar_bot::build_id` hashes the engine's and the bot's content codes and the ruleset id only (`crates/citar-bot/src/lib.rs:248-260`); only the engine and the bot have content-code build scripts. citar-sim's runner changes games too (a seat with no driver passes, an unanswered chat expires: `crates/citar-sim/src/runner.rs:290-310`), and so does the build flavour: the `ci` profile turns on debug assertions and overflow checks (`Cargo.toml`, `[profile.ci]`), the engine and the bot hold 127 `debug_assert!`s and 6 `cfg(debug_assertions)` blocks, and `cargo xtask develop` builds `ci` by default (`xtask/src/develop.rs:339`).
- **The baseline's options are not a job spec.** citar-sim's `Options` (`baseline/spec.rs:44-75`) carries a directory, workers, a name, `max_minutes`, `smoke` and `checks`; `play_one` runs under a wall-clock budget (`baseline/play.rs:275`, `runner.rs:276-279`) that turns a slow game into a crash line; its lines carry `seconds`, `cpu_s` and crash traces. The lab's result line carries `errors`, which holds `Crash::record()` texts with traces (`lab.py:341, 368, 405`; `runner.rs:116-118`).
- **Grants:** `admission._purpose_ok` treats an empty purposes list as every purpose, "the common case" (`citar/pool/admission.py:124-127`), and administrators bypass grants altogether (`:156`).
- **The 0.1.5 system unit** that `citar setup --worker` installs restarts the helper with `Restart=always`, `RestartSec=15` (`citar/wizard/worker.py:167-189`).
- **`helper.js`** guesses `arm64` for every Mac without client hints (`citar/web/js/helper.js:24-30`), which Safari and Firefox never send.
- **This laptop:** WSL's networking mode is `nat` and `claude` has `Linger=no`; `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CITAR\config\servers.json` exists beside the repository's `config/servers.json` (S10 fingerprints both).

**Docs:** `docs/server/WORKERS.md` ("What the server can and cannot do", `:299-311`), `SECURITY.md` (worker tokens in scope; no threat model for CPU jobs or updates), `KNOWN_ISSUES.md` ("A cancelled request still runs on a helper", "CPU sharing waits for the next helper", "There is no helper build for Intel Macs", "Energy on helper machines is estimated").

## P4.1 Goals, non-goals and exit

### P4.1.1 What Phase 4 delivers

1. **Protocol v2** (P4.2): one normative specification, typed frames on both sides, and conformance fixtures both sides run.
2. **The Rust helper** (`citar-helper`, P4.3): the same asset names plus Intel Mac, the same configuration files, flags and first-run prompt, a streaming LLM proxy whose cancel really stops generation and which returns raw completions, the hardware scan and power samples, CPU jobs under hard limits where the operating system allows, a machine key, signed results, signed self-update, and service installation per OS.
3. **Jobs in Rust** (`citar-jobs`, P4.4): a closed, typed vocabulary of bot-only work (lab games, ranking games, reference games), a Python-free lab game, the state-hash chain, canonical result hashes, signed claims, and one job host process that runs jobs under limits for both the helper and the server.
4. **The server side** (P4.5): the hub on v2 with tool-call parsing on the server, the job dispatcher with local and remote executors replacing the lab's Windows-only subprocess runner, results into the lab, the ratings and the ledger, trust tiers with replication, sampling, promotion, quarantine and withdrawal, the update relay, and the admin UI.
5. **The Python worker retired**, and a 0.1.5 helper told plainly to upgrade (P4.2.8).
6. **A threat model** (P4.6) written into `SECURITY.md`, and `WORKERS.md` rewritten.

### P4.1.2 Owner decisions that bind this phase

From the plan (§1) and the owner's instructions for Phase 4:
- **R1:** one workspace; `citar-helper` links the same engine and bot. **R4:** the Homebrew tap ships only the prebuilt helper (Phase 5).
- **C1:** CPU sharing is bot-only work in 0.1.6: lab games, bot rankings, reference games.
- **C2:** trust tiers: re-run until N = 5 match, then about 10% sampled, promotion to trusted after 50 verified jobs with no mismatch, a mismatch quarantines; results signed with ed25519 with a per-turn state-hash chain; LLM work attributed, not re-verified.
- **C3:** limits set on the site per machine, enforced by the helper, capped by a local ceiling (local always wins), hard where the OS allows.
- **C4:** a Rust helper replaces the Python worker with the same asset names plus an Intel Mac build; protocol v2 with raw completions parsed on the server; real cancel; job frames; the hardware scan with auto/manual per section; signed self-update; one machine = one row with LLM and CPU capabilities.
- **Priorities:** stability and speed first; fix bugs rather than carry them; no users yet, so protocol, schema and data may break freely; strictly one agent at a time; packages sized for one sitting with local, re-runnable gates; CI-only gates named.
- **Safety** (the owner's rules, P4.7.1): no system service, scheduled task, startup entry, system or security setting, or elevation on this laptop; never the owner's real helper configuration or data directory; never a private signing key in the repository; never a test helper against `citar.jimmieprodgers.com` or any non-local server; small, short CPU-limit tests; never port 8799 or `C:/dev/citar-play`; never load, unload or swap LM Studio's models or change its settings; never push; never the VPS; the main checkout is only read.

### P4.1.3 Non-goals

- **Phase 5:** the release workflow's gate removal, the signed manifest's real key and who holds it (an owner question), attestations and the dry run; the Homebrew tap formula for the helper; Scoop and winget; install scripts that download the helper; code signing and notarisation of the binaries. Phase 4 leaves the hooks (P4.10).
- **Phase 6:** the VPS, the owner's Framework Desktop switching to the new helper (the runbook is P4.2.8's).
- **Not in 0.1.6:** games with LLM seats on helpers (C1), model calls relayed from CPU jobs, GPU compute jobs, streaming completions to the server (v1's unused `CHUNK`), canary prompts for LLM work (optional in plan §6.7; the hook is a ticket class), several helpers on one machine row (the newest connection still wins), protocol v1 (P4.2.8).

### P4.1.4 Exit criteria

| Criterion | Measured by |
|---|---|
| The helper passes conformance on every target | The shared transcripts (P4.2.9) from the helper's side (`cargo nextest -p citar-helper -p citar-jobs`) and the hub's side (Python), and the end-to-end tests of the real binary against the real hub, on Windows x64 and on Linux x64 in WSL locally; `helper.yml` on macOS arm64, macOS x64, Linux arm64 (and again on Windows and Linux x64) CI-only; the `jobs` golden set on all five targets in `determinism.yml`, CI-only. |
| A remote lab experiment is verified end to end | `qa_phase4.py exit`: a server in WSL, a Windows helper and a WSL helper (each in its own home, under limits), a member's experiment granted `lab` plus ranking and reference games; every accepted result signed, its chain consistent, replicated or sampled as its machine's tier says, written once to the lab's results, rated, and in the ledger as given by the machine's owner with the right verification; the Windows and Linux results of replicated jobs equal byte for byte. |
| A faulty helper gets quarantined | The same run's third helper, built with `test-faults`: caught at its first altered result while `new` (evidence "same chain, different result: tampering or an incompatible build"), its owner refused CPU work on any machine. In a second pass a helper that alters only chosen jobs (`result-at:`) reaches `provisional` and is caught by sampling; every unverified result it ever gave is withdrawn, including one accepted before a later verified match: removed from the results file, re-run elsewhere, its ledger events `rejected` with no credits and their budget settlements reversed, and the ratings recomputed without them. |
| The LLM proxy is real, and so is cancel | `qa_phase4.py real`: LM Studio's `google/gemma-4-e2b` (checked loaded before and after, never loaded, unloaded or swapped) through the Windows helper: a short game with an LLM seat and a hybrid seat, chats answered by the models' own calls parsed on the server; a long generation cancelled at 2 s is acknowledged within 1 s and the next short request takes no longer than the baseline measured in the same run; no slot drains. |
| CPU limits hold | Per OS (P4.3.6, P4.4.3; 4-08's and 4-10's gates): Windows' Job Object and Linux's cgroup scope cap CPU and memory hard; the fallbacks and macOS report themselves soft, and the dialog says so. |
| Updates are signed | `qa_phase4.py update`: a throwaway key generated at run time; an old build updates to a new one; every tampering is refused with the binary unchanged. |
| Services install per OS | WSL: `systemd --user` install, start, connect, stop, uninstall, in one invocation; a revoked helper under its supervisor does not loop; the other OSes' generated definitions (restart rules, exit codes, the Windows service's account and ACL) match expected files; Windows' real service and macOS launchd CI-only. |
| The Python worker is gone | No `citar.worker`, no `citar-worker`, no `worker` extra; a v1 hello gets the upgrade message and close code 4426. |
| Access is closed by default | `audit_routes.py --strict` 0 unguarded; every new route's stranger, owner, admin, moderator and visitor tests. |
| Nothing of the owner's was touched | S10 (P4.7.5) before and after every package: the fingerprints of the owner's data, no helper or job host left running, no service, task or startup entry anywhere on Windows, no `citar` unit left in WSL. |
| It all works together | `qa_phase4.py all` exits 0; the browser checklist (P4.7.7) passes with screenshots; S1-S10 green, recorded in "As built in 4-15". |

### P4.1.5 What changed since the plan was written

Each checked against `1d5ca71`:
1. **Much of §6.7's site side exists** (3-18): `capabilities.cpu`, the three modes, the last scan, classes, `trust_tier` on users and machines, `flags.overreports`, `machine_settings()`. Phase 4 adds the frames that carry them and the automation that acts on them.
2. **The chain and the cross-target matrix exist** (Phases 1-2), macOS x64 included. Proof of work needs a job runner that turns the chain on and a hash of the result, not new engine machinery.
3. **The lab's game is still played in Python** around `run_game` (P2.4.1 left it to Phase 4): 4-07 ports its result building to Rust, which the helper and the server's own executor then share.
4. **The lab runner is a separate process** with Windows-only orphan handling; the plan's "dispatcher replacing the lab's subprocess manager" becomes a dispatcher inside the server and a job host process per executor (P4.5.5-P4.5.6).
5. **The VPS starts Phase 6 on a fresh database** (plan §7 Phase 6), so the owner's 0.1.5 helper's token will not exist on the 0.1.6 server whatever the protocol: keeping v1 alive would buy that machine nothing (P4.2.8).
6. **Defects found on the way**, fixed in Phase 4: the worker-auth rate limit that never refuses, the socket address that trusts `X-Forwarded-For` unconditionally, `last_ip` never written (P4.0), the JSON tool mode through helpers (the plan's §2 item, still open), the catalog never re-sent, `ingest_lab` charging all lab CPU to the host as `verified`.
7. **`verification` has no `rejected`** (plan §6.6 lists it): added (P4.5.7).
8. **Python's standard library has neither ed25519 nor blake3**, and canonical JSON is easy to get subtly wrong across languages: hashing, claims, signatures and manifests are checked by the same Rust code on both sides, the server through `citar._engine` (P4.4.4).
9. **Ranking and reference games had no definition:** P4.4.1 gives them one.
10. **The build id is not a jobs compatibility key, and the baseline's options are not a spec** (P4.0, the review's findings): jobs get `jobs_build` (P4.4.5) and typed specs that run with no wall-clock budget (P4.4.1).

## P4.2 Protocol v2

`crates/citar-helper/PROTOCOL.md` is the normative text (4-01); this section is its summary. The fixtures (P4.2.9) are its executable form.

### P4.2.1 Transport, framing and versioning

- **Transport:** the helper dials out to `wss://<server>/ws/worker` (`ws://` for loopback and plain-HTTP servers), as v1 did, so a v1 helper's attempt still reaches a server that can tell it to upgrade. One WebSocket per machine row; the newest connection wins (`shutdown`, code `replaced`, to the older).
- **One machine is one row** (C4): one helper process, one connection, one token and one key carry both capabilities, LLM and CPU; a machine with no model endpoint runs the helper with `--no-llm` and lends CPU only, and one that lends no CPU says so locally or on the site.
- **Frames:** UTF-8 JSON text frames, each an object with `t` (the type) and `ts` (the sender's Unix time, informational only). At most 8 MB a frame (v1's cap); a `job_result` is well under 1 MB.
- **Versioning:** `hello.protocol = 2`; the server accepts the protocols in `HUB_PROTOCOLS = (2,)`. Inside v2 a change must be additive: a new frame type or a new optional field. Both sides ignore frame types they do not know (logged once per type at debug) and fields they do not know. A breaking change is v3, and whether a release then accepts both is that release's own decision (0.1.6's answer for v1 is no, P4.2.8); a server refuses a protocol it does not speak with P4.2.8's refusal, which every helper from v2 on reads as `refused` code `protocol` and handles by waiting for its own update (P4.3.8). **Job specs are the exception:** they are typed and closed (unknown keys refused), because they decide what code runs and what it computes (P4.4.1).
- **Ids:** request ids (16 hex, the scheduler's), job ids `cj_…` and run ids `cr_…` (the server's), all opaque to the helper.

### P4.2.2 The handshake

**`hello`** (helper → server, first frame, within 30 s of the connection):

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `protocol` | 2 |
| `token` | the worker token (never logged; v1's redaction) |
| `helper` | `{version, build, jobs_build, target, label}`: the helper's semver version, its engine build id (`citar_bot::build_id`, shown on the card), its jobs compatibility key (P4.4.5), the Rust target triple, `git describe` or `unknown` |
| `machine` | `{hostname, name, os, arch, public_key}`: `name` the owner's `--name`; `public_key` the machine key, base64 of 32 bytes (P4.3.7) |
| `llm` | `{enabled, provider, slots, stream, models: [{key, label, context, loaded}]}`; `enabled` false for a CPU-only helper (`--no-llm`) |
| `cpu` | `{enabled, ceiling: {threads, cpu_percent, memory_mb, work}, limits: {cpu, memory}, verifiable, bots, ruleset}`: the local switch (off unless the machine's owner turned it on, P4.3.2) and the local ceiling (P4.3.6), how hard this OS can hold each limit (`"hard"` or `"soft"` each), the answer of the canary and the self-check (P4.4.3), the bot versions compiled in, the ruleset id |
| `report` | `{hardware, hardware_source}`: the scan (P4.3.5) or the owner's file, as 3-18's report; `hardware_source` is `"scan"`, `"file"` or `"none"` |
| `running` | the run ids the helper is still playing from before a reconnect |
| `results` | the run ids whose results it holds unacknowledged (P4.2.5) |
| `updates` | `auto`, `notify` or `off`: the helper's local policy |
| `quiet_until` | when the helper's own quiet hours in force end, or null |

**The server's answer** is `welcome` or `refused`, then nothing else until the helper has its welcome (3-05's ordering, kept).

`welcome`: `{protocol: 2, machine: {id, name}, settings, ping_s, server: {version, build, jobs_build}, update, jobs: {keep: [...], drop: [...], resend: [...]}}`: `settings` as P4.2.3; `update` the signed manifest the server relays, or null (P4.3.8); `jobs.keep` and `jobs.drop` say which of the helper's `running` runs are still assigned to it (the others it aborts), and `jobs.resend` which of its held `results` the server still wants (the others it deletes).

`refused`: `{code, message, retry_after_s?}`; the server closes the socket after it. The helper's reaction is part of the contract:

| `code` | Why | The helper |
|---|---|---|
| `token` | unknown or revoked token, machine disabled or deleted, owner not active | a **permanent refusal**: says to issue a new token (`citar-helper setup` asks for one); from a console it exits 78; as a service it idles, logs once, and tries again hourly (P4.3.9), so no service manager restarts it into a loop |
| `key` | the machine already has another key registered | a permanent refusal, as `token`, saying the owner must reset the machine's key on the Servers page (P4.5.1) |
| `protocol` | the server does not speak v2: a 0.1.5 server answers a v2 hello with a v1 `error` frame and close code 4426, and a 0.1.6 server answers any hello whose `protocol` is not 2 the same way (P4.2.8); the helper reads either as `protocol` | waits: retries every 10 minutes, logging that the server must be upgraded or its own update applied; never exits on it |
| `rate` | too many refused tokens from this address | retries after `retry_after_s` |
| `build` | reserved for v3 | as `protocol` |

**Server-side checks, in order** (P4.5.1): the protocol (P4.2.8's refusal, which costs nothing and must reach a 0.1.5 helper in the one shape it reads), the address's rate limit (before any lookup), the token, then the key.

### P4.2.3 Settings, catalog, hardware, power and status

- **`settings`** (server → helper, in `welcome` and pushed on every change of the machine's settings): `{llm: {enabled, slots, allow_swaps}, cpu: {enabled, threads, cpu_percent, memory_mb, work, yield_to_llm}}`, from `servers.machine_settings(id)`. `0` for a CPU figure means "the helper's ceiling".
- **`settings_ack`** (helper → server, after each `settings`): `{effective: {llm: {enabled, slots}, cpu: {enabled, threads, cpu_percent, memory_mb, work, yield_to_llm}}, capped: [field, ...], limits: {cpu, memory}}`: what the helper will actually do, each figure `min(site, ceiling)`, `capped` naming where its ceiling won. The scheduler's capacity and the dispatcher's threads read `effective`, never the site's figures alone (C3: local always wins), and never more than the site's either: the hub clamps each acknowledgement to what it asked (each number the smaller, `work` the kinds both list, model or CPU work on only when both say so) and flags one that claims more `settings_overclaim` (PROTOCOL.md; 4-01's fix round).
- **`catalog`** (helper → server, whenever the endpoint's model list or loaded set changes; the helper polls it every 30 s): `{models: [...]}` as in `hello.llm.models`. 3-18's `record_report` applies it by the models mode, and the scheduler's loaded-model rule follows it at once.
- **`hardware`** (helper → server, when the scan changed, and after a `rescan`): `{scan, source}`. Kept as the last report and applied by the hardware and power modes (3-18, unchanged).
- **`rescan`** (server → helper): scan now (the dialog's "Rescan now", P4.5.11).
- **`power`** (helper → server, once a minute when it can measure anything): `{samples: [{t, gpu_w, cpu_util}]}`, from 10-second readings. Written as the machine's power rows (`usage`'s `power-<machine>-YYYY-MM.jsonl` format, P4.5.7); they are measurements, so the power mode (which governs the static figures) does not gate them.
- **`status`** (helper → server, on change, at most every 5 s): `{quiet_until, llm_in_flight, jobs_running, jobs_paused}`. The scheduler treats a machine whose helper is in its own quiet hours like one in the site's quiet hours (tickets refused as temporary with `retry_at`), instead of learning it from `closed` refusals as v1 forced it to.

### P4.2.4 Completions

- **`request`** (server → helper): `{id, model, messages, tools, params, timeout_s}`. `messages` is the whole OpenAI-format list, system prompt included (and, in `json` tool mode, the JSON protocol appended by the server: the fix of the plan's defect); `tools` OpenAI function definitions, or null; `params` at most `temperature max_tokens top_p seed reasoning_effort` (anything else is dropped by the helper, logged once). The helper decides the endpoint, the key and whether to stream; nothing in a request can change them.
- **`done`** (helper → server): `{id, message: {content, reasoning, tool_calls: [{id, type, function: {name, arguments}}]}, finish_reason, usage: {prompt_tokens, completion_tokens, reasoning_tokens, cached_tokens} | null, gen_s, ttft_s, streamed}`. The completion as the endpoint returned it, assembled from the stream when streaming: **nothing is parsed or repaired on the helper** (P4.5.2). `gen_s` is from sending the HTTP request to its last byte; `ttft_s` to the first token; `usage` null when the endpoint gave none.
- **`error`** (helper → server): `{id, code, message, retryable}` with `code` one of `busy` (at its local slots), `closed` (local quiet hours), `unknown_model` (outside its allowlist), `stopping`, `endpoint` (the local endpoint failed or refused), `timeout`, `bad_request`.
- **`cancel`** (server → helper): `{id}`. The helper drops the HTTP response stream, which closes the connection to the endpoint (LM Studio, Ollama, llama.cpp and vLLM stop a streaming generation when its client goes), and only then answers **`cancelled`** `{id, gen_s, tokens_out}`. A request that finished before the cancel arrived has had its `done`; the cancel is then ignored and nothing more is sent.

The server frees the request's slot when `cancelled` (or a crossing `done` or `error`) arrives. Draining stays only as the safety net for a helper that does not acknowledge within `CANCEL_ACK_SECONDS` (5 s): the slot drains as in P3.3.3 and the machine gets `flags.cancel_unacked` (P4.5.3).

**A lost connection cancels every completion in flight on the helper** (the same drop as `cancel`, with nobody to acknowledge to): the hub has already failed those requests, so a generation nobody will read must not hold the GPU, or the helper's local slots after it reconnects (where the server's next requests would meet `busy`). A transcript holds it: the fake endpoint sees its client gone within 1 s of the socket's close.

### P4.2.5 Jobs

| Frame | Direction | Fields |
|---|---|---|
| `job_offer` | server → helper | `job, run, kind, spec, spec_hash, jobs_build, deadline_s, mem_mb, priority, offer_ttl_s` |
| `job_accept` | helper → server | `run` |
| `job_reject` | helper → server | `run, reason`: `busy`, `build` (not its jobs build), `kind` (not in its local work kinds), `quiet`, `disabled`, `limits` (the job host could not be started under the limits), `memory` (its memory per thread is below `mem_mb`), `bad_spec` (refused by validation, or its hash differs), `unverifiable` (the canary or the self-check failed) |
| `job_progress` | helper → server | `run, round, of, elapsed_s, cpu_s, paused_s, peak_mb`: every 10 s or 25 rounds; it is also the run's heartbeat |
| `job_result` | helper → server | `run, outcome: {result, result_hash, chain: {head, rounds, digests}, cpu_s, wall_s, paused_s, peak_mb}, claim, signature` (P4.4.4) |
| `job_result_ack` | server → helper | `run`: sent once the dispatcher has committed the result (or found it a duplicate) |
| `job_failed` | helper → server | `run, code, message, retryable`: `code` `memory` (the memory limit ended it), `deadline`, `host` (the job host died), `aborted_locally` (the owner stopped CPU sharing), `internal` |
| `job_abort` | server → helper | `run, reason` |
| `job_aborted` | helper → server | `run` |
| `jobs_pause` | server → helper | `reason` (`benchmark`): pause every running game, whatever `yield_to_llm` says |
| `jobs_resume` | server → helper | (none) |

A game that panics is a **result**, not a failure: its outcome is the crash record (P4.4.2), which a replica must reproduce. `job_failed` is for the infrastructure. The same frames, without `claim`, `signature`, the acknowledgement and the offer's time to live, are the job host's own protocol on its standard input and output (P4.4.3), so the helper relays them and signs results, and the server's local executor speaks them directly.

**The rules the frames carry** (each with its transcript, P4.2.9):
- **Results are acknowledged.** The helper writes each result to `helper/results/<run>.json` before sending it and deletes the file only on its `job_result_ack`. After a reconnect it lists the held runs in `hello.results`; the welcome's `jobs.resend` names those the server still wants (sent again at once), and the helper deletes the others. A second `job_result` for a run already settled gets another `job_result_ack` when its hashes are identical, and is a `claim` failure (P4.5.8) when they differ.
- **Offers expire.** A run with neither `job_accept` nor `job_reject` within `offer_ttl_s` (30 s) is `expired` and its job offered elsewhere; a late `job_accept` gets `job_abort`.
- **A silent run expires.** A running run with no `job_progress` for 5 minutes while its helper is connected (the clock restarts at each connection and at the server's start), outside a pause the helper reported, is `expired` and its job re-queued; a late `job_result` for it is acknowledged and dropped (not counted, not credited).
- **Re-offers back off** per (machine, reject reason): 2 s doubling to 10 minutes, cleared for `busy` and `quiet` by a `status` that shows room, and for `build`, `kind`, `disabled`, `limits` and `unverifiable` by the next `settings_ack` or hello.
- **Paused time is reported** (`paused_s`, from `yield_to_llm` and `jobs_pause`) and extends the run's deadline on both sides, up to twice the deadline (P4.5.5).

### P4.2.6 Updates

- **`update`** (server → helper, when the server learns of a manifest while the helper is connected): `{manifest, signature}`, as `welcome.update`.
- **`update_status`** (helper → server): `{state: "available"|"downloading"|"verified"|"restarting"|"applied"|"failed"|"refused"|"unavailable", version, message}`. Shown on the Servers page.

### P4.2.7 Keep-alive and shutdown

- `ping`/`pong` every `ping_s` (20 s); a connection is stale after 70 s without a frame (v1's figures). The helper sends its own ping when it has heard nothing for 45 s, so it notices a dead link without waiting for the server.
- **`shutdown`** (server → helper): `{reason, code}` with `code` `revoked`, `disabled`, `deleted`, `suspended`, `replaced`. The helper aborts its jobs and requests and treats it as a permanent refusal (P4.2.2): from a console it exits 78, as a service it idles and tries again hourly. `replaced` means another process holds the machine's token; an update's hand-off never causes one, because the old process closes its socket before the new one dials (P4.3.8).

### P4.2.8 The v1 transition

**A 0.1.6 server refuses a v1 helper; it does not serve it.** The hub accepts only a hello whose `protocol` is 2. Any other hello (0.1.5's, which carries `version: 1` and no `protocol`; one with another `protocol`; one with neither key) gets one v1-shaped `error` frame (`{"t": "error", "id": "", "message": …}`, the shape the 0.1.5 helper reads) with the message below, and close code 4426: no token lookup, no audit row, no rate-limit count, and at most one log line per address an hour. It is the handshake's first check and a single branch, never a second code path (the owner's decision of 2026-10-14, `ops/p3-notes.md`):

> This server runs CITAR 0.1.6, which needs the new CITAR helper (protocol 2). Download it from the Servers page of this server, or from https://github.com/jprodgers/CITAR/releases/latest, and run it with a token issued there.

The 0.1.5 helper run by hand handles this well: its `Rejected` path logs "The server rejected this worker: <message>" and stops instead of retrying (`agent.py:434-440`). **Run as the system unit `citar setup --worker` installed** (`Restart=always`, `RestartSec=15`, P4.0), it is restarted every 15 s into the same refusal until the unit is disabled: cheap for the server (no lookup, throttled logging) but noisy, so the runbook's first step on such a machine is `sudo systemctl disable --now citar-worker`, and `WORKERS.md` says so. **The other order works too:** a v2 helper whose server is still 0.1.5 receives v1's "This server speaks worker protocol v1..." error and close 4426, reads it as `refused` code `protocol`, and waits, retrying every 10 minutes, so it connects by itself once the server is upgraded. Either side can move first; in between, that machine is simply offline, and nothing half-works.

**Why not accept v1 for a transition window:**
1. **It would buy the owner's machine nothing.** Phase 6 starts the VPS on a fresh database (plan §7), so the Framework Desktop's 0.1.5 token does not exist on the 0.1.6 server; the machine must be registered again and given a new token whatever the protocol, and downloading the new helper is one more step in the same sitting.
2. **It would keep two protocols alive in the hub:** parsing on the helper beside parsing on the server, draining as the normal cancel, `busy` as a routine answer, and a second set of tests, all to serve one machine for a few minutes.
3. **There are no other users** (plan §1, revised), and the plan's own rule is to fix rather than carry.
4. **The owner decided it** (2026-10-14, `ops/p3-notes.md`): protocol v2 only, no compatibility layer, no transition period, no grace mode; the Python worker deleted once the Rust helper replaces it. The development order keeps the same rule: the hub carries exactly one protocol at every merge (P4.11).

**The runbook for Phase 6** (into `docs/server/WORKERS.md` in 4-14): deploy the server; register the machine (or keep its imported row) and issue a token; on the machine, stop the 0.1.5 helper (`sudo systemctl disable --now citar-worker` where `citar setup --worker` installed it as a service; otherwise close it), download `citar-helper-<target>` from the Servers page, run `citar-helper setup` (it reads the old `helper.json`'s endpoint, models and quiet hours, asks for the new token, and asks whether to lend CPU, default no); optionally `citar-helper service install`.

### P4.2.9 Conformance fixtures

- **Where:** `crates/citar-helper/protocol/v2/`: `frames/valid/*.json` and `frames/invalid/*.json` (one frame each, an invalid one with the reason it must be refused), and `transcripts/*.json`.
- **A transcript** is a named sequence of steps: `{"s2h": frame}` (the server sends), `{"h2s": pattern}` (the helper must send a frame matching it next), `{"endpoint": {...}}` (the fake model endpoint's next behaviour: a scripted response, a stream of deltas with delays, or an expectation such as `expect_disconnect_within_ms`), `{"host": {...}}` (the fake or real job host's), and `{"state": {...}}` (an assertion on the receiving side's state, read by that side only: for the hub, "the slot is free and nothing drains"; for the helper, "no request in flight"). Patterns use a small matcher language: `"<any>"`, `"<int>"`, `"<number>"`, `"<string>"`, `"<hex64>"`, `"<base64>"`, `"<id:NAME>"` (binds a value and requires it equal wherever the name recurs), `"<golden:SET/NAME>"` (a value from a committed golden file).
- **Both sides run every transcript.** The Rust side (`crates/citar-helper/tests/conformance.rs`) plays the server's frames and the endpoint from an in-process fake server and checks every frame the real helper code sends. The Python side (`tests/test_helper_conformance.py`) plays the helper's frames from a `FakeHelper` against the real hub (uvicorn, a real WebSocket) and checks the hub's frames and its scheduler's and dispatcher's state. A transcript is therefore checked from both ends; a change to the protocol changes a fixture, and both suites must agree with it.
- **Both sides hold the same frame table:** PROTOCOL.md's table of frame types is parsed by a test on each side and compared with the types each side's code knows.

**As built in 4-01: protocol 2's specification, the frame types on both sides, the shared fixtures** (P4.2, P4.3.1's xtask rules; Appendix E). One agent, 2026-10-14, from 05:50 to 07:40 (-04:00), about 110 minutes, on branch `p-4-01` from `2a76076`. Rust changed (a new crate, xtask); the hub, the Python worker and the scheduler are untouched: nothing on the server speaks protocol 2 until 4-05.

- **`crates/citar-helper/PROTOCOL.md`** (886 lines) is the normative text: transport and framing, the value types and checks, versioning, close codes, the handshake with every `refused` code and the helper's reactions and exit codes, settings, catalog, hardware, power, status, completions and cancel, jobs and their rules, updates, keep-alive and shutdown, the v1 refusal, the frame table (34 rows), every frame field by field, and the fixtures with the transcript format and the matcher language. Decisions the design left open, each written there:
  - **Bounds on every value**, matched to what the site already holds (`machine_settings`: slots 0..16, threads 0..1024, memory 0..16,777,216 MB, 500 models, keys of 200 characters): `int` 0..2^53 − 1 and `number` 0..10^15 unless a range is given, `text` 4,000 characters, ids of `A-Z a-z 0-9 _ -` up to 64 (`cj_`/`cr_` for jobs and runs), canonical base64. A correct helper never reaches them (it drops a model with a longer key and truncates labels).
  - **Ten protocol errors with a path** (`too_large`, `too_deep`, `not_json`, `not_object`, `no_type`, `protocol`, `missing`, `type`, `value`, `unknown`), checked in that order. `protocol` is a `hello` whose `protocol` is not exactly the integer 2, checked before its other fields: the hub's "first check" (P4.2.8) is one `except` on that code. What a receiver does with a bad frame (the hub closes a bad first frame with 4400; the helper answers a bad `request` with `bad_request` and a bad `job_offer` with `bad_spec`) is in the text.
  - **JSON as serde_json reads it**, on both sides: an integer literal outside −2^63..2^64 − 1, and `-0`, is a float (never an `int`); `NaN`, `Infinity` and a number beyond a 64-bit float are not JSON, nor is a lone surrogate; the 8 MiB cap counts UTF-8 bytes; nesting is capped at 64, scanned on the raw text before parsing (Python parses first and scans only when the text is not JSON, which gives the same answer).
  - **Direction.** Each side accepts only the other side's types; a type it does not accept from that sender (unknown, or the other direction's) is ignored, not refused. `error` exists in both directions (the helper's request error; the server's v1 refusal, which keeps protocol 1's optional `refusal` and `retryable` because a 0.1.5 server sends them), as do `ping` and `pong`.
  - **Enumerations are closed within protocol 2** (a new reject reason or kind is a breaking change); purely informational strings (`job_abort.reason`, `jobs_pause.reason`) are free text. `capped` lists its names in a fixed order. The job claim is the one closed object (its bytes are signed); whether it agrees with the outcome and the server's records is verification's (4-11), not a frame check.
  - **`jobs_build` may be null** in `hello.helper` and `welcome.server`: the builds of 4-03 to 4-06 have none, and a null jobs build is never offered a job; `job_offer.jobs_build` is always one.
  - **Close codes** keep protocol 1's (4400, 4401, 4403, 4408, 4409, 4426) and add 4429 for `rate`, with the helper's reaction when a close comes with no frame before it. `status` carries counts (`jobs_paused` included); `power.samples[].cpu_util` is a fraction and `gpu_w` all GPUs together, as `usage.PowerSampler` writes its rows.
  - **The server's builders refuse a field the frame does not have** (a typo a receiver would silently drop).
- **One schema for both sides.** The fixtures exercise every field but not every bound, so each side also describes the field tables its code checks as JSON (`check::describe`/`protocol::schema`; `helper_protocol.describe`/`schema`), and a test on each side compares its description with the committed `protocol/v2/schema.json`, type by type (`CITAR_BLESS_SCHEMA=1` rewrites it from the helper's side after a deliberate change). The two sides agreed on the first comparison; a planted one-off bound on either side fails its test.
- **The fixtures** (`protocol/v2/`, 215 files at first, 230 after the fix round below): 43 valid frames (each type's canonical example `<from>.<type>.json` and variants; `expect` where the parse drops a field), 93 invalid frames (one defect each, every code covered, every type with fields has its own; a frame too large to commit is written as `pad_bytes`), 6 ignored frames, and **72 transcripts** with 352 frames: the handshake (every `refused` code, a 0.1.5 server's answer, the v1 refusal of a 0.1.5 hello, a hello for protocol 3 and one with neither key, a bad hello, no hello, the key reset), settings (capped, not lent, pushed, `--no-llm`), catalog, hardware, power, status, completions (streamed tool call, not streamed, usage absent, busy, closed, unknown model, timeout, endpoint error, bad request, the parameter whitelist), cancel (before the first token, mid-stream, after done, unacknowledged), the lost connection, jobs (offer to acknowledgement, the nine reject reasons, abort, deadline, memory failure, reconnects keeping and dropping runs, reconnect after a sent result, a held result not wanted, a duplicate result, a lost offer, a silent run, re-offer backoff, `jobs_pause`), updates (notify, unavailable, pushed, a bad signature), keep-alive, shutdown and the reconnect backoff (the fix round's 87 transcripts with 420 frames are below). 49 are played from both sides; 12 from the helper's only (they script a server that is not the 0.1.6 hub: a 0.1.5 server, a future one, a broken request) and 11 from the hub's only (they script a misbehaving helper: a protocol 1 hello, no hello, a lost offer, a silent run, a duplicate); each says why in `one_side`. `plays` names the package each side plays it from: the helper's side 4-03 (24), 4-04 (14), 4-06 (3), 4-10 (16) and 4-13 (4); the hub's side 4-05 (35), 4-06 (3), 4-10 (19) and 4-13 (3). The fixtures are written by a generator kept outside the repository (the session's scratchpad `p401/gen_fixtures.py`); the committed JSON is the source of truth, edited by hand from here on.
- **The transcript format** extends the design's five step kinds (`s2h`, `h2s`, `endpoint`, `host`, `state`) with `close`, `drop`, `connect`, `wait` (with `quiet`) and `act` (something happening around one side), and the step modifiers `within_s`, `note` and `invalid` (a frame sent broken on purpose, refused with that code). **Background frames** (`ping`, `pong`, `status`, `power`) come on their own clocks and are skipped unless the next step expects their type. Setup, endpoint and host keys are a closed vocabulary that both loaders enforce and a test holds to PROTOCOL.md. The matcher language adds `<bool>`, `<hex12>`, `<hex16>` and ranges (`<int:LO..HI>`, `<number:LO..HI>`) to the design's; a string of a known matcher kind must be well formed, and any other string is a literal. **Realizing** a pattern (the scripted side's frame) takes bindings, then `values`, then golden values, then the frame type's canonical example, then the matcher's default.
- **Rust** (`citar-helper`, a library and a binary): `protocol` (`check.rs`, the field descriptions and the checker; `frames.rs`, every frame written once by the `object!` macro, which makes both the serde struct and its field list, and `wire_enum!` for closed string sets; `mod.rs`, `parse_helper_frame`, `parse_server_frame`, `FrameSet::encode`, the constants and close codes); `conformance` (the fixture loaders, the frame table parser, `matcher.rs`, `transcript.rs`); `version` (`citar-helper --version`: the version, the build id over the embedded ruleset, protocol 2, the target triple from `build.rs`, the label). Features `dev-guard`, `test-faults`, `test-update-key` declared empty, for xtask to hold. The crate is `forbid(unsafe_code)` and has its own relaxed `clippy.toml`, as the host crates do.
- **Python:** `citar/server/helper_protocol.py` (the same tables as Python types, `parse_helper_frame`/`parse_server_frame`, `encode`, a builder for each of the 16 server frames, `helper_frame` for 4-05's fake helper, `v1_refusal()`, `HUB_PROTOCOLS`, the close codes, `ProtocolError(code, path)`, `Ignored`); `tests/helper_fixtures.py` (the server's copy of the loaders, the matcher and the transcript checks, which 4-05's `FakeHelper` will play from).
- **xtask** (P4.3.1): the crate graph gains `citar-helper: [engine, bot, sim, jobs]` and the reserved `citar-jobs: [engine, bot, sim]`, and citar-py may add citar-jobs. A new check, `helper`, refuses an `unsafe` token anywhere in citar-helper and in citar-jobs outside `src/sys/{windows,unix}.rs` (skipped until the crate exists), and the helper crates' `test-faults`, `test-update-key`, `test-jobs` and `dev-guard` (and any feature reaching one) in their default features, in a dependency's feature list outside the test crates, in a feature that forwards to one (unless it is itself one, or citar-py's `test-ops`), in pyproject's maturin features, on any line of `release.yml`, and in `helper.yml`'s jobs whose id starts with `artifact` (4-14 names its artifact jobs so), with `--all-features` too. citar-py's `test-ops` may now add the helper crates' test features to its forward of the engine's. rust.yml's path filters take `release.yml` and `helper.yml`, which the checks read. Each rule bit on the real tree in a planted check (an `unsafe` block in the helper, `dev-guard` in its defaults, `--features test-faults` on a release line).
- **Gates.** (1) Every valid frame parses and serializes back on both sides, every invalid one is refused with its code and path on both, every ignored one ignored (`crates/citar-helper/tests/fixtures.rs`, `tests/test_helper_protocol.py`). (2) The frame table equals the types each side knows (34 rows). (3) 2,000 mutations: proptest with 2,000 cases in `tests/robustness.rs` (no panic; what is accepted reads back as its type) and 2,000 seeded mutations in Python (nothing but `ProtocolError`), each side also checking that the mutations reach the refusals. (4) Every transcript loads on both sides, every one of its 352 frames realizes into a frame that passes its checks and reads back unchanged (no field the protocol does not have), and each matcher accepts and refuses on both sides. The edges both sides share (numbers, the caps, the hello's check first, the v1 refusal's words in PROTOCOL.md and its fixture) are a test on each side. Totals: the helper crate 26 tests (`cargo nextest run -p citar-helper`), `tests/test_helper_protocol.py` 24, xtask 103 (7 new). Reading the transcripts against the protocol showed that `status` and `power` frames would interleave with every scripted exchange; they became background frames.

The `act` and `state` words were each player's own in the first pass; the fix round made them a closed vocabulary (PROTOCOL.md, "Actions" and "State expectations"), so a package that needs another word adds it to PROTOCOL.md and to both loaders, and a player refuses a word it cannot yet do.
- **Fix round** (08:08 to 09:16, -04:00): the review found 2 majors and 7 minors, all valid and all fixed.

Every transcript that reaches a welcome now gives the site's settings (the hub's `machine.settings` and the welcome's `settings`, one object) and the helper's `max_concurrent` and `cpu` (its switch and ceiling), and its `settings_ack` is what PROTOCOL.md's rules make of them, written out, so the fake helper acknowledges what a correct helper would (`completion-busy` no longer leans on the canonical example's two slots). The transcripts a helper plays before 4-04 keep the site's model work off, so their acknowledgement is the same before and after the helper serves models. Every frame the server starts and every drop by the server now follows a hub act: `submit_request`, `force_request` (past the scheduler's checks), `cancel_request`, `drop_connection`, `queue_job`, `force_offer` (past the dispatcher's eligibility), `lab_stop` (holds a re-queued job where a transcript checks it queued), and the existing ones. A test on the server's side holds every hub-played transcript to that, and the act and state words became a closed vocabulary like the setup keys. A `lost` frame step says a frame went with the connection (`job-reconnect-after-send`, and the new `job-result-ack-lost`); `cancel-after-done` orders its steps so both sides see the crossing.

The orchestrator is asked to write this into 4-07's gate 5 and 4-08's gates (`ops/p4-notes.md`).

New transcripts: `close-without-frame-reconnect` (4408), `-permanent-4401`, `-permanent-4403`, `-permanent-4409`, `-protocol` (4426), `-rate` (4429), `close-bad-hello-received` and `job-offer-malformed` on the helper's side; `completion-bad-done`, `job-frame-malformed` and `settings-ack-overclaim` on the hub's; `job-reject-limits`, `job-reject-unverifiable`, `job-failed-host` and `job-result-ack-lost` on both (the first pass's "nine reject reasons" had seven). Not added: `job_failed` `aborted_locally` and `internal`, because how an owner stops CPU sharing on a running helper is not designed yet (left to 4-10).

PROTOCOL.md's "What the helper caps": at most 500 models (its allowlist first, then the loaded ones, then by key), `--name` cut to 120 characters, the hostname to 255, every message to 4,000. A bad hello's 4400 carries the protocol error's code and path as its reason (`helper_protocol.close_reason`, 123 bytes), and the helper logs it.

It reads helper.yml's workflow-wide `env:` and `defaults:`, and it refuses a feature list held in a variable in release.yml and the artifact jobs. Unit tests cover each case, and a planted helper.yml and a planted `default = ["conformance"]` were caught on the real tree (`C:/dev/target/p-4-01-fix-planted.log`).

The crate graph allows a crate to name itself only as a dev-dependency, and xtask refuses `conformance` in shipped builds as it refuses `dev-guard`. `cargo tree` shows the feature on dev edges only, and the release binary holds none of the module's strings.

Numbers given as text are read, `temperature`, `top_p` and `max_tokens` are clamped, and a value that cannot fit is dropped and named. It is ready for 4-05's `WorkerConversation.step`.
