## P4.4 Jobs and proof of work (`citar-jobs`)

A library crate shared by the helper and the server's bindings: the job vocabulary, the Rust lab game, the job host and its limits, hashes, claims and signatures, and the update manifest's verification. Everything that must give the same bytes on both sides of the wire lives here, once.

### P4.4.1 Job kinds and specs

| Kind | What it is | Spec | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| `lab_game` | one game of a member's (or an admin's) lab experiment | `LabSpec`: `lab.game_spec(exp, i)`'s keys, typed (`exp, i, seed, map_type, size, speed, turns, difficulty, barbarians, barbarian_difficulty, nation, city_states, ai_base_values, map_edges, river_density, resources, seats[{label, bot, difficulty, params, aggression, profile, profile_rev, profile_name, levels, nation}]`) | `LabResult`: today's result line, P4.4.2 |
| `ranking_game` | a game of a ranking experiment: the site's own ladder (an experiment with `kind: "ranking"`, admins only, always `collection`) | `LabSpec` | `LabResult` |
| `reference_game` | a game of a reference run: the statistical baseline the bots are compared against (an experiment with `kind: "reference"`, admins only, `collection`) | `ReferenceSpec {i, seed, size, map_type, barbarians, speed, turn_limit, params}`: one game of citar-sim's baseline, typed, with no budget, path, workers, name, smoke or checks (the baseline's `Options` describes a run of many games on one machine, P4.0) | `ReferenceResult`: the baseline writer's game line (`summarize.py` reads it) without `seconds` and `cpu_s`; a crash as `{crash: message}` only |

- **No wall-clock budget:** a job never runs with `RunSpec.budget`. Its deadline (P4.3.6, P4.5.5) ends a run as `job_failed deadline`, never as a result, and `SimError::TimedOut` is never an outcome, so a slow, capped or paused machine computes the same result as a fast one.
- **Closed and typed:** specs deserialize with `deny_unknown_fields`; bounds are checked (players within the ruleset's limits, a known size, map type, speed and difficulty, `turns` at most the speed's time-victory turn, bot versions compiled in, overrides through `citar_bot`'s own `clean`, strings at most 200 characters, `i` below 1,000,000). A spec is data: no path, URL, code or ruleset text. The kinds differ only in who may queue them and which machines opt in (`capabilities.cpu.work`), so an owner may lend CPU to the site's ladder without lending it to members' experiments.
- **`spec_hash = blake3("CITAR-SPEC-V1\0" ‖ kind ‖ "\0" ‖ canonical bytes)`**, where the canonical bytes are the typed spec re-serialized by serde (fixed field order, maps as `BTreeMap`). The server computes it when it creates a job (through the binding); the helper and the job host recompute it and reject a mismatch (`bad_spec`).

### P4.4.2 The lab result in Rust

- `citar_jobs::lab::play(spec, control) -> LabOutcome` ports `lab.play()`'s result building (`lab.py:314-406`): the players by `str(pid)` with label, bot, difficulty, alive, levels, profile and revision, build, version, fingerprint, clean params, fixed and played aggression, score and share, rank, techs, cities, policies, religion, great people, spaceship parts, unhappy share, checkpoints at 50…300 with the twelve stat keys, era turns, the event counts and the 25 most built items; the winner, its label, the victory and the turns. Integer maps are `BTreeMap`, so the JSON is the same bytes on every target.
- **The deterministic part** is everything above plus `exp`, `i`, `seed`, `map`, `engine` (the jobs build, P4.4.5) and `errors`, which holds the bots' error messages only, normalised (no traces, no addresses; `Crash::record()` puts traces there today, P4.0). `seconds`, `started_ts`, `cpu_s`, `finished` and any trace are measurements or diagnostics, outside the hash, added by whoever writes the line.
- **Crashes:** a panic caught by the runner is the outcome `{crash: {turn, player, label, message}}` (the message only; the backtrace is logged, not hashed: frame addresses differ between builds of one source). It is final: recorded in `crashes.log`, never retried (a Rust panic is a bug, not bad luck; 0.1.5's three retries were for a Python process dying), and a replica must reproduce it.
- `lab.play()` in Python becomes a call of `engine_api.run_job("lab_game", spec)` (4-07); the Python result building and its event listener go.

### P4.4.3 The job host and its limits

- **One program, two parents:** `citar_jobs::host::main(stdin, stdout)` is the job host; the helper runs it as `citar-helper job-host`, the server as `python -m citar.jobs host` (which calls it through the extension). Its protocol is the job frames of P4.2.5 as JSON lines, plus `pause`, `resume` and `limits` (sent once at start, with the parent's `jobs_build`) from the parent, and `ready {limits: {cpu, memory}, verifiable, jobs_build}` and `heartbeat` to it. A host whose `jobs_build` differs from the one in `limits` refuses to start, and a parent refuses a `ready` with another one (P4.3.6).
- **Inside:** at most `threads` runs, each on its own thread with its own `Runner`; between seat steps each run checks its abort flag, the pause flag and its deadline (paused time not counted), so abort and pause take effect within one seat step; progress every 10 s or 25 rounds.
- **Verifiable or not, at start:** the canary checks that denormals are neither flushed to zero (`black_box(f64::MIN_POSITIVE) / 2.0 != 0.0`) nor read as zero (`black_box(f64::MIN_POSITIVE / 4.0) * 4.0 == f64::MIN_POSITIVE`), and the **self-check** plays the shortest job of the `jobs` golden set (embedded, P4.4.5) and compares its result hash and chain head with the committed ones. Any failure makes `verifiable: false`, which refuses every job (`unverifiable`) rather than producing results nobody could check: a target or environment that computes differently is never offered work, so it is never quarantined for it. `citar-helper --self-test` runs the same checks.
- **Memory is counted:** the job host's `#[global_allocator]` is a counting wrapper of the system allocator with per-run-thread live and peak tallies (a thread-local run id), so `job_progress` and `job_result` carry each run's real peak, and the server's cost table learns from them (P4.5.4).
- **Limits** (`citar_jobs::limits::apply`) are applied by the host to itself at start, per the table of P4.3.6 (Windows: a Job Object it creates and joins, legal since Windows 8's nested jobs; Linux: confirms the scope its parent made, else `RLIMIT_DATA` and `nice`; macOS: background priority and the watchdog). The parent side (`citar_jobs::host::spawn` for the helper, `citar/jobs/host.py` for the server) adds the `systemd-run --user --scope` wrapper on Linux when `systemctl --user` answers, and reads `ready` to learn what holds.
- **Ending:** standard input closing (the parent died) aborts every run and exits within 2 s, so no game outlives its parent: this replaces 0.1.5's orphan adoption. **A memory death ends the whole host**, and the parent classifies it from what it sees: the `memory allocation of N bytes failed` line on the host's standard error (Rust's abort when a Job Object's memory limit or `RLIMIT_DATA` refuses a commit), a SIGKILL with the cgroup's `memory.events` `oom_kill` raised, or the abort's exit code. Every run of that host then fails as `memory`; the dispatcher raises each job's `mem_mb` estimate to the largest peak its run reached and offers it only to executors with room (P4.5.5). Any other death fails the runs as `host`, which are retried.

### P4.4.4 Hashes, the chain, the claim, the signature

- **`result_hash = blake3("CITAR-RESULT-V1\0" ‖ kind ‖ "\0" ‖ serde_json bytes of the typed deterministic result)`.** The server never hashes Python JSON: it passes the received result through the binding, which parses it into the typed result (refusing unknown keys), re-serializes it and hashes, and compares with the claim.
- **The chain** (the plan's "per-turn state-hash chain": one digest per round, after every seat's turn of it): every job game runs with `set_chain(DigestChain::new(spec_hash))`; the outcome carries the chain's head, its round count and every round's digest (64 hex each: about 21 KB for a 330-round game). The server checks that the digests fold to the head from `chain_0` (`chain_check` in the binding); replicas compare heads, and on a mismatch the first differing digest names the round where two machines parted.
- **The claim** (typed, fixed field order): `{v: 1, job, run, machine, kind, spec_hash, jobs_build, bots, result_hash, chain_head, rounds, key}`, `bots` the sorted bot versions the spec names, `machine` the id the welcome gave, `key` the public key. **`signature = ed25519(secret, "CITAR-CLAIM-V1\0" ‖ the claim's bytes)`.** The run and machine ids inside it make a signature useless for any other run; the spec hash and jobs build bind it to exactly what was asked for.
- **Server-side verification** (`engine_api.verify_claim(claim, signature, public_key, result, chain)`): the signature against the registered key, the claim's job, run, machine, kind, spec hash and jobs build against the server's records, the result's hash, the chain's fold. Any failure rejects the run and quarantines its machine as a tampering verdict (P4.5.8): a correct helper never produces one.
- The server's own executor's results are not signed: it is the server (P4.5.6).

### P4.4.5 Determinism the proof stands on

- Every job's game is the engine's ordinary game under `set_chain`; the engine's determinism rules (the keyed RNG, `libm`, defined iteration orders, the lint against hash iteration) and the five-target matrix already hold it.
- **The jobs build,** the compatibility key every job carries: `jobs_build = hex12(blake3("CITAR-JOBS-BUILD" ‖ build_id ‖ SIM_CODE ‖ JOBS_CODE ‖ flavour))`. `SIM_CODE` and `JOBS_CODE` are the content codes of citar-sim and citar-jobs, made by the same `content_code.rs` machinery as the engine's and the bot's (both crates gain build scripts in 4-07), so a change to the runner or to how a result is built changes it. `flavour` records `debug_assertions`, `overflow_checks` and the engine's `checks` feature: a `ci` build and a `release` build can compute different outcomes (a `debug_assert!` that fires is a crash in one and nothing in the other). `hello.helper.jobs_build`, `job_offer.jobs_build`, the claim, `cpu_jobs.jobs_build` and the local executor's report carry it; the build id alone stays what the card shows.
- **A `jobs` golden set** (4-07; `crates/citar-jobs/golden/jobs.json`, checked by citar-jobs' own test binary, which builds the engine without `legacy` or `test-ops`, unlike citar-testkit): four short jobs (two lab duels of 30 rounds, a small four-bot lab game capped at 40 rounds, one reference game capped at 40), each with its spec hash, chain head, rounds and result hash; blessed by the same rule as `cargo golden` (a bless commit saying why). It runs in both the `ci` and the `release` profiles: locally on Windows (S7) and in WSL (S9), and in `determinism.yml` and `helper.yml` on the five targets (CI-only). The helper and the job host embed its shortest job as their self-check (P4.4.3). A target that computes another result fails there before any volunteer's machine does, and a machine whose own build does fails its self-check and is offered nothing.
- **Results are only compared within one jobs build:** a job offered to a helper of another jobs build is rejected (`build`), and the dispatcher never matches or compares across jobs builds. QA builds the helper and the extension in the same profile (`cargo xtask develop`'s, `ci` unless `--release`) and records which. A mismatch within one jobs build that the self-check did not catch is reported as "tampering or an incompatible build" (P4.5.8), never as tampering alone.

### P4.4.6 Faults for testing

`citar-helper --features test-faults` adds `--test-fault MODE`: `result:N` (from the Nth job, alter one score in the result and recompute its hash, so the claim is validly signed and the content wrong: the cheater), `result-at:N,M,…` (alter only the listed jobs, counted from the machine's first: the selective cheater), `digest:R` (alter round R's digest and fold the chain from it: broken hardware), `sig` (sign with another key), `cpu:F` (report F times the CPU seconds), `abandon:N` (accept the Nth job and never finish it), `hold:S` (hold each result S seconds before sending it, reporting the held time as CPU). Each mode's detection is a gate of 4-11 or 4-12 and the exit.

---

## P4.5 The server side

### P4.5.1 The hub on v2

- **`/ws/worker`** keeps its path (where v1 helpers dial, so they can be told to upgrade). Its order: (1) the protocol: anything but `protocol == 2` gets P4.2.8's message and 4426 (no lookup, no count, throttled logging); (2) the address (`ratelimit.client_ip`, honouring the trusted hops: defect P4.0 (2)) is checked against `worker_auth` **before** any lookup, and an address over it is `refused rate` with `retry_after_s`; the limit counts **distinct** failing token hashes per address (kept in memory for the window), so a helper retrying one revoked token counts once and cannot lock its owner's other machines behind the same address out, while a brute force, whose tokens all differ, is counted in full; (3) the token: a failure counts toward the limit and is audited (as today); (4) the key (P4.3.7); (5) registration (`admit`, 3-05's double check, unchanged), `welcome`, then `record_connection` off the loop. `WorkerToken.last_ip` and `last_seen_at` are written on success.
- **Frames from the helper** are handled on the event loop without blocking it: completions resolve their pending requests (as today); `cancelled` frees the slot (P4.5.3); `catalog`, `hardware` and `power` go to `asyncio.to_thread` writers (`record_report`, the power rows); `settings_ack` and `status` update the connection and call `scheduler.helper_changed`; job frames are put on the dispatcher's queue (P4.5.5), never handled on the loop.
- **Settings are pushed:** the settings route (`PUT /api/machines/{id}/settings`) already refreshes the scheduler; it now also calls `hub.push_settings(id)`.
- **The machine row gains** `public_key`, `key_at`, `helper` (the last hello's helper block, limits and update state) and `trust` (P4.5.8), in revision `0016c_helper_jobs` (P4.5.4).
- `WorkerConnection` keeps one protocol. The hub is v1 until 4-05 and v2 only from 4-05, the package that also deletes the Python worker (P4.11); v1's frame definitions (`citar/worker/protocol.py`) go with it, replaced by `citar/server/helper_protocol.py` (4-01).

### P4.5.2 Tool-call parsing on the server

- `citar/agents/providers/completion.py` (new) holds `parse_completion(message, finish_reason, mode, tools) -> (StepResult, assistant_entry)`: the post-processing `OpenAIConversation.step` does today (native calls, `__invalid_json__`, `repair_tool_calls`, text calls in XML, GLM, plain and Gemma 4 syntax, the JSON protocol), moved without change of behaviour.
- `OpenAIConversation.step` converts the SDK's message to the same dict and calls it (4-02; the Python worker, which reuses `OpenAIConversation`, reaches it unchanged until 4-05 deletes it); `WorkerConversation.step` sends `request` frames (OpenAI-format tools; in `json` mode the system prompt with `JSON_PROTOCOL` and the listing, and no tools) and calls it on `done.message` (4-05). One parser, so a model's quirk fixed once is fixed for helpers too: the 3-19 Gemma 4 fix needed shipping to helpers; from 4-05 it never will.
- Usage maps `prompt_tokens`, `completion_tokens`, `completion_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens` and `prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens` onto the server's usage keys; tokens are capped as P3.3.4 says.

**As built in 4-02:** `parse_completion` and the corpus as this section says; the details, and what the corpus showed the parser does today, are in P4.5.4's "As built in 4-02". Its fix round made the parser fit for a helper's text (P4.6): time linear in the reply, no exception on any message protocol 2 accepts, at most 512 calls with arguments nested at most 64 deep. The usage mapping stays where it was (`OpenAIConversation.step`, unchanged): mapping `done.usage` is 4-05's, with the caveat noted there.

### P4.5.3 The scheduler on v2

- **Capacity** = the site's slots capped by the helper's `settings_ack.effective.llm.slots` (it was `hello.max_concurrent`).
- **Cancel** (`WorkerConversation.close` → `hub.cancel`): the caller's wait ends at once, as today; the slot is freed by `cancelled` (or a crossing `done`/`error`). If neither comes within `CANCEL_ACK_SECONDS` (5 s), the slot drains as P3.3.3 describes and the machine's `flags.cancel_unacked` counts it (shown to admins). A v2 helper that drains is misbehaving; draining is no longer the normal path, which is what 3-19's "Left for later" asked.
- `busy` from a v2 helper means its local slots are below the site's and its `settings_ack` was missed: the existing lower-and-requeue rule (P3.3.3) stays as the net.
- A helper's `status.quiet_until` is read like the site's quiet hours.

**As built in 4-05: the hub on v2 only; the Python worker retired** (P4.5.1, P4.5.2's helper half, P4.5.3, P4.2.8, P4.11; Appendix E). One agent, 2026-10-14 to 15, from 22:43 to 01:25 (-04:00), on branch `p-4-05` from `ac92716`. Python (the hub, `WorkerConversation`, the scheduler, two routes), one Rust binding (`key_fingerprint`), JS, the tests, both QA scripts, the docs; the Python worker deleted (`citar/worker/`, `installer/helper_entry.py`, `tests/test_worker.py`, `tests/test_helper_config_cases.py`: 2,423 lines). Before this note, 82 files changed, +5,486 -3,724 lines; `tests/data/helper_config_cases.json` stays as the record 4-03 meant it to be.

- **The hub** (`citar/server/workers.py`, 1,211 lines, rewritten on `helper_protocol`). `/ws/worker`'s order is P4.5.1's: (1) the hello's parse: anything but protocol 2 (a 0.1.5 hello, `protocol: 3`, neither key) gets `HP.v1_refusal()` in protocol 1's `error` frame and 4426, with no lookup and no count, logged once an hour per address (`V1_LOG_SECONDS`); (2) `AuthLimiter`: the address (`ratelimit.client_ip`, so `X-Forwarded-For` counts only behind a trusted proxy) is refused `rate` with `retry_after_s` and 4429 once `RULES["worker_auth"]` (20 in 300 s) distinct failing token hashes came from it, before any lookup (in memory, at most 10,000 addresses); (3) `authenticate` in a thread: the token (a refusal is `token`, audited `worker.auth_failed` and counted), the machine key (trust on first use: the first hello registers `public_key`/`key_at`; another key is `refused key` until the reset), `last_seen_at` and `last_ip`; (4) `admit` (an older connection of the machine is sent `shutdown replaced` and closed 4409); (5) `welcome` with the site's settings, `ping_s` held to 5..300, the server block (`version`, `build`, `jobs_build` none) and the hello's `running` dropped (no run before 4-10). Frames: `done`/`error`/`cancelled` resolve their pending request (a `done` that does not parse resolves it as an error, so nothing waits); `settings_ack` is held to what was sent (`clamp`: each number the lesser, a site's 0 takes the helper's, kinds in both lists, enabled only when both) and an overclaim flags the machine (`flags.settings_overclaim`); `catalog` and `hardware` go to 3-18's `record_report` in a thread; `status.quiet_until` is kept for the scheduler; a `ping` is answered; later packages' frames (`power`, the job and update frames) are logged once per connection and ignored. The hub's clock is injectable (`hub().clock`; the tests' `FakeClock`) and its ping loop naps on an event (`poke`), so the keep-alive transcripts run in milliseconds.
- **Settings are pushed and acknowledged** (`machines_api.settings_changed`): the settings route refreshes the scheduler, then `hub().push_settings(id, wait=2.0)`, so its answer already shows the capacity the helper acknowledged (`ACK_WAIT_SECONDS`); a helper that does not acknowledge leaves the site's figures in force.
- **The scheduler** (P4.5.3): capacity is the row's slots capped by the acknowledged `llm.slots` (held to the site's); a helper serving no models (`--no-llm`, or model work off in its acknowledgement) is refused as `disabled` ("its helper serves no models"); a cancel (`WorkerConversation.close` -> `hub().cancel`) marks the slot `cancelling` until `cancelled` or a crossing `done`/`error` frees it, and after `CANCEL_ACK_SECONDS` (5 s) without either it drains as P3.3.3 says while the helper is still connected, counted in the `cancel_unacked` counter and the machine's `flags.cancel_unacked` (`flag_cancel_unacked` -> `workers.flag_machine`); the helper's `status.quiet_until` is merged with the site's quiet hours (the later end wins).
- **`WorkerConversation` on v2** (`worker_provider.py`, 197 lines): OpenAI-format tools (`completion.openai_tools`); in `json` mode no tools and the system prompt with `JSON_PROTOCOL` and the listing (`system_prompt`, the one `OpenAIConversation` sends); the seat's parameters through `request_params` (`max_tokens` 0 left unset); `parse_completion` on `done.message`; `usage_of` (cached prompt tokens moved out of `input_tokens` into `cache_read_input_tokens`).
- **Routes and the card:** `POST /api/machines/{id}/key/reset` (owner or admin; audit `server.key_reset` with the old fingerprint; trust unchanged) and `POST /api/machines/{id}/rescan` (409 when no helper is connected); the workers route gains `helper` (the last hello's version, build, target, protocol) and `key` (`fingerprint`, `registered_at`). The Helper dialog shows "CITAR helper <version> · protocol 2 · build <build> · <target>", the key's fingerprint and "Reset key…"; the fingerprint comes from the Rust binding `key_fingerprint` (BLAKE3, the first 8 bytes in hex), the code the helper's `--version` uses.
- **Retirement:** `citar/helper_pointer.py` says where to download the helper; `citar worker`, `citar setup --worker`, `install.sh --worker` and `install.ps1 -Worker` print it and exit 2; the `worker` extra and the `citar-worker` script are gone from `pyproject.toml`; release.yml's helper job builds the Rust helper (`cargo build --release --locked -p citar-helper`, with its Fedora TLS check); test.yml's `build-ext` job builds the `dev-guard` helper as an artifact and each test job runs the end-to-end tests with it (`CITAR_HELPER_BIN`). `install.sh --uninstall` still removes a 0.1.5 `citar-worker` link. A 0.1.5 system unit is retried into the refusal every 15 s until disabled: WORKERS.md's "Moving from 0.1.5" runbook.
- **The tests.** `tests/helperfake.py` (`FakeHelper` over a real socket, `HubPlayer` for the transcripts, `HubServer`: the app under uvicorn on loopback with `proxy_headers` off as production runs it, `Responder`, `StubConnection`, `RealHelper`); `tests/test_helper_conformance.py` (the 40 transcripts marked for 4-05, each a test, and one that the set is complete); `tests/test_hub.py` (44: what remains true of the hub from `test_worker.py`, and gates 3-6 and 10); `tests/test_helper_parity.py` (4); `tests/test_helper_e2e.py` (5, the real binary); `tests/test_one_protocol.py` (5, gate 9). The ported tests (`test_machine_settings`, `test_scheduler`, `test_session_leases`, `test_scheduler_routes`, `test_pooled_work`, `test_scheduler_e2e`, `test_pool`, `test_ci_scripts`) use the v2 fakes.
- **QA:** `qa_phase3.py`'s helpers are the Rust binary (`--helper-bin`, default `citar-helper` in `CITAR_EXT_DIR`) with `--home` in the QA directory, one home per machine so a restart keeps its key; its scripted endpoint streams server-sent events over HTTP/1.1 keep-alive and records when each client went. `scripts/qa_phase4.py` (526 lines) has the parts `helper` (12 checks) and `llm-real` (8; `--stand-in` plays LM Studio with the scripted endpoint), the later parts listed and refused, `--wsl` refused until 4-10.
- **Found and fixed on the way** (each held by a test but the fourth): (1) the limit on refused worker tokens never refused anything: it was counted after the lookup and its refusal was dropped; (2) it took the address from `X-Forwarded-For` whatever `behind_proxy` said; (3) a token's `last_ip` was never written; (4) the Helper card, the setup checklist, INSTALL.md, KNOWN_ISSUES, the FAQ and DEPLOY.md still pointed at `citar worker` or the `worker` extra; (5) two of this package's own cancel tests never ran (a helper method named `run` shadowed `TestCase.run`), found by the planted bugs.
- **Decisions.** (1) The settings route waits up to 2 s for the acknowledgement rather than answering before it: the dialog shows what is in effect, and a slow helper costs at most 2 s once. (2) A slot drains only while its helper is still connected: a gone helper's slot is free at once, as in P3.3.3. (3) The v1 refusal is logged once an hour per address (P4.2.8 says "throttled"): a 0.1.5 unit retries every 15 s. (4) The CPU capability `yield_to_llm` defaults on; the dialog's checkbox is 4-10's. (5) The hub's tests run the app under uvicorn with `proxy_headers` off, as `citar.server.__main__` does: uvicorn's default trusts loopback's `X-Forwarded-For`, which would make gate 5's forged header pass for the wrong reason. (6) The helper's facts (version, build, target, protocol, the key's fingerprint and "Reset key…") are in the machine's Helper dialog, opened from its card, which stays one line; the dialog's "Rescan now" button is 4-06's (H2), with the hardware scan in Rust: the route and its client call are here.
- **Gates.** (1) **The transcripts:** the 40 marked for 4-05 (the handshake with every `refused` code and the v1 refusal, settings, catalog, hardware, status, keep-alive, completions, cancel, shutdown) pass from the hub's side, played by `HubPlayer` against the real app over a real socket (`tests/test_helper_conformance.py`, 41 tests: one a transcript, one that the set is complete), on Windows and in WSL. (2) **Parsing parity:** the 59 cases of 4-02's corpus through `WorkerConversation` and a `FakeHelper` give `OpenAIConversation`'s steps, stored entries and usage; a `json`-mode seat's request carries `JSON_PROTOCOL` and the listing, byte for byte, and no tools (`tests/test_helper_parity.py`, 4 tests; protocol 1's worker never sent them, and the planted "json mode without its protocol" is caught). (3) **Cancel:** acknowledged after 30 ms, the ticket behind starts within 200 ms of the acknowledgement, `draining` stays 0 and nothing is flagged; never acknowledged, the slot drains after 5 s, counted in `cancel_unacked` and flagged on the machine (`test_hub.Cancel`). (4) **Keys:** the first hello registers its key, the same key reconnects, another is `refused key` (4401) until the owner's reset (one `server.key_reset` row, the trust unchanged), after which it registers; the routes' access: a visitor 401, another member and a moderator 404, the grantee 403, the owner and an administrator 200, and a rescan of an offline machine 409 (`test_hub.Keys`). (5) **The rate limit:** 20 distinct bad tokens from one address, then a good token is `refused rate` (4429, with `retry_after_s`) and the patched `authenticate` is not called; one revoked token retried 40 times counts once and another machine's good token from the same address connects; with `behind_proxy` off a forged `X-Forwarded-For` is counted against the socket's own address (and with a trusted proxy, against the forwarded one) (`test_hub.RateLimit`). (6) **The refusal:** a 0.1.5 hello (the fixture), `protocol: 3` and a hello with neither key each get P4.2.8's message in a v1 `error` frame and 4426, with no token lookup, no audit row and no count; 100 from one address log one line (`test_hub.Refusal`). (7) **End to end** with the real binary (`tests/test_helper_e2e.py`, 5 tests): the card's fingerprint equals `--version`'s; a seat plays three turns with tool calls from the scripted endpoint and a ticket's step is parsed on the server; a cancelled ticket's slot is free within 1 s with nothing draining, the endpoint's client gone within 1 s of the cancel, at most the one event already on its way stamped after the cancel and none after the client went; a revoke drops it within a second (exit 78, no reconnect); it reconnects by itself after a server restart. On Windows (`C:/dev/target/p-4-05-gates.log`, with gates 1-6, 9 and 10's hub tests: 99 tests in 137 s), then in WSL Ubuntu 24.04 (`C:/dev/target/p-4-05-wsl.log`, script `p-4-05-wsl.sh` beside it): a clone of a bundle at `96fd58a` in `~/p-4-05-wsl`, a venv `~/venv-p-4-05` made without pip and given the Windows venv's pure-Python pip (this WSL has neither pip nor ensurepip), `cargo xtask develop` there (the project's requirements from PyPI, the extension and the `dev-guard` helper into `~/target/citar-p-4-05`): the 5 tests in 9.7 s, and the hub's 94 (conformance, hub, parity, one protocol) in 105 s; the strengthened cancel test (`2d15f24`) passed three more times in each. (8) **QA** at the final code: `qa_phase3.py queue` 30 of 30, `machines` 16 of 16 and `exit` 26 of 26 with the Rust helper (`C:/dev/target/p-4-05-final-qa-*`); `qa_phase4.py helper` 12 of 12 (`p-4-05-final2-qa4-helper`; its capacity check made strict at `e30cb91`). `qa_phase4.py llm-real` **was not run against the real model**: LM Studio listed `google/gemma-4-e2b` as not loaded at 00:04 and at 01:17 (only its model list was read), so the part refused with nothing sent to it, as it should; with `--stand-in` it passes 8 of 8 (the tool call parsed on the server, the 1,500-token generation's cancel acknowledged in 11 ms, the requests after it as fast as before). It is the owner's to run with the model loaded: `python scripts/qa_phase4.py llm-real --dir <a new directory>`. (9) **One protocol** (`tests/test_one_protocol.py`, 5 tests): no `citar.worker`, `citar-worker` or `PROTOCOL_VERSION = 1` in any tracked file outside `design/` and nine allowed lines (each with why, each checked to be still needed); the hub's frame dispatch names only protocol 2's helper frames; nothing in `citar/server` imports protocol 1's module or names its frames and fields; the v1 refusal is the one branch that answers a hello that is not protocol 2. (10) **Overhead:** the hub's own time per round trip, median 0.275 ms (p95 0.333 ms) over 1,000, against 3 ms (`test_hub.Overhead`); the session's 2.334, 2.359 and 2.416, a median of 2.359 against 2.87 (`C:/dev/target/p-4-05-overhead-{1,2,3}.{log,json}`). **Planted bugs:** 20 of 20 caught (`C:/dev/target/p-4-05-mutate.log`, script `p405/mutate.py`): the limit after the lookup, or counting every failure; the refusal after a lookup, or logged every time; no clamp; no overclaim flag; another key accepted; no `pong`; the hello's 30 s on the real clock; a bad `done` left waiting; the helper's quiet hours dropped; no `shutdown replaced`; no `last_ip`; the acknowledgement ignored, or never given up on; the acknowledged slots not read; json mode without its protocol; cached tokens counted twice; the reply parsed as protocol 1 did; seat parameters sent unbounded. The first full run missed three, and so found the cancel tests never ran (a helper method named `run` shadowed `TestCase.run`), the test `Responder` freeing a slot without acknowledging, and the transcripts' slots state not reading the capacity: fixed at `bd855a3`. **S1:** 1491 tests OK in 601 s (`C:/dev/target/p-4-05-s1.log`; 1446 before: `test_worker.py` and `test_helper_config_cases.py` went, 99 came), after `cargo xtask develop`; 8 tracebacks, the known set ("disk on fire" twice, the registry import's JSON error and its `ImportFailed`, the teardown race's "no such table" for `owned_items` and for `games`, each chained); "the local model is not loaded" is gone with the Python worker. **S1b:** 1491 OK in 582 s on Python 3.12 under `TZ=UTC0` (`C:/dev/venv/py312-check` with this worktree's extension; `p-4-05-s1b.log`), 12 tracebacks, the same set (`games` three times). **S2** ruff clean. **S3** 190 routes gated, 28 public on purpose, none unguarded (the two new ones gated). **S4** 38 files, 270 internal links, all resolve. **S5** `mkdocs build --strict` (`C:/dev/target/p-4-05-site`); `build_wiki.py` regenerated and committed. **S6** 114 of 114 front-end tests; 42 modules parse as ES modules. **S7** (`C:/dev/target/p-4-05-s7.log`): clippy (`--workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -D warnings`), rustdoc with `-D warnings`, nextest **1604 passed, 3 skipped** (263 s), the doctests, `cargo xtask check` all clear, the refcheck run clean, and `cargo golden check` all 16 sets with the committed hashes (`p-4-05-golden.log`); fmt failed on the binding at first and passed after `80b0059`; the helper's test comment edited later (`0db80c5`): fmt, clippy of the helper and its test rerun. **S8:** `qa_phase4.py helper` as in gate 8, and the browser checklist's H1 as far as 4-05 reaches (screenshots in `C:/dev/target/p-4-05-final-qa4-helper/screenshots`): the Helper dialog of bob's machine shows "CITAR helper 0.1.5 · protocol 2 · build 0b412a18fe95 · x86_64-pc-windows-msvc", the machine key's fingerprint and "Reset key…"; a helper run with a new home was refused (`refused key`, exit 78), "Reset key…" asked to confirm and the dialog then said no key was registered, and the next run registered its key, the dialog "connected" with the fingerprint `citar-helper --version` printed for that home. H1's CPU and update rows are 4-10's and 4-13's. **S9** in WSL: `cargo nextest run -p citar-helper -p citar-jobs --locked` 119 passed, 1 skipped, and the helper with `dev-guard` 117 passed, 1 skipped; `cargo golden check` the same 16 hashes as Windows (`p-4-05-wsl-golden.log`); `xtask check` all clear; `--version` on `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`. **S10** (`C:/dev/target/p-4-05-s10-after.log`): as 4-04's merge left them: the repository's `config/servers.json` sha256 starts `586888cbb61aa9c7`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CITAR\config\servers.json` `2561079bd2f123e1`, `citar.db` mtime 2026-09-23 00:38, `secret_key` 2026-09-20 22:16; no `helper.json`, `machine-key.json` (in the base or its `helper/`) or `config/worker.json`; no `citar-helper`, job host, QA or dev server process; no service, Run value or scheduled task; no `CITAR_*` variable; no citar unit in WSL; the main checkout shows only `.claude/launch.json`. Every helper this package ran had a home of its own under the system's temporary directory or the QA directory and dialled loopback only; LM Studio was only asked for its model list. **CI-only, not run:** test.yml on its three OSes (new here: the `build-ext` job's helper artifact and the end-to-end tests with it), rust.yml (the binding's `key_fingerprint`), release.yml's helper job (on a tag only), determinism.yml (no engine change).

- **Fix round** (2026-10-15, from 01:43 to 03:46 (-04:00), on `p-4-05` from `0828ab1`). The review's ten findings were checked; each one held. Each is fixed, and each behaviour fix is held by a test that fails without it. 15 planted bugs, one for each fix, were all caught (`C:/dev/target/p-4-05-fix-mutate.log`, script `p405/mutate_fix.py`, which restores each file from memory, not from git).
  1. **Slot changes made in the machine editor never reached a connected helper.** `WorkerConnection.effective` clamps the acknowledgement to the settings sent at connect, so a raise of "Requests at once" (`PUT /api/pool/servers/{id}`, `POST /api/servers`) stayed at the old figure until the helper reconnected. Protocol 1 took it within the scheduler's 5 s reread, so this was a regression. The fix has three parts:
     - every route that saves a machine's row calls `machines_api.settings_changed` after its commit: the pool editor, the registry's save, its key, hardware collect and import. The call refreshes the scheduler and pushes without waiting.
     - the keep-alive pushes any difference between `site_settings` and what a connection was last sent (`WorkerHub._sync_settings`, in a thread), which catches `admin_cli` and other processes.
     - `serve` reads the settings again after `admit`, and pushes a change that came in between right after the welcome.
     `WorkerHub.push_settings` sends only when the settings differ (`test_hub.Settings`, three tests).
  2. **Concurrent hellos bypassed the refused-token limit** (80 at once: 80 lookups). `WorkerHub.gate(address)`, an asyncio lock per event loop and address, removed when nobody holds it, makes the check, the lookup and the count of a failure one step. A lookup takes milliseconds, so helpers behind one NAT barely wait, and an address over the limit is answered from memory. 40 hellos sent together: 20 lookups, 20 `token`, 20 `rate` (4429), 20 audit rows (`RateLimit.test_hellos_sent_at_once_get_no_more_lookups_than_the_limit`). The alternative, counting lookups in flight, was not taken: it would refuse good tokens from one NAT that connect together.
  3. **Every frame, the unauthenticated first one included, was parsed on the event loop.** The changes:
     - a first frame over 2 MiB (`HP.MAX_HELLO_BYTES`) is closed with 4400 `too_large` unread;
     - a frame over 64 KiB (`PARSE_INLINE_CHARS`) is parsed on a thread, a first frame on a one-thread pool of its own (`_HELLO_PARSER`), so a flood of them takes at most one core and never queues in front of token lookups;
     - a bad frame's id is read in the same thread call.
     To keep "a correct helper never sends a frame the hub refuses" (PROTOCOL.md), the Rust helper now caps its hardware report at 256 KiB as sent: a file over 1 MiB is not read, and a larger report is not sent and is logged once an hour (`session::read_hardware_file`, test `a_hardware_report_too_large_for_a_hello_is_not_sent`). The cap is measured as sent because `1e9` takes 3 bytes in a file and 12 as serde_json writes it. PROTOCOL.md says all this. Measured with a `call_soon` chain on the hub's loop (exact, not rounded to Windows' 15.6 ms timer), the loop's longest stall at `0828ab1` was 957 ms for an 8 MiB first frame and 259-277 ms for 1.9 MiB of the worst shapes. Now it is 6-7 ms for the 8 MiB one (receiving it), 16-33 ms for the 1.9 MiB ones (one GIL handoff on Windows), and 23 ms for a welcomed helper's 8 MiB bad frame. `Frames.test_a_long_frame_is_parsed_off_the_event_loop` bounds each by a third of the parse's own time in the same run, never under 50 ms.
  4. **An acknowledgement was judged against the newest settings sent,** so one that crossed a push flagged an honest helper `settings_overclaim`. The connection now keeps the settings frames sent and not yet acknowledged, oldest first (`SettingsSent`, the welcome's recorded before `admit`). Each frame is recorded and queued under one lock (`_settings_lock`, under which nothing else is called), so the record's order is the wire's. Each `settings_ack` settles the oldest and is held to it. `effective` still clamps the newest acknowledgement to the newest settings, and a waiter wakes only on its own frame's answer, after the scheduler was told (`Settings.test_an_acknowledgement_is_held_to_the_settings_frame_it_answers`).
  5. **`authenticate` mapped any exception to `token`.** A database failure is now `Identity("error")`: close 1011 with no frame (PROTOCOL.md: a reconnect with backoff), no count and no audit row. The first key is registered by a conditional `UPDATE … WHERE public_key IS NULL`, then read back, so of two first hellos with different keys one registers and the other is refused `key` (or, on SQLite, closed 1011 when its snapshot went stale), never let in over the other's key (`Tokens`, two tests).
  6. **The hello's 30 s polled the clock every 50 ms per silent socket** (1,000 sockets: 18.7% of a core). `_first_frame` and the keep-alive's nap now wait on `WorkerHub.tick()`, a future of the running loop that `poke` resolves when a test's clock moves. With the real clock nothing polls (`Frames.test_waiting_for_a_hello_does_not_poll`: at most 5 clock reads in 0.6 s, against about 13).
  7. **The settings route waited 2 s even for a change of nothing,** with no bound per machine. It now waits only when `M.update` changed something, and `push_settings` lets one caller a machine wait (`ack_waiter`); another returns at once. A helper that never acknowledges therefore holds at most one request thread a machine (`Settings.test_a_change_that_changes_nothing_waits_for_nothing_and_one_request_a_machine_waits`).
  8. **A key reset left a connected helper serving under the forgotten key.** `key_reset` now calls `WorkerHub.disconnect` after its commit: close 1012 with no `shutdown`, what is in flight failed as retryable, so the helper reconnects and registers its key at once (`Keys.test_a_reset_while_connected_makes_the_helper_reconnect_and_register_its_key`). The real binary reads that close as a reconnect, not a refusal: it stays up, connects again within its backoff and registers its key (`test_helper_e2e.RealHelper.test_a_key_reset_makes_it_reconnect_and_register_its_key_again`, Windows and WSL; `qa_phase4.py helper`'s new 13th check: 2.37 s after the reset). WORKERS.md and the confirmation box say that a token someone else used must be revoked as well.
  9. **`helper_binary()` ignored a `CITAR_HELPER_BIN` that is not a file,** so the end-to-end tests could skip their way to a green CI run. It now raises `NoHelperBinary`, and `require_helper_binary` (used by `test_helper_e2e` and `test_scheduler_e2e`) fails rather than skips when `CI` is set (`Hub.test_a_helper_binary_named_but_missing_fails_rather_than_skips`).
  10. **`test_worker.py`'s "only the hash is stored" was dropped in the split.** It is back in `test_hub.Tokens`, with the token issued through `POST /api/pool/servers/{id}/workers/tokens`. So are the lookup's refusals of an unknown and an empty token, and the seat and game checks of a pooled machine that `Authentication` held.
  - **Not changed:** `setup_api`'s first-run wizard makes a new machine the server dials, under a new id, so no helper can be connected to it; the keep-alive's look covers anything else.

A failure that leaves the later checks meaningless (the helper not connecting, a request raising) ends the run as a failed check, not an exception. The model's state is read again after the run whatever happened in it. A long generation already over at the cancel fails its check at once, rather than waiting 10 s for an acknowledgement that cannot come.

    - they **pass against a stand-in that stops a cancelled generation**; its side agrees: eight streamed completions, the essay's client gone 1 ms after the cancel with no event after it, cut short at 31 of 100 words;
    - they **fail against one that runs on**: the cancel is acknowledged in 11 ms, but the first answer after it took 4.23 s, waiting out the abandoned generation's 4.0 s, against a bound of 1.38 s;
    - they **fail when the long generation is over before the cancel**, a **model that is not loaded** (nothing sent, no helper started), and **one unloaded during the run**.
  - **Found and fixed on the way: a stopped ledger waited out every later stop.** A stopped ledger starts no writer again, so an event put after its stop was never written, and every later flush or stop waited its whole timeout. In a test process, each hub stopped after the first waited 10 s: 12 of 16 stops across the hub's five test modules, 120 of their 200 s. `Ledger.flush` now writes such events on the calling thread, as a ledger without threads does (`test_ledger.WriteVolume.test_what_is_put_after_a_stop_is_written_by_the_next_stop_at_once`, which fails without the fix). The five modules now take 80 s; S1 599 s (710 s at the fix round) and S1b 531 s (651 s).

    - the part without its load check before, without its bound after the cancel, and without its load check after;
    - the hub never sending the cancel;
    - a stand-in that never runs on, one with no model lock, one that never unloads, and one that does not answer at length;
    - the ledger's flush waiting out a stopped ledger.
    The tenth, the part not requiring that the generation was cancelled, is equivalent now that a generation over before the cancel fails without waiting: it differs only when a `done` crosses the cancel.

### P4.5.4 The job store (revision `0016c_helper_jobs`)

One Alembic revision (4-02, "schema first" as in P3.10.1) with every Phase 4 table and column; later packages fill in behaviour.

- **`cpu_jobs`:** `id` (`cj_…`), `kind`, `ref` (`lab:<exp>:<i>`, the ledger's activity id of 3-10), `item_kind`/`item_ref` (the owned experiment, for access), `owner_id` (the consumer; null for `collection`), `spec` (JSON), `spec_hash`, `jobs_build`, `priority`, `mem_mb` (the memory estimate, raised by a memory death), `state` (`queued running settling accepted crashed failed withdrawn cancelled`), `accepted_run`, `result_hash`, `attempts`, `created_at`, `updated_at`; indexed on (`state`, `priority`, `created_at`) and on `ref`.
- **`cpu_job_runs`:** `id` (`cr_…`), `job_id`, `machine_id`, `executor` (`local`/`remote`), `role` (`primary`, `replica`, `tiebreak`), `state` (`offered running done failed aborted expired`), `offered_at`, `started_at`, `last_progress_at`, `ended_at`, `deadline_at`, `cpu_s`, `wall_s`, `paused_s`, `peak_mb`, `result` (JSON, pruned once the job is settled), `result_hash`, `chain_head`, `rounds`, `digests` (JSON, pruned once matched; kept as evidence on a mismatch), `claim`, `signature`, `acked_at`, `verdict` (`reference match mismatch unchecked rejected`), `error`.
- **`job_costs`:** per (`kind`, `size`, `seats`, `rounds` bucket of 50): `cpu_s` (the median over the local executor's and verifiers' runs: the **reference cost**), `peak_mb` (the 95th percentile of every run's counted peak), `samples`, `updated_at`; seeded from a committed table measured on the golden and benchmark jobs (4-09), so the first jobs already have an estimate.
- **`servers`:** `public_key`, `key_at`, `helper` (JSON), `trust` (JSON: `{verified, streak, mismatches, last_verified_at, last_mismatch, abandons, since, verifier, speed}`: `since` the registration or the last release from quarantine, `verifier` an administrator's mark, `speed` the machine's measured speed against the reference cost).
- **`compute_events.verification`** gains `rejected` (a string column: no DDL); `settings_kv.verification` holds P4.5.8's settings.

**As built in 4-02: the schema and the one parser on the server** (P4.5.2, P4.5.4; Appendix E). One agent, 2026-10-14, from 09:54 to 10:35 (-04:00), on branch `p-4-02` from `8e7db41`. Python, tests and docs only: no Rust, no front end; the hub, the Python worker and the scheduler are untouched.

- **Revision `0016c_helper_jobs`** (`citar/migrations/versions/0016c_helper_jobs.py`, written by hand from `models.py`, as the baseline was): `cpu_jobs`, `cpu_job_runs`, `job_costs`, and four columns on `servers`. Choices the design left open:
  - `cpu_jobs`: ids `cj_` + 16 hex (`new_id`); the queue's index `ix_cpu_jobs_state_priority_created` and `ref` as the design says, and `owner_id` indexed like every foreign key here, set null when the account goes; `item_kind`/`item_ref` nullable (an experiment's owned item may not exist yet); `jobs_build` 16 characters (a hex12 with room); `mem_mb` an integer (`job_offer.mem_mb` is an `int`); `accepted_run` a plain id (a foreign key back to the runs would be circular).
  - `cpu_job_runs`: ids `cr_…`; deleted with their job (`CASCADE`); **`machine_id` kept by id with no foreign key**, as `compute_events` keeps it, so a run stays the evidence of what a machine returned after the machine's row is deleted; `job_id`, `machine_id` and `state` indexed (the dispatcher rebuilds its view from the offered and running runs at start); `cpu_s`, `wall_s`, `paused_s` and `peak_mb` non-null floats defaulting to 0 (`peak_mb` is the protocol's `number`); `signature` 128 characters (base64 of 64 bytes is 88); `error` is JSON: a failure's `{code, message}`, or a mismatch's evidence.
  - `job_costs`: primary key (`kind`, `size`, `seats`, `rounds`), `rounds` the bucket's first round; empty until 4-09 seeds it.

### P4.5.5 The dispatcher

- **`citar/jobs/dispatcher.py`:** one thread per server process (like the scheduler and the ledger writer), restarted if it dies, surviving an exception in its loop; it wakes every 2 s and on events (a job frame, an executor change, a new experiment). Its state is the database; its in-memory view is rebuilt at start, which re-queues every run that was `offered` or `running` on the local executor (whose job host died with the old process) and keeps remote runs until their helpers reconnect or their deadlines pass.
- **Jobs come from the lab's queue:** experiments by priority then age (`lab.load_queue`, unchanged files), each game without an accepted result and without a live job becomes a `cpu_jobs` row, at most 4 x the executors' total threads queued ahead (so a 10,000-game experiment does not become 10,000 rows at once). An experiment whose games are all accepted moves to `done/` (as today).
- **Matching** a queued job to an executor with a free thread, highest priority first, round-robin across owners within a priority. An executor is eligible when: online; CPU enabled on the site and by the machine's own switch, with the job's kind in its effective `work` as the hub clamps it (the kinds the site and the acknowledgement both list, P4.2.3); the same jobs build; `cpu.verifiable`; its effective memory per thread (`memory_mb / threads`) covers the job's `mem_mb`; not quarantined, nor its owner (`users.trust_tier = quarantined`); within P4.5.8 rule 3's caps on `new` machines; outside the site's restricted hours and its helper's own quiet hours; no benchmark lease live on it (P3.3.6's rule extended to CPU); not backing off this job's reject reason (P4.2.5); and the job's owner may use it: their own machine; a `collection` job on a machine whose owner opted into that kind; or a grant on its group whose purposes **list `lab` explicitly**, checked by `admission.check(..., purpose="lab")` (3-09's admission, its budget included). **CPU work is opt-in per grant:** `_purpose_ok` changes so an empty purposes list means every model purpose and never `lab`, and a grant made to lend a GPU for games never lends the CPU. **Administrators' bypass of grants does not apply to CPU work:** an administrator's own lab experiment is eligible where a member's would be, and only the `collection` kinds go to every machine that opted into them.
- **Deadlines:** `deadline_s = clamp(10 × expected, 300 s, 6 h)`, where `expected` is the cost table's `cpu_s` for the job (P4.5.4) divided by the executor's effective share of a core per game (`cpu_percent / 100 × logical CPUs / threads`, at most 1) and by its measured speed (`trust.speed`, 1 until known). Paused time (`paused_s`) extends a run's deadline, up to twice it. A run past its deadline plus 60 s, an offer unanswered after `offer_ttl_s`, and a running run with no progress for 5 minutes while its helper is connected, outside a reported pause, are `expired` and their jobs re-queued (P4.2.5). A machine with three expired or abandoned runs in an hour gets no offer for an hour (`trust.abandons`); an expiry caused by a pause never counts.
- **Failures:** `job_failed` with `retryable` re-queues the job (at most 3 attempts, then `failed`, shown on the Lab page); `memory` raises the job's `mem_mb` to the peak its run reached before re-queueing it (P4.4.3); a crash outcome is accepted as the game's outcome (P4.4.2) once verified like any result.
- **Pause:** `citar lab stop` and the Lab page's Stop pause the dispatcher's offers (running runs finish); `citar lab resume` resumes. **A benchmark lease** going live on a machine sends it `jobs_pause` (the local executor's host gets `pause`), whatever `yield_to_llm` says, and its end `jobs_resume`.

### P4.5.6 Executors

- **The local executor** (`citar/jobs/local.py`) is the host's machine row: it runs a job host child (`python -m citar.jobs host`) with the host row's CPU settings, restarted if it dies, ended with the server. It is **trusted by definition** (it is the server): its runs' verdict is `reference`, and it is the verifier every `new` machine's replicas run on (P4.5.8). Its report carries the extension's `jobs_build`, so a server built in another flavour than a helper never meets that helper's jobs (P4.4.5). Defaults for the host row: local mode, CPU on with threads = half the logical CPUs; server mode, on with 1 thread, 50% and min(1,024 MB, 25% of the RAM) (the VPS has one vCPU and 961 MB, plan §9, so about 240 MB there, below what the OOM killer would take from the server itself; jobs whose `mem_mb` does not fit go elsewhere; an admin raises or lowers them in the host's machine settings). These replace the lab runner's `workers`/`night_workers` (`saves/lab/config.json`); the host's restricted hours replace `quiet_now`. On the VPS, a system service with no user manager, the local job host's CPU share is soft (`nice` and its one thread are the real cap) and its memory hard (`RLIMIT_DATA`); the host's card says so like any machine's.
- **Remote executors** are connected helpers: the dispatcher sends `job_offer` through the hub (`send_threadsafe`), and job frames come back through its queue. Threads are the helper's `settings_ack.effective.cpu.threads`, clamped to the site's (P4.2.3).
- **Replacing the lab runner:** `lab.run`, `_runner_step`, `_Adopted`, `_find_running_games`, `_collect_orphans`, `cmd_play`, the job files and the status file go (4-09); `python -m citar.lab run` and `citar lab run` say the lab now runs inside the CITAR server (local or server mode) and exit 2; `submit`, `status`, `report`, `stop` and `resume` stay. `lab.overview()` reads the dispatcher.

### P4.5.7 Results into the lab, the ratings and the ledger

- **The lab:** an accepted job appends one line to `saves/lab/results/<exp>.jsonl` (`reference` experiments to `saves/lab/reference/<name>.jsonl`), as `_record` does today, plus `executor: {machine, run, verification}`. Writes go through `fsutil` (replace on rewrite) on the dispatcher's thread. A **withdrawal** rewrites the file without the withdrawn lines (P4.5.8).
- **The order of writing** makes a crash between the two writes harmless: the dispatcher commits the job `accepted`, with its result, first; then it appends the line, skipping it when a line for the same (`exp`, `i`, `run`) is already there; and at start it reconciles every active experiment's results file with the database (an accepted job with no line gets one, a line whose job is not accepted is removed). A crash between the commit and the append therefore leaves exactly one line after the restart.
- **The ratings** read the results files as before (only accepted lines are there), and their cache, keyed on the files' signature, recomputes after any append or rewrite.
- **The ledger:** each completed run writes one `cpu` event when it completes, through the ledger's writer: `activity = lab:<exp>:<i>`, `activity_kind = lab`, `task_class = cpu`, `machine_id` the executor's row, `provider_id` its owner (null for the host), `consumer_id` the job's owner for a primary run and null for a replica or tiebreak (verification is the server's own work, given by the machine's owner and used by nobody), `cpu_core_s = min(reported cpu_s, observed wall × 1.05 + 1, 1.5 × reference)`, where `reference` is the job's reference cost (the cost table's `cpu_s`, P4.5.4, or the replica's own `cpu_s` when the job was replicated). The seconds are the helper's own report, so they are bounded twice: by what the server observed (a game is one thread; `flags.overreported_cpu` past it, which no honest helper can reach and which blocks promotion), and by the work itself (`flags.capped_cpu` past 1.5 × the reference: a CPU job is worth at most 1.5 times what it costs a reference machine, however long it is held or however slowly it runs; informational, since a slow honest machine reaches it too). The rate per core-second is still the machine's CPU class's (3-10); a machine's measured speed (`trust.speed`: the median of reference over its own `cpu_s` on its replicated jobs) is shown beside its class to administrators, and `flags.class_suspect` is set when it is under half the median speed of the other measured machines of its class, for an administrator to pin its class (`class_override`). `verification` is `pending` until the job settles, then `verified` (matched, or the host's), `unverified` (accepted without a replica) or `rejected` (credits set to 0, the hour's rollups recomputed, and any budget settlement it made reversed). `ingest_lab` and its reading of the results files go. A job admitted under a grant with a budget settles its primary run's credits into that budget as a model step does (3-10's keeper, through the ledger's writer), and admission checks the budget before each offer, so a hard budget stops CPU work at its limit.
- **Power rows** from helpers' `power` frames are written as the host's sampler writes its own (`usage`), so costing prices a helper machine's measured energy; the KNOWN_ISSUES item narrows to machines whose power cannot be read.

### P4.5.8 Trust tiers: replication, sampling, promotion, quarantine, withdrawal

**Settings** (`settings_kv.verification`, admins; defaults from plan §1 where it gives them): `new_streak` 5, `provisional_rate` 0.10, `trusted_rate` 0.02, `promote_verified` 50, `overreport_days` 30, `new_per_owner` 2, `new_verify_share` 0.5.

**Verifiers.** Only the local executor and machines an administrator marked `verifier` (`trust.verifier`, on a `trusted` machine) run replicas, and only matches against them count toward `streak` and `verified`. A `new` machine's replicas run on the local executor only. A trusted machine of another owner is not enough: one person with two accounts could have their trusted machine vouch for their fake one.

**The rules,** held by `citar/jobs/trust.py` and its table-driven tests:
1. **Replication is decided, and the replica created, only after the primary's result is committed.** On committing a primary's signed `job_result`, the dispatcher replicates the job always for a `new` machine, and for a `provisional` or `trusted` one when `blake3(server secret ‖ job id)`, read as a fraction, is below `provisional_rate` or `trusted_rate` (the machine's tier when the result arrived). Only then is a replica created and offered. Nothing about a primary's offer or run differs between sampled and unsampled jobs, and no replica exists while the primary is in flight, so a helper cannot learn which of its jobs will be checked before it has answered. If the primary fails, the job is simply re-queued. Runs on the local executor are never replicated.
2. **Visibility:** replicas, tiebreaks and the `settling` state are shown only to administrators until the job settles. To everyone else (the experiment's owner and viewers, the machine's owner) a job is `done` from the moment its primary's result is committed, in `GET /api/lab/jobs`, `GET /api/machines/{id}/jobs` and the Queue and Lab pages; once it settles they see the accepted run and its verification badge. What a helper's owner learns then is about a result already given.
3. **Load on the verifier:** a `new` machine is offered work only while its owner has fewer than `new_per_owner` other `new` machines with work in hand (a third waits); replicas of `new` machines take at most `new_verify_share` of the local executor's runs while other work waits (with one thread, every other run), the rest going to its own and to sampled work. A `new` machine whose replicas cannot be placed gets no more offers until they can (the Lab page says "waiting for a verifier").
4. **Settling a job:** with no replica, the primary's result is accepted (`unverified`). With one, the job is accepted when both match (spec hash, jobs build, result hash, chain head and rounds): `verified`. A replica that fails is re-offered: an infrastructure failure says nothing about the primary.
5. **A mismatch** against the local executor quarantines the primary's machine. Against a remote verifier, a tiebreak runs on the local executor, and every machine that disagrees with it is quarantined (a verifier can be wrong too). The verdicts and their evidence, kept on the run and the machine:
   - `result`: same chain, different result. A correct engine of one jobs build cannot produce it, so the evidence reads "tampering or an incompatible build" (the self-check makes the second unlikely, P4.4.3).
   - `chain`: the first round where the digests differ; worded as hardware or an incompatible build.
   - `signature`: a signature that does not verify against the registered key.
   - `chain_inconsistent`: digests that do not fold to the claimed head.
   - `claim`: a claim naming another job, run, machine, spec or jobs build, or a second result for a settled run with other hashes.
6. **Promotion:** each verified match against a verifier adds to `trust.verified` and `trust.streak`. `new` with `streak >= new_streak` becomes `provisional`; `provisional` with `verified >= promote_verified`, no mismatch, and no `flags.overreports` or `flags.overreported_cpu` in the last `overreport_days` becomes `trusted`. Each change writes an audit row (`server.trust`, actor `system`).
7. **Quarantine:** the machine gets no CPU job, and admission refuses it for any purpose but its owner's own use (code `quarantined`): a machine that returned a wrong game is not relied on for anyone else, its LLM work included. Its running runs are aborted and re-queued. A **tampering verdict** (`result`, `signature`, `claim`, `chain_inconsistent`) also sets the owner's `users.trust_tier` to `quarantined`: every machine of that owner is refused CPU work, and a newly registered row starts nowhere, until an administrator releases the owner. A `chain` verdict quarantines the machine only. A key reset never changes `trust`.
8. **Withdrawal:** on any quarantine, every `unverified` accepted result the machine produced since `trust.since` (its registration, or its last release from quarantine) is withdrawn, not only those after its last verified match: a matched replica proves only its own job, and a selective cheater is honest exactly when it guesses it is watched. Each withdrawn job's line leaves the results file, its run's event becomes `rejected` (credits 0, rollups recomputed, any budget settlement reversed), the job is re-queued to run elsewhere, and the ratings recompute. The evidence lists the withdrawn jobs, and words a `chain` verdict as hardware and a tampering verdict as tampering or an incompatible build.
9. **A verifier quarantined:** every job it verified (its replica or tiebreak runs that matched) returns to `unverified` and is queued for a fresh replica on the local executor; every machine whose promotion counted its matches loses those matches and, if it falls below its tier's threshold, returns to `new`.
10. **Administrators** can set any tier, mark a trusted machine `verifier`, release a machine (back to `new`, counters reset, `since` now) or an owner (the existing user administration's `trust_tier`) from quarantine, withdraw by hand when quarantining (`withdraw: true`), and ask that a machine's next N jobs be replicated (`POST /api/admin/machines/{id}/replicate`).
11. **LLM work** stays attributed, not re-verified (C2): its events carry `flags.tier`, the machine's tier when the step ran; over-reporting blocks promotion (rule 6) but does not quarantine. **Model results from helper machines** (benchmark games, probes, the diplomacy leaderboard's games) count toward the global leaderboards only when their machine was `trusted`, a site machine, or owned by an administrator when they ran; the others are listed with an "unverified machine" badge and left out of the global boards until an administrator trusts the machine. An LLM-only machine has no CPU jobs to be promoted by, so its owner asks an administrator, who can judge it by its results.

**Reading plan §6.7's "only verified or trusted results feed lab results, ratings and pooled data":** this design counts a `provisional` machine's unsampled results (rule 4), with rule 8 taking them all back if the machine is ever caught. The strict reading would keep 90% of a provisional machine's work out of the lab until it reached 50 verified jobs, which at a 10% sample is about 450 jobs: the work would be wasted or held for hours. With withdrawal back to the machine's registration, a cheater gains nothing: everything unverified it ever contributed leaves the lab, the ratings and its credits, and its owner is refused CPU work. For model work, rule 11 is the same sentence's reading ("pooled data" taken as the global leaderboards). Both readings are one owner question for confirmation.

### P4.5.9 The update relay

`citar/server/helper_updates.py`: fetches the manifest and signature of the server's own version (P4.3.8) at most hourly on a background thread (never on a request path; a failure keeps the last good manifest and is logged once an hour), verifies it with `engine_api.verify_manifest` against the keys the extension embeds (the same `update-keys.pub`; a test-ops build of the extension also trusts `CITAR_HELPER_UPDATE_KEY`, so the Python suite can use a throwaway key while no shipped wheel can be fooled by the environment), puts it in every welcome and sends `update` to connected helpers whose version is older. The Servers page shows each helper's version, the latest signed one, and its `update_status`.

### P4.5.10 Routes

Every route below is gated, gets P3.9.1's tests (stranger 404, visitor 401, owner, admin, moderator, a grantee where a group lends the machine), and joins `audit_routes.py --strict`.

| Route | Who | What |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /api/machines/{id}/key/reset` | MANAGE (owner, admin) | forget the registered key; the next hello registers its own; audited |
| `POST /api/machines/{id}/rescan` | MANAGE | send `rescan` to its helper; 409 when offline |
| `GET /api/machines/{id}/jobs` | MANAGE | its recent runs (state, kind, verdict once settled, times), experiment names only where the caller may view the experiment, its trust counters and last mismatch; replicas and unsettled verdicts for administrators only (P4.5.8 rule 2) |
| `GET /api/lab/jobs?exp=NAME` | VIEW on the experiment | its games' jobs and accepted runs, executors named where the caller may view the machine ("a member's machine" otherwise); a job is `done` from its primary's result until it settles, replicas for administrators only |
| `GET /api/admin/jobs` | admin | the dispatcher: queued by experiment, running per executor, verifiers, paused or not |
| `GET /api/admin/verification` | admin | the settings, quarantined machines and owners with evidence, recent mismatches and withdrawals, verifiers |
| `PUT /api/admin/verification` | admin | the settings (bounded: rates 0..1, counts 1..10,000) |
| `POST /api/admin/machines/{id}/replicate` | admin | replicate its next N jobs (1..100) |
| `PUT /api/admin/machines/{id}/trust` | admin (exists) | gains `withdraw` and `verifier` |

`GET /api/lab` and `GET /api/queue` change shape (the dispatcher's health; a "CPU jobs" section per machine the caller may view, others as "someone's lab game"), keeping their gates.

### P4.5.11 Pages

- **Servers page, a helper machine's card:** helper version, build, target and protocol; "update available" or its `update_status`; the key's fingerprint and "Reset key…"; CPU in effect (threads, CPU %, memory, each marked hard or soft for this OS); CPU runs now and today; the trust tier with verified count and streak; for admins, the last mismatch with its evidence, the measured speed beside the CPU class, the `verifier` mark, and Quarantine / Release.
- **Machine settings dialog** (3-18): the CPU section's note "takes effect with the 0.1.6 helper" goes; `yield_to_llm` joins it; what the helper's ceiling capped is shown, and "not lent by this machine's owner" when the helper's own switch is off; the grants whose purposes include `lab` are named; "Rescan now" on the hardware tab.
- **Groups and grants** (3-09's dialog and API): "Lab (CPU work)" is an opt-in purpose, never implied by an empty list (P4.5.5).
- **Queue page:** "CPU jobs" per machine.
- **Lab page:** per experiment, games accepted (verified, unverified, reference), running, waiting, withdrawn; per game its executor and, once settled, a verification badge; "waiting for a verifier" when that is why nothing starts.
- **Admin:** `#/admin/verification` (settings, quarantined machines and owners with evidence and Release, mismatches, withdrawals, verifiers) linked from the admin users page; a member gets the router's "not found".
- **The helper card** (`helper.js`): five platforms, Intel Mac included; on a Mac with no architecture hint (Safari, Firefox) both macOS downloads are offered as primary choices, "Apple silicon" and "Intel"; the pipx sentence goes.
