## P4.6 Threat model

**Assets:** a lender's machine (its CPU, memory, GPU, files, network, the API key a helper may front, its uptime); the server's data (lab results, ratings, the ledger and credits, model scores); members' accounts and the machines they own; the update channel.

**Actors:** (A) a malicious or compromised CITAR server, or its operator, against lenders; (B) a malicious lender or a modified helper against the server's data; (C) a broken but honest machine (bad RAM, an overclock, a denormal-flushing environment); (D) a network attacker between the two; (E) an attacker on the release channel (a compromised GitHub account or workflow); (F) another member against a machine they were not lent.

| Threat | Mitigation | Left over |
|---|---|---|
| A makes the helper run code, read files or reach the lender's network | No command, script or code is ever accepted; jobs are a closed vocabulary of typed, bounded specs running only compiled-in engine and bots (P4.4.1); completions go only to the configured endpoint with whitelisted parameters and the model allowlist; nothing reads outside the home | A bug in the engine or a spec parser on hostile input: the frames are fuzzed (4-01), with `job_offer.spec` an opaque object there; the typed specs are fuzzed once they exist, 2,000 mutated `LabSpec` and `ReferenceSpec` values through citar-jobs' spec parsing and the binding's `check_spec` (4-07) and through the job host's standard input (4-08), with no panic and nothing but `bad_spec` (4-01's fix round; their gates to carry it); and the job host runs under limits |
| A exhausts the lender's machine | CPU lending is off until the lender turns it on at the machine; the local ceiling caps every figure (local always wins); hard limits where the OS allows, soft and labelled where not (P4.3.6); local quiet hours; `yield_to_llm`; spec bounds and deadlines | macOS limits are soft; a lender who needs hard limits there sets fewer threads |
| A pushes malicious or old code as an update | Manifests are verified against public keys compiled into the helper, whose secrets the server never holds; no downgrade; sha256 and size checked; https only, redirects included; the staged binary self-tests; policy `off`/`notify` locally (`notify` in packaged builds) | The release key itself (E) |
| A learns more about the lender than it should | Hardware reports can be a file or nothing; power off locally; the key's secret never leaves | The prompts a lender's model serves are seen by the lender, as in 0.1.5: lending a GPU means seeing what it computes |
| B fakes results or poisons the ratings | Every result is a signed claim bound to its run, machine, spec and jobs build; chains and result hashes recomputed by the server; replication of every job of a `new` machine and samples after, decided only once a result is in and invisible to its owner until settled; verifiers only the server and administrator-marked machines; quarantine on any mismatch, of the owner too on tampering; withdrawal of everything unverified since registration or release; quarantined machines serve nobody else | A patient cheater on a `trusted` machine contaminates about 50 jobs on average (2% sampling) before it is caught; all of them are withdrawn when it is |
| B costs the server CPU, or fails a seat's turn, with a crafted completion (`done.message`: up to 8 MiB of text) | `parse_completion` (P4.5.2) takes time linear in the reply, since no pattern rescans the rest of the text from each place it might start (4-02's fix round: 1 MiB of the worst shape parses in under 0.4 s on the owner's laptop, where the code before took hours); it never raises on a message protocol 2 accepts; it yields at most 512 calls, with arguments that are objects nested at most 64 deep; a helper answers only the requests the server sends, one per seat step, and the parse runs in the seat's thread, never on the event loop | An 8 MiB reply of the worst shape still costs a few seconds of one core (about 3 s on the owner's laptop) |
| B inflates credits | CPU seconds bounded by observed wall time and by 1.5 times the job's reference cost; generation time by observed time (P3.3.4); a class out of line with the measured speed flagged; rejected runs earn nothing and reverse their budget settlements; replicas earn the provider credit but no consumer pays | A helper can be slow on purpose or hold results: it earns at most 1.5 times a job's reference cost and misses deadlines |
| B swamps verification or dodges quarantine with sybils (more accounts, re-registered rows) | Owner quarantine on tampering; at most `new_per_owner` new machines per owner offered work; `new` machines' replicas capped at half the local executor; a key reset keeps the tier | Many approved accounts can still occupy the verifiers' share; administrators see it on `#/admin/jobs` and suspend accounts |
| A local user of a lender's machine reads its token or key | `machine-key.json` 0600 or an owner-only DACL, refused at start when others can read it; the Windows service as a virtual account with a locked-down home (P4.3.7, P4.3.9) | An administrator of that machine can read anything on it |
| B serves a smaller model than claimed | Not verifiable (C2); events carry the tier; admins see `flags.overreports` | Canary prompts are a later option (P4.1.3) |
| B steals another machine's identity | Tokens hashed at rest and revocable; keys bound on first use and resettable only by the owner or an admin; the rate limit before any lookup, counting distinct tokens | A token stolen before the real helper's first hello registers the thief's key; the fingerprint on the Servers page shows it, and a reset fixes it |
| C produces honest wrong results | The canary refuses jobs where denormals are flushed or read as zero, and the self-check where the golden job comes out differently; otherwise the same machinery as B, with evidence naming the round and worded as hardware, and the machine's owner not quarantined for a `chain` verdict | — |
| D reads or alters traffic | TLS with the OS verifier; `--insecure` only for loopback and private hosts; `--ca-file` for self-signed servers | — |
| E signs a malicious release | Phase 5's custody decision (offline key, or a protected environment with a required reviewer); two key slots for rotation; attestations | The owner's key management |
| F uses a machine they were not lent | Admission by grant and an explicitly listed `lab` purpose for every offer (an empty list never lends CPU); administrators' own experiments admitted like members'; owners' own experiments only on their own machines otherwise; `collection` kinds only on machines that opted in | — |

`SECURITY.md` gains this as its "Helpers and shared machines" section (4-12, extended in 4-13): what a worker token allows (serving completions and running the closed job vocabulary under the owner's limits), the claims a lender can rely on (P4.3.10), and the verification scheme. `WORKERS.md` "What the server can and cannot do" is rewritten from the same table.

---

## P4.7 Cross-cutting rules

### P4.7.1 Safety on this laptop (binding on every package)

1. **Never** install a system service, a scheduled task or a startup entry on Windows, change a system or security setting (firewall, Defender, UAC, any registry value), or run anything elevated. Service code is tested by `--dry-run` and unit tests of the generated definitions; real installs run only in WSL with `systemd --user` (per-user), and in CI. A test that could install anything for real if it were elevated (4-14's `install --system` check) first asserts the process is not elevated and aborts if it is.
2. **Never** touch the owner's real helper configuration or data: `helper.json`, `worker.json` and `machine-key.json` in their real places (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\CITAR`, the repository's `config/`), `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CITAR\citar.db`, `secret_key`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CITAR\config\servers.json` and the repository's `config/servers.json`. Every helper run uses `--home` (and `CITAR_DATA_DIR`) in its own scratch or QA directory, and every helper the packages build is a `dev-guard` build that refuses to start without an explicit `--home` outside `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CITAR` (P4.3.2). S10 fingerprints them before and after.
3. **Never** generate a release signing private key into the repository or commit any private key. Tests generate throwaway keys at run time in their temporary directory (`cargo xtask keygen` refuses a path inside the repository; a gate greps the tree for key files and PEM headers). The real key's custody is the owner's question for Phase 5.
4. **Never** connect a test helper to `citar.jimmieprodgers.com` or any non-local server: the QA harness starts its own servers on loopback (or inside WSL), helpers built with test features or `dev-guard` (every build `cargo xtask develop` makes) dial loopback only (P4.3.1), and no package hands a helper a URL from anywhere else.
5. **CPU-limit tests** use small limits and short runs: at most 2 cores' worth and 10 s each, at most 512 MB.
6. **Never** port 8799 or `C:/dev/citar-play` (the harness refuses them, as `qa_phase3.py` does).
7. **LM Studio** (`http://localhost:1234`, `google/gemma-4-e2b`): read its model list and ask it for completions of that model only; never load, unload or swap a model or change its settings; check that the model is loaded before and after.
8. **WSL** is `wsl -d Ubuntu-24.04 -u claude` (root via `-u root` only inside WSL, and only where a package says why); never handle the WSL password; build in `~/` (never under `/mnt/c`), in a copy of the branch made by `git bundle` or `git clone`, with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=~/target/citar-p-4-xx`. **A service gate runs in one `wsl` invocation** whose script installs, checks and uninstalls under a `trap` that uninstalls on any exit: `claude` has `Linger=no`, so the user manager stops when an invocation's session ends, and a unit left enabled would start again at the next one. **The QA server binds `0.0.0.0` inside WSL only after `wslinfo --networking-mode` says `nat`** (in mirrored mode it would listen on the LAN); otherwise it binds `127.0.0.1`, and the part records whether Windows still reaches it.
9. Never push, never touch the VPS, never write in the main checkout (it is only read).

### P4.7.2 Conformance fixtures

P4.2.9: every frame type has valid and invalid fixtures; every behaviour the protocol promises has a transcript; the helper's Rust tests and the hub's Python tests both run all of them; a package that changes the protocol changes PROTOCOL.md, the fixtures and both sides in the same commit series. A behaviour that is only tested on one side is a review finding.

### P4.7.3 Access and the route audit

P3.9.1 stands: every new or changed route gets stranger, visitor, owner, admin and moderator tests (and a grantee's where groups lend the machine), 404 rather than 403 where the caller cannot see the object, `Depends(get_db, scope="function")` (3-19's rule, enforced by `test_access`), and `audit_routes.py --strict` with 0 unguarded. Job frames are authorised by the connection (a helper only ever acts on runs offered to it: a frame naming another machine's run is dropped and logged).

### P4.7.4 Locks, threads, the event loop, the database

- P3.9.2 stands (no model call, disk write or database write under a session lock; every wait has a timeout; every timer thread survives its own exceptions and is restarted).
- **The event loop never blocks:** database writes, scans' records, power rows and token lookups go through `asyncio.to_thread`; job frames go onto the dispatcher's queue.
- **The dispatcher** is the only writer of `cpu_jobs` and `cpu_job_runs` (one thread); routes read them. Results files are written only by it, through `fsutil` (atomic replace on a rewrite); a listing of files newest first uses `fsutil.newest_first` (`p3-notes.md`).
- **The helper:** one session task owns the socket; requests and jobs are tasks; blocking work on `spawn_blocking`; the job host is a separate process.

### P4.7.5 The standard gates

Every package's gates include, besides its own (P3.9.3's, renumbered where Phase 4 adds):
- **S1** the Python suite (`python -m unittest discover -s tests`), with `cargo xtask develop` (which from 4-03 also builds the helper, with `dev-guard`, into `citar-ext/`, where the end-to-end tests find it) when Rust changed; its count recorded. **S1b** the whole suite on Python 3.12 under `TZ=UTC0` (`C:/dev/venv/py312-check` with `CITAR_EXT_DIR` pointing at the worktree's extension), as Phase 3's merges ran it.
- **S2** ruff. **S3** `audit_routes.py --strict`. **S4** `check_links.py --strict`. **S5** when `docs/` changed: `mkdocs build --strict` and `build_wiki.py` with `wiki/` committed.
- **S6** the front-end checks (ESM parse of every module, `node --test citar/web/tests/`).
- **S7** when Rust changed: `cargo fmt --check`; clippy `--workspace --all-targets --all-features --locked -D warnings`; nextest `--workspace --all-features --locked`; the doctests; `cargo xtask check`; the refcheck run (`--fixtures refcheck/fixtures-mini --fixtures refcheck/fixtures-late --strict --with-bot`); `cargo golden check`; from 4-07, when the engine, the bot, citar-sim or citar-jobs changed, the `jobs` golden set in the `release` profile too (`cargo nextest run --cargo-profile release -p citar-jobs --locked`).
- **S8** the package's part of `scripts/qa_phase4.py` (P4.7.6) exits 0, and its browser items (P4.7.7).
- **S9** when `citar-helper` or `citar-jobs` changed: in WSL, `cargo nextest run -p citar-helper -p citar-jobs --locked` (and from 4-07 the same for citar-jobs in the `release` profile) and `cargo golden check` in the branch's copy (Linux x64, CI's stand-in for ubuntu).
- **S10** the owner's data and the machine are as they were: before and after the package's runs, the sha256 of the repository's `config/servers.json` (starts `586888cbb61aa9c7`) and of `%LOCALAPPDATA%/CITAR/config/servers.json` (starts `2561079bd2f123e1`), the mtimes of `%LOCALAPPDATA%/CITAR/citar.db` and `secret_key`, the presence, size and mtime of `%LOCALAPPDATA%/CITAR/helper.json`, `machine-key.json` and the repository's `config/worker.json`; no `citar-helper`, `job-host` or QA server process left; `sc query citar-helper` reports no such service; no `CITAR` value under `HKCU\...\Run`; no `CITAR` scheduled task; in WSL no `citar` unit under `~/.config/systemd/user` and none listed by `systemctl --user list-unit-files`; no `CITAR_*` variable left set; the main checkout shows only `.claude/launch.json`.
- **CI-only** (a worktree never pushes): test.yml on its three OSes; rust.yml; determinism.yml (the `jobs` set on five targets, both profiles); `helper.yml` from 4-14 (P4.7.10). Nobody labels or dispatches nightly.yml, docs.yml or release.yml without the owner's OK.

### P4.7.6 The QA script

- `scripts/qa_phase4.py <part>|all [--port N] [--dir D] [--keep] [--helper-bin PATH] [--wsl]`, reusing `qa_phase3.py`'s harness (the dev server child, the refusals of port 8799, `C:/dev/citar-play` and non-empty foreign directories, the accounts, the evidence files, the scripted endpoint), imported rather than copied.
- **Helpers** are the real Rust binary (`--helper-bin`, else `CITAR_EXT_DIR/citar-helper[.exe]`), each started with `--home <dir>/helper-<n>`, `--server http://127.0.0.1:<port>` (or the WSL server's forwarded `localhost`), and its own token; the harness stops them all at its end and checks none is left.
- **The scripted endpoint** (3-19's) gains streaming (SSE with per-delta delays, reasoning deltas, tool-call deltas) and records, for each request, the tokens it sent and when the client disconnected, so cancel is checked at the endpoint.
- **Parts:** `helper` and `llm-real` (4-05, LM Studio), `scan` (4-06), `lab` (4-09), `remote` (4-10), `verify` (4-11, 4-12), `update` (4-13), `service` (4-14, WSL), `exit` and `real` (4-15), and `all` (the others in that order; `real` and `llm-real` need LM Studio and can be skipped with `--skip` for development runs, never at the exit).
- **Topology for `exit` and `remote --wsl`:** the dev server runs **in WSL** (bound to `0.0.0.0` inside WSL only once `wslinfo --networking-mode` says `nat`, else `127.0.0.1`, P4.7.1 rule 8; in its own directory, with a venv and extension built there by `cargo xtask develop`), the Windows helper dials it through WSL's localhost forwarding (`http://localhost:<port>` from Windows), and the WSL helpers dial `127.0.0.1` inside WSL; the server's local executor (Linux) is the verifier. This makes "a remote lab experiment" cross-OS (Windows results replicated against Linux ones) without a server on Windows listening beyond loopback, which could raise a firewall prompt. If Windows cannot reach the WSL server through localhost forwarding, the part records it and runs the fallback: the server on Windows bound to `127.0.0.1` with two Windows helper processes and the local executor, and the WSL helper's conformance against a WSL server separately.
- `qa_phase3.py`'s parts keep working with the Rust helper (4-05 switches its helper processes), so Phase 3's QA stays a regression gate.
- **The extension and the helper are built in one profile** (`cargo xtask develop`'s), so the local executor and the helpers share a jobs build; the evidence records the profile.

### P4.7.7 The browser checklist

Run in the browser pane against the QA script's server (`--keep`), each item with screenshots in the QA directory, ticked in the package's as-built note:
- **H1** a helper machine's card: helper version, build, target, protocol 2; the key's fingerprint and "Reset key…"; CPU in effect, hard and soft marked; the latest signed helper and an "update available" state.
- **H2** the machine settings dialog: the CPU section live with what the helper's ceiling capped, "not lent by this machine's owner" for a helper whose own switch is off, and the grants that include `lab`; "Rescan now" updates the last report's time.
- **H3** the Queue page's CPU jobs per machine: the owner's own experiment named, another member's "someone's lab game".
- **H4** the Lab page: per game its executor and, once settled, its verification badge; a sampled job shown `done` to its owner until it settles, with no replica in sight; a withdrawn game shown re-queued; "waiting for a verifier".
- **H5** `#/admin/verification`: settings, a quarantined machine and its quarantined owner with the evidence (the round where it parted, or "same chain, different result: tampering or an incompatible build") and the withdrawn jobs, Release; a member's "not found".
- **H6** the helper card: five downloads, Intel Mac among them, no pipx sentence; with a Safari user agent on a Mac, both macOS downloads offered.
- **H7** the group's grant dialog: "Lab (CPU work)" offered as an opt-in purpose and not implied by "every purpose".

### P4.7.8 Docs

Each package updates what its behaviour touches, named in its scope: `docs/server/WORKERS.md` (4-03, 4-05, 4-06, 4-08, 4-10, 4-11, 4-12, 4-13, 4-14: rewritten section by section into "the CITAR helper"), `SECURITY.md` (4-12, 4-13), `docs/API.md` (every new route), `docs/CONFIGURATION.md` (the helper's settings, the host's CPU defaults, `helper_manifest_url`), `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` (4-02, 4-05, 4-07, 4-09, 4-11), `docs/BOTS.md` and `docs/research/BOT_TUNING.md` (the lab runs in the server, 4-09), `CONTRIBUTING.md` (the helper's dev loop, WSL, `qa_phase4.py`), `KNOWN_ISSUES.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` (each package's lines; 4-15's pass), `packaging/README.md` (4-14); the wiki regenerated with them.

### P4.7.9 Performance

Measured as P3.9.7 says: the baseline in the same run, alternating, five times each, medians compared; never a number from another day; CI-run timing assertions get three times their local bound (`p3-notes.md`); durations by `time.perf_counter` in Python and `Instant` in Rust; never sleep-then-assert (wait on the condition with a deadline).
- **The session's overhead** (`scripts/session_overhead.py`) at most 2.87 where the scheduler or hub changed (4-02, 4-05).
- **The scheduler:** under 1 ms a step with nothing queued (P3.9.7), unchanged by v2.
- **The hub:** 1,000 request and done round trips through a `FakeHelper` that answers at once: the hub's own time per round trip (observed minus the FakeHelper's) median under 3 ms.
- **The dispatcher:** a tick with 2,000 queued jobs and 10 executors under 50 ms (median of 100 ticks).
- **Job overhead:** a 30-round duel through the local job host against `engine_api.run_job` in-process, same run: at most 10% plus 0.2 s more; the same duel through a helper on the same machine against the local job host: at most 15% plus 0.5 s more. The Rust lab game against the Python `lab.play` it replaces (4-07): no slower.

### P4.7.10 CI parity and CI-only gates

- Locally: Windows x64 natively, Linux x64 in WSL (S9), Python 3.12 under `TZ=UTC0` (S1b), `--locked` everywhere, the `ci` profile's checks in nextest.
- **CI-only, named in each package that relies on them:** macOS arm64 and x64 and Linux arm64 builds and conformance (`helper.yml`, from 4-14; until then rust.yml's three OSes run the helper's tests on Windows, Linux and macOS arm64); the `jobs` golden set on all five targets in the `ci` and `release` profiles (determinism.yml, from 4-07); the Windows service installed, started, stopped and removed on `windows-latest`; the launchd agent on macOS (plist lint, bootstrap where the runner allows); Linux `systemd --user` on ubuntu (after `sudo loginctl enable-linger runner`); macOS's soft limits reported; `test.yml`'s end-to-end helper tests on its three OSes.

### P4.7.11 Process

As Phase 3: a worktree per package (`.claude/worktrees/p-4-xx`, branch `p-4-xx`); `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=C:/dev/target/citar-p-4-xx` before every cargo command (outside OneDrive); files written with the Write tool (Git Bash mangles backslashes in heredocs); small commits ending with the Co-Authored-By line; never `ops/`, `saves/` or `.claude/launch.json`; never a push. Every package ends with its gates, then a review and a fix round (the owner's standing rule), and its as-built note records its start and end times. Handoff notes for the next package go to `ops/p4-notes.md` (gitignored), as `p3-notes.md` did.

---

## P4.8 Decisions taken in this design

1. **v1 is refused, with a message a 0.1.5 helper prints, and a v2 helper waits for an old server** (P4.2.8); no transition window, and the hub carries exactly one protocol at every merge (P4.11).
2. **One WebSocket path**, `/ws/worker`, for both the refusal and v2.
3. **Additive within v2; job specs closed** (P4.2.1).
4. **Raw completions; one parser on the server** (P4.5.2); `json` mode's protocol prompt built by the server.
5. **Streaming between the helper and its endpoint, for cancel;** one `done` to the server.
6. **Cancel is acknowledged;** draining only for an unacknowledged cancel, flagged.
7. **Settings are acknowledged with what is in effect;** the scheduler and dispatcher read the acknowledgement.
8. **Rate limit before lookup, hop-aware address, `last_ip` written** (defects of P4.0).
9. **Machine keys are trust-on-first-use per row,** resettable by the owner or an admin without changing the machine's trust; owner-only on disk (0600, or an owner-only DACL), refused at start otherwise; signing in the helper's parent process, never in the job host.
10. **A shared `citar-jobs` crate** holds everything both sides must compute identically: specs and hashes, the lab result, the chain, claims and signatures, the update manifest, the job host and its limits.
11. **The kinds:** lab, ranking and reference games; ranking and reference are admins' `collection` experiments; reference results are baseline lines.
12. **Crashes are results:** final, verified like any other, never retried; a job never runs under a wall-clock budget, so a deadline is a failure and never a result.
13. **One job host process per executor,** applying its limits to itself, used by the helper and by the server alike; no orphans (stdin EOF ends it); a canary and a golden self-check before any job; memory counted per run; a host of another jobs build refused.
14. **Limits per OS** as P4.3.6's table, each reported hard or soft and shown.
15. **`yield_to_llm`** pauses a helper's games while its model works; no CPU job offered while a benchmark lease is live on the machine.
16. **The dispatcher lives in the server process;** `citar lab run` is retired; the host's machine row is the local executor and its settings replace the lab's worker counts.
17. **The local executor is trusted by definition** and verifies every `new` machine; otherwise only machines an administrator marked `verifier` verify, so no owner can vouch for their own machines through a second account.
18. **Sampling is keyed by the server's secret and decided after the result is in;** replicas are invisible to non-administrators until the job settles; trusted machines are still sampled at 2%.
19. **Provisional results count, with withdrawal on quarantine of everything unverified since registration or release** (P4.5.8; an owner question confirms the reading, and rule 11's for model results).
20. **Quarantine stops a machine's work for anyone but its owner,** CPU and LLM alike; a tampering verdict quarantines the owner too; release returns a machine to `new`.
21. **Ledger events are written per run** with `pending` until settled; replicas are given by their machine's owner and used by nobody; CPU seconds are bounded by observation and by 1.5 times the reference cost; rejected runs earn nothing and reverse their budget settlements; `ingest_lab` goes.
22. **Updates follow the server's own version,** relayed and verified by the server, verified again by the helper, never downgraded, applied when idle and handed off by a supervisor that rolls back; `notify` only in packaged builds; no key ships in Phase 4.
23. **Services:** per-user by default (systemd user unit, LaunchAgent, HKCU Run entry with its own supervisor), a real Windows service (a virtual account, a locked-down home) and a system unit on request with elevation done by the owner; exit codes 0, 75 and 78, and no exit at all on a permanent refusal in service mode.
24. **Test features** (`test-faults`, `test-update-key`, `test-jobs`) and the development guard (`dev-guard`) never ship (xtask) and confine a build to loopback; `dev-guard` also demands an explicit `--home`.
25. **The Linux release builds stay glibc-based** (on ubuntu-22.04, as today's helper), with rustls and the platform verifier, so TLS no longer depends on where a distribution keeps its CA bundle.
26. **The Python worker is deleted,** with its extra, its script, the wizard's flow and the install scripts' paths replaced by a pointer to the helper.
27. **Release names are unchanged** plus `citar-helper-macos-x64`; 4-14 switches release.yml's helper job to cargo with those names and a test holds helper.js to them; the rest of the release is Phase 5's.
28. **QA runs the server in WSL** for the cross-OS remote experiment, so no Windows listener ever leaves loopback.
29. **Jobs have their own compatibility key,** `jobs_build`, covering the runner, the jobs crate and the build flavour (P4.4.5).
30. **CPU lending is off on the machine until its owner turns it on there,** and a grant lends CPU only when it lists `lab`; administrators' own experiments need grants like anyone's.
31. **The protocol acknowledges results, expires offers and silent runs, and backs off re-offers;** a lost connection cancels the helper's completions.

## P4.9 Risks

1. **A target computes another game.** The engine's matrix holds today; the `jobs` set adds the job runner to it on five targets in both profiles, WSL checks it locally, every job host plays the shortest golden job before taking work (a machine that computes it differently is `unverifiable`, never quarantined), jobs are compared only within one jobs build, and a mismatch between honest machines that slipped past all that would show as a quarantine with "chain parted at round R", which the evidence makes diagnosable. Mitigation for an outbreak: an admin can release machines and set the rates to 1.0 while it is investigated.
2. **OS limit APIs** (Job Objects, cgroup delegation, macOS's lack of hard caps). One small `unsafe` module with SAFETY comments; each limit measured by a test; soft limits reported as soft, never claimed hard.
3. **The update channel.** Signatures, no downgrade, self-test, rollback; no key until the owner decides custody.
4. **Verification load on a one-vCPU VPS.** New machines double their work until five matches, and their replicas run only on the host, which verifies at 1 thread by default; at most two new machines per owner get work, and new machines' replicas take at most every other run of the host's while other work waits; admins can mark trusted machines `verifier`; "waiting for a verifier" is visible rather than silent.
5. **WSL networking.** Localhost forwarding from Windows into WSL is assumed and probed; the fallback topology is defined (P4.7.6).
6. **LM Studio's behaviour on a closed stream.** Measured, not assumed: the cancel check compares a following request's latency with the same run's baseline.
7. **A helper newer than its server** (updated elsewhere), or built in another profile: LLM work goes on (same protocol); CPU jobs are not offered across jobs builds until the two match, and the Servers page says so.
8. **This laptop throttles** (P3.9.7): timings within a run only; limits checked as upper bounds.
9. **Fifteen packages in sequence.** Each sized like a Phase 3 package or smaller; the two at the upper bound (4-05, 4-09) name where to split, and the proof of work is split in two (4-11, 4-12) from the start.

## P4.10 What Phase 5 builds (the hooks Phase 4 leaves)

- **The helper's release job** (rewritten in 4-14, enabled when Phase 5 removes the gate): `cargo build --release --locked -p citar-helper` on `windows-latest` (`x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` → `citar-helper-windows-x64.exe`), `macos-15` (`aarch64-apple-darwin` → `citar-helper-macos-arm64`), `macos-15-intel` (`x86_64-apple-darwin` → `citar-helper-macos-x64`), `ubuntu-22.04` (`x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` → `citar-helper-linux-x64`) and `ubuntu-22.04-arm` (`aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu` → `citar-helper-linux-arm64`), each with its `.sha256`, each run once with `--version` and `--self-test`; no features (the `packaged` feature only in the tap's, bucket's and winget's own builds, P4.3.8).
- **The manifest:** `cargo xtask helper-manifest` over the five assets (versioned tag URLs) → `citar-helper-manifest.json`; signed by `cargo xtask sign-manifest` → `.sig`; both attached to the release beside the binaries.
- **The key:** `crates/citar-jobs/update-keys.pub` gets the owner's public key in a release commit; where the secret lives and who signs is the owner's decision (owner question 1). Until then helpers report updates `unavailable`.
- **Packaging:** the Homebrew tap formula for the helper only (R4: macOS arm64 and x64, Linux), Scoop and winget, install scripts downloading the helper for `--worker`, attestations, code signing if ever, the release dry run.

## P4.11 Work breakdown at a glance

Fifteen packages, strictly in this order, one agent at a time. Each ends with its gates, a review and a fix round, and its as-built note.

| # | Package | Plan §7 Phase 4 part |
|---|---|---|
| 4-01 | Protocol v2: the specification, the frame types on both sides, the shared fixtures | 1 |
| 4-02 | The schema and the one parser on the server (the hub untouched) | 1 |
| 4-03 | The Rust helper: configuration, the connection, keys, settings, catalog | 2 |
| 4-04 | The Rust helper's LLM proxy and real cancel | 2 |
| 4-05 | The hub on v2 only; the Python worker retired | 1, 2 |
| 4-06 | The hardware scan and power samples in Rust | 2 |
| 4-07 | Jobs in Rust: the vocabulary, the lab game, reference games, the jobs build, the chain and the result hash | 3 |
| 4-08 | The job host and CPU limits per OS | 3 |
| 4-09 | The dispatcher and the local executor; results into the lab, the ratings and the ledger | 3 |
| 4-10 | Remote executors: CPU jobs on helpers | 3 |
| 4-11 | Proof of work: signed claims, verification, trust tiers, quarantine, withdrawal | 4 |
| 4-12 | Proof of work, continued: credits by reference cost, model results by tier, the admin UI | 4 |
| 4-13 | Signed self-update | 5 |
| 4-14 | Service installation, the release names, helper.yml, the helper docs | 5 |
| 4-15 | Phase 4 exit: conformance, the remote experiment verified, the faulty helper, the real model, the evidence | exit |

The order follows the plan's five parts and four rules of its own:
- **Exactly one protocol in the hub at every merge** (the owner's decision of 2026-10-14). The hub stays v1, untouched, until 4-05, which switches it to v2 only and deletes the Python worker in the same package. Before that, 4-02 lands only what does not touch the hub (the schema, and the one parser, which the Python worker reaches unchanged through `OpenAIConversation`), and 4-03 and 4-04 build the Rust helper against the transcripts, played by an in-process fake server: the same transcripts the hub's side plays in 4-05. The repository has a working helper at every merge: the Python worker until 4-05, the Rust helper from it.
- **The Rust lab game (4-07) and the job host (4-08) before the dispatcher (4-09),** so the dispatcher's first executor runs the same code its remote executors will.
- **Remote executors (4-10) before verification (4-11, 4-12),** with remote work offered only to machines an administrator trusted in between.
- **The update (4-13) before the services (4-14),** whose exit codes carry the update's restart.

**The design review** (2026-10-14) raised 1 blocker, 12 majors and 10 minors. Each was checked against the code at `1d5ca71`, the plan and the owner's decisions; all were found valid and are fixed in this revision. Five are fixed in a form that differs in one point from the review's suggestion, each explained in the row (the last column names the sections and packages).

| Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Blocker: the hub would speak v1 and v2 during 4-02 and 4-03 ("v2 beside v1", gate "v1 still serves"), against the owner's decision of 2026-10-14 | The review's main fix: 4-02 lands only the schema and the one parser (`OpenAIConversation` calls it; the hub untouched); the Rust helper is built in 4-03 and 4-04 against the transcripts on an in-process fake server; 4-05 switches the hub to v2 only and deletes the Python worker in the same package, with `qa_phase3.py` on the Rust binary and the end-to-end tests. The old gate "v1 still serves" is gone; P4.11 says one protocol at every merge; the refusal is the handshake's single first check | P4.2.8, P4.5.1, P4.5.2, P4.11; 4-02 to 4-05 |
| `build_id` misses citar-sim, citar-jobs and the build flavour, so honest machines of "one build" can disagree and be quarantined; the canary misses denormals-are-zero | `jobs_build` over the build id, citar-sim's and citar-jobs' content codes (new build scripts) and the flavour, carried by the hello, offers, claims, `cpu_jobs` and the local executor; matching and comparison only within it; QA builds both sides in one profile; the canary checks DAZ too; every job host plays the shortest golden job before taking work (`unverifiable` rather than quarantined); evidence reads "tampering or an incompatible build" | P4.0, P4.2.2, P4.2.5, P4.4.3, P4.4.5, P4.5.8; 4-07, 4-08 |
| `ReferenceSpec` as citar-sim's `Options` carries a path and a wall-clock budget, and hashed results carry `seconds`, `cpu_s`, traces and `errors` with traces | Typed `ReferenceSpec` and `ReferenceResult` with no budget, path, workers, name or checks and no measurements; no job runs with `RunSpec.budget` (a deadline is `job_failed`, never a result); `errors` holds messages only; measurements and traces written outside the hash; 4-07 tests a slowed run, 4-08 a run capped at 10% and paused | P4.0, P4.4.1, P4.4.2; 4-07 gate 3, 4-08 gate 3 |
| Withdrawal only after the last verified match lets a selective cheater keep earlier fakes | Withdrawal of every unverified result since registration or release from quarantine; `result-at:` fault mode; a gate with an altered job before a later verified match | P4.4.6, P4.5.8 rule 8; 4-11 gate 3, 4-15 gate 3 |
| Sampling visible while the primary is in flight (job listings, Queue and Lab pages) | Replicas created only after the primary's result is committed; replicas, tiebreaks and `settling` shown to administrators only until settled; a job is `done` for everyone else from its primary's result; a gate that the owner's listings are identical whether or not a job will be sampled | P4.5.4 (`settling`), P4.5.8 rules 1-2, P4.5.10, P4.5.11; 4-11 gate 4 |
| Collusion through another account's trusted machine; quarantine dodged by re-registering; new machines can starve the verifier | Verifiers are the local executor and administrator-marked machines only (only their matches count; `new` machines' replicas on the local executor only); a quarantined verifier's matches re-opened and promotions that relied on them undone; tampering quarantines the owner (`users.trust_tier`); a key reset keeps the tier; at most two new machines per owner offered work; new machines' replicas capped at half the local executor's runs while other work waits. One test per rule | P4.5.8 (verifiers, rules 3, 7, 9, 10), P4.6; 4-11 gates 2, 5 |
| CPU credits from self-reported seconds bounded only by observed wall time (up to 10x by holding results); class rate from a self-reported scan; budget not refunded on rejection | `cpu_core_s = min(reported, observed × 1.05 + 1, 1.5 × reference)` with the reference cost table (median of local and verifier runs; the replica's own when replicated); budget settlements reversed on `rejected`; measured speed beside the class and `class_suspect`; `cpu:3` and the new `hold:S` gated. **Differs from the suggestion in one point:** `overreported_cpu` (which blocks promotion) is set past the observation bound, which no honest helper reaches; passing the reference cap sets the informational `capped_cpu`, because a slow honest machine passes it too and must still be promotable | P4.5.4 (`job_costs`), P4.5.7, P4.4.6; 4-12 gate 1 |
| Protocol gaps: no result acknowledgement or idempotency, no offer expiry, no stale-run detection, orphaned completions after a dropped connection, unbounded re-offers | `job_result_ack`, `hello.results`, `welcome.jobs.resend`, results kept on disk until acknowledged, duplicates acknowledged or treated as claim failures; `offer_ttl_s` 30 s with `job_abort` for a late accept; a run silent for 5 minutes expires; a lost connection cancels every in-flight completion; re-offers back off per (machine, reason). Transcripts for each; gates for reconnect-after-send and the lost offer | P4.2.2, P4.2.4, P4.2.5, P4.3.3, P4.3.6; 4-01, 4-04 gate 1, 4-10 gate 1 |
| Update hand-off: the console run fights `replaced`, a long-lived job host keeps old code, package-managed installs, redirects | The old process stops work and its job host, reports `restarting`, closes its socket, swaps, and supervises the new binary (rollback when it exits before `healthy`); service runs exit 75 and the new binary's start count rolls back; `replaced` never the success signal; the job host reports its `jobs_build` and a different one is refused; the `packaged` feature (Phase 5) defaults to `notify` and refuses `auto`; redirects followed over `https` only, P4.3.10 says so | P4.2.6, P4.2.7, P4.3.6, P4.3.8, P4.3.10; 4-13 gates 3-4 |
| Exit semantics vs service managers (Run entry unsupervised, SCM non-crash failures, systemd/launchd loops on permanent refusals) and the rate limit locking out neighbours | Exit codes 0, 75, 78; in service mode a permanent refusal idles and retries hourly (launchd cannot exclude a code), with `RestartPreventExitStatus=78` and `Restart=on-failure` as systemd's second net; `run --detach` runs a supervisor; the Windows service's failure actions with the non-crash flag; the rate limit counts distinct failing token hashes per address. **Differs in one point:** idling in service mode applies to every service manager, not only launchd, so all behave alike | P4.2.2, P4.2.7, P4.3.9, P4.5.1; 4-05 gate 5, 4-14 gates 1-3 |
| The Windows system service as LocalSystem with its home under `%ProgramData%` (Users can read the token and key); P4.3.7's ACL claim false outside `%LOCALAPPDATA%` | The service runs as `NT SERVICE\citar-helper`; install copies the binary into `%ProgramData%\CITAR\helper\bin` and sets the home's DACL (SYSTEM, Administrators, the account; no inheritance); the helper writes the key with an owner-only DACL and refuses a key file others can read, warning about `helper.json`. **Differs in one point:** the DACL check goes through citar-jobs' `sys` module (renamed from `limits/sys`), not `icacls`, whose output is localised | P4.3.1, P4.3.7, P4.3.9, P4.6; 4-03 gate 4, 4-14 gate 1 |
| An empty grant purposes list admits other members' lab jobs onto a GPU lender's CPU | `_purpose_ok`: an empty list means every model purpose and never `lab`; the grants UI shows "Lab (CPU work)" as opt-in; the dialog names the grants that include it; 4-10's eligibility test covers an empty list. **Also found:** administrators bypass grants in `admission.check`, which would put an administrator's own lab experiment on every member's opted-in machine; that bypass no longer applies to CPU work | P4.0, P4.5.5, P4.5.11, P4.6; 4-10 gate 4, browser H7 |
| The local ceiling defaults to lending almost everything, so "local always wins" protects nothing | Local `cpu.enabled` off by default; `setup` asks (default no); `--cpu` for one run; explicit-less defaults half the CPUs, 50%, a quarter of the RAM; the dialog says "not lent by this machine's owner"; a migrated 0.1.5 `helper.json` gets CPU off (4-03 gate 1) | P4.2.2, P4.3.2, P4.5.11, P4.6; 4-03 gate 1 |
| v1 detection rule refuses every v2 hello; "does not hammer" false under the 0.1.5 system unit | The hub accepts only `protocol == 2`; any other hello gets the one-line refusal and 4426, no lookup, throttled logging; the runbook and `WORKERS.md` say to `systemctl disable --now citar-worker` | P4.0, P4.2.8, P4.5.1; 4-05 gate 6, 4-14 |
| Memory: per-run peaks unmeasurable, memory deaths unclassified, no per-job requirement, a server-mode default above the VPS's RAM | A counting global allocator with per-run tallies; classification from the allocation-failure line, the cgroup's `oom_kill`, or the abort's code; `mem_mb` per job from the cost table, raised by a memory death, matched against memory per thread; server mode min(1,024 MB, 25% of the RAM) | P4.2.5, P4.4.3, P4.5.4, P4.5.5, P4.5.6; 4-08 gates 1-2, 5; 4-09 gate 5 |
| The `jobs` golden set in citar-testkit (refused by the crate graph; built with `legacy` and `test-ops`, `ci` only) | The review's better option: the set lives in citar-jobs with its own test binary, no engine test features, run in `ci` and `release` locally (S7, S9) and on five targets (determinism.yml, helper.yml); the helper's self-check embeds its shortest job | P4.4.5, P4.7.5, P4.7.10; 4-07 gate 2 |
| Gates that cannot fail or are ambiguous (CPU upper bounds only; ±3% sampling; no `cpu:F` gate; withdrawal scope) | Lower bounds (burn reaches at least 40% of the cap; games' rounds advance); the binomial bound (the distance between observed and p at most 4·sqrt(p(1−p)/n)) for each rate and two secrets; the `cpu:3` gate; the selective withdrawal gate | 4-08 gate 1, 4-10 gate 2, 4-11 gates 1, 3, 4-12 gate 1 |
| A fake `nvidia-smi` first on `PATH` loses to System32's on Windows; script fakes are not found | Probe programs injectable through `CITAR_HELPER_NVIDIA_SMI`, `CITAR_HELPER_POWERSHELL`, `CITAR_HELPER_SYSTEM_PROFILER` in `dev-guard` and test builds only; the fake is a small test binary. **Differs in one point:** honoured under `dev-guard` as well as test features, so the Python end-to-end test can use the binary `xtask develop` builds; release builds never honour them | P4.3.5; 4-06 gate 3 |
| Safety hardening: the default home holds the owner's data, the loopback guard only in test builds, S10 misses one `servers.json`, an elevated shell would install for real, WSL session lifetime, WSL mirrored networking | `dev-guard` in every `xtask develop` build (loopback only, explicit `--home` outside the per-user base); S10 fingerprints `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CITAR\config\servers.json` (`2561079bd2f123e1`); install tests assert not elevated; the WSL service gate in one invocation with a `trap`; `0.0.0.0` in WSL only when the networking mode is `nat` | P4.3.1, P4.3.2, P4.7.1, P4.7.5, P4.7.6; 4-03 gate 5, 4-14 gates 2, 4 |
| Paused time and deadlines: a yielding GPU machine misses deadlines and collects abandons; deadlines ignore the executor's share; running jobs contaminate benchmarks when `yield_to_llm` is off | `paused_s` in progress and results; deadlines extended by paused time up to twice; pause-caused expiries never abandons; deadlines from the reference cost scaled by the executor's share and measured speed; `jobs_pause` to a machine whenever a benchmark lease goes live | P4.2.5, P4.3.6, P4.5.5; 4-08 gate 4, 4-09 gates 4, 6, 4-10 gate 3 |
| `helper.js` offers the arm64 Mac build to Intel Macs without client hints | Both macOS downloads offered as primary choices when there is no architecture hint; a node test with a Safari user agent | P4.0, P4.5.11, P4.7.7 (H6); 4-14 |
| Result write order unspecified (a crash between append and commit duplicates or orphans a line) | Commit `accepted` first, append idempotently by (exp, i, run), reconcile files with the database at start; a gate injecting a crash between the commit and the append | P4.5.7; 4-09 gate 3 |
| Plan §6.7's rule unstated for model results from unverified helper machines | Rule 11: model results from helper machines count on the global leaderboards only when the machine was trusted, a site machine or an administrator's when they ran; the rest are badged and left out until an administrator trusts the machine. **Differs in one point:** administrators' own machines count without promotion, because an LLM-only machine has no CPU jobs to be promoted by (the owner's Framework Desktop is one). Folded into owner question 2 | P4.5.8 rule 11; 4-12 gate 2 |

**Also changed on the way:** the `hold:S` fault mode (the credit attack the review described, now gated); the job state `verifying` renamed `settling` and hidden from non-administrators; P4.9's risks 1, 4 and 7 updated for the jobs build, the verifier caps and profiles; the decisions list gained 29-31.

## Appendix E: Phase 4 work breakdown

Each package is sized for one implementation session (a Phase 3 package or smaller: about 1,800 lines of code at most besides its tests, as 2-11 was), starts from the previous package's merge, and ends with its gates passing, then a review and a fix round. The standard gates S1-S10 (P4.7.5) are named where they apply; every other gate is local and can be rerun by a reviewer, and CI-only gates are named as such. Every route a package adds or changes gets the tests of P4.7.3 whether or not its gates name them, and every package keeps the safety rules of P4.7.1. The hub carries exactly one protocol at every merge (P4.11).

### 4-01: Protocol v2: the specification, the frame types on both sides, the shared fixtures

- **Depends on:** nothing (`v0.1.6` at `1d5ca71`)
- **Estimated size:** ~750 Rust (the crate's skeleton, `protocol.rs`, the matcher, the fixture runner) + ~500 Python (`helper_protocol.py`, the matcher, the fixture loader) + ~70 fixture files + PROTOCOL.md
- **Scope:** `crates/citar-helper` as a workspace member (library `citar_helper` and a binary that prints `--version` only), its row in the crate graph (and `citar-jobs`'s, reserved for 4-03 and 4-07), the xtask rules of P4.3.1 (no `unsafe` in the helper or outside citar-jobs' `sys` module; the test features and `dev-guard` never in shipped builds; the loopback guard itself is 4-03's code), `crates/citar-helper/PROTOCOL.md` (P4.2 in full: every frame, its fields and types, who sends it and when, the reactions to `refused` and `shutdown` with the exit codes, the v1 refusal, the job rules of P4.2.5, the matcher language), `protocol.rs` (every frame as a serde type; unknown fields ignored; the 8 MB cap), fixtures under `crates/citar-helper/protocol/v2/` (valid and invalid frames for every type, the v1-shaped refusal among them; transcripts for the handshake with every `refused` code and the refusal of a 0.1.5 hello, settings, catalog, hardware, power, status, completions, cancel and the lost connection, jobs (offer to acknowledged result, rejects, abort, deadline, reconnects keeping and dropping runs, reconnect after a sent result, a duplicate result, a lost offer, a silent run, re-offer backoff, `jobs_pause`), and updates, written now and played by each side as its package arrives), `citar/server/helper_protocol.py` (builders and parsers for every frame, with per-type checks; `ProtocolError`), `tests/test_helper_protocol.py`, and the frame table parsed from PROTOCOL.md on both sides. Docs: none beyond PROTOCOL.md.
- **Gates:**
  1. Every valid frame parses on both sides and re-serializes to an equal JSON value; every invalid frame is refused on both sides with the reason its fixture names.
  2. The frame types each side knows equal PROTOCOL.md's table (a test on each side).
  3. Robustness: 2,000 random mutations of valid frames (keys dropped, types swapped, deep nesting, huge numbers, NaN-like strings) never panic the Rust parser (proptest) and raise nothing but `ProtocolError` in Python.
  4. The transcripts parse on both sides and their matchers are exercised by unit tests (each matcher accepting and refusing).
  5. S1-S4, S7, S9.

### 4-02: The schema and the one parser on the server (the hub untouched)

- **Depends on:** 4-01
- **Estimated size:** ~550 Python (the revision 200, `models.py` 50, `completion.py` 250 moved out of `openai_provider.py`, `OpenAIConversation` 50) + ~450 tests (the parsing corpus 300, migrations 150)
- **Scope:** revision `0016c_helper_jobs` (P4.5.4: every Phase 4 table and column, `job_costs` included) and `models.py` to match; `VERIFICATION` gains `rejected`. `citar/agents/providers/completion.py`: `parse_completion` extracted from `OpenAIConversation.step` without change of behaviour, and called by it (P4.5.2). **The hub, the Python worker and the scheduler are not touched:** the hub stays v1 until 4-05, and the worker, which reuses `OpenAIConversation` (`agent.py:301-341`), reaches the parser unchanged. A corpus of raw completions as files (`tests/data/completions/*.json`: native calls; invalid JSON arguments; merged XML repaired; XML, GLM, plain and Gemma 4 text calls; the JSON protocol; reasoning; no tool call), each with the `StepResult` and stored assistant entry the code gave before the extraction (the corpus is committed before the code moves); 4-05's parity gate reuses it. Docs: `ARCHITECTURE.md` (one parser on the server).

### 4-03: The Rust helper: configuration, the connection, keys, settings, catalog

- **Depends on:** 4-02
- **Estimated size:** ~1,350 Rust (cli and config 400, home and log 150, session and reconnect 400, key with its file permissions 150, discovery and catalog 200, quiet 50) + ~650 Rust tests (the in-process transcript server and the session half of the conformance runner among them)
- **Scope:** P4.3.2, P4.3.3, P4.3.7 (the key; signing comes with 4-11) and the discovery half of P4.3.4: `helper.json`/`worker.json`/`CITAR_WORKER_*`/flags compatibility (first recording `build_config`'s answers for a table of configurations into `tests/data/helper_config_cases.json` from the Python worker, which exists until 4-05), `--home`, `dev-guard` (loopback only; an explicit `--home` outside the per-user base), the first-run prompt and `setup` with the CPU question, the local CPU switch off by default and `--cpu`, quiet hours with v1's parser, `--ca-file` and the restricted `--insecure`, the redacting log, the session with tokio-tungstenite and rustls' platform verifier, the handshake and every `refused` and `shutdown` reaction with the exit codes (78 from a console; `--service` idles and retries hourly), reconnect with jitter, the helper's own ping, the machine key (generated; 0600, or an owner-only DACL on Windows; refused at start when others can read it; a warning for a readable `helper.json`), `settings`/`settings_ack` with the local ceiling, model discovery for the four endpoint kinds and `catalog` on change, `status` for local quiet hours, `--hardware FILE` and `--no-hardware` (the Rust scan is 4-06's). Until 4-04 the hello says `llm.enabled: false`, and `cpu.enabled` stays false until 4-10. **citar-jobs is created holding only its `sys` module** (the Windows DACL calls; the limit calls join it in 4-08). `crates/citar-helper/tests/conformance.rs` with the in-process transcript server (it plays the server's frames and a fake endpoint, and checks every frame the helper sends); binary-level tests spawn the real binary against the transcript server on loopback. `cargo xtask develop` builds the helper (with `dev-guard`) into `citar-ext/`. Docs: `WORKERS.md` (the helper's configuration and first run, CPU lending off by default), `CONTRIBUTING.md` (building the helper, WSL).
- **Gates:**
  1. **Compatibility:** every case of `helper_config_cases.json` (helper.json alone; worker.json by `--config`; environment only; flags over file over environment; quiet strings across midnight and by weekday; the model list; `--no-hardware`; a missing token or server) gives the helper the same effective settings as the Python worker gave; a migrated 0.1.5 `helper.json` lends no CPU.
  2. **The session transcripts** pass from the helper's side: `refused token` and `key` exiting 78 from a console and, with `--service`, idling and retrying hourly (a fake clock); `protocol` waiting 10 minutes (fake clock) both on a v2 `refused protocol` and on the v1-shaped `error` and 4426 a 0.1.5 server sends; `rate` retrying after its delay; `shutdown` as a permanent refusal; a dropped socket reconnecting with backoff.
  3. **The real binary** against the transcript server on loopback (Windows, then WSL): it connects, sends its key (whose fingerprint equals `--version`'s), acknowledges settings with its ceiling (a site figure above it capped and listed in `capped`), reports a fake endpoint's models and a change of the loaded set within 35 s, and on `shutdown revoked` exits 78 without reconnecting.
  4. **Keys:** a new key file is 0600 on Unix and has an owner-only, non-inherited DACL on Windows (read back through the `sys` module); a key file the test opens to others (mode 644, or an ACE for Users on a file in its own temporary directory) is refused at start with the command that fixes it.
  5. **Isolation:** a run with `--home <tmp>` leaves every file outside `<tmp>` as it was (the S10 fingerprints, taken by the test itself); the `dev-guard` binary refuses `--server https://example.com` before dialling, and refuses to start with no `--home` or with one inside the per-user base (the test points `LOCALAPPDATA`, `XDG_DATA_HOME` and `HOME` at a temporary directory, so the real base is never even named).
  6. S1, S1b, S2, S4, S5, S7, S9, S10.

### 4-04: The Rust helper's LLM proxy and real cancel

- **Depends on:** 4-03
- **Estimated size:** ~900 Rust (the proxy 500, stream assembly 250, cancel and slots 150) + ~550 Rust tests (the fake streaming endpoint 200 among them)
- **Scope:** the proxy of P4.3.4 (streaming with delta assembly of content, reasoning and tool calls by index; the non-streaming path and the fallback for an endpoint that refuses streaming with tools; whitelisted params; the allowlist; local slots and quiet hours; timeouts; error codes; `done` with the raw message, `gen_s` and `ttft_s`); real cancel with `cancelled`; a lost connection cancelling every completion in flight (P4.2.4); the hello's `llm.enabled`. A Rust fake endpoint (SSE with per-delta delays, reasoning and tool-call deltas; it records each request, the tokens it sent and when its client went). **An opt-in real-model test** (`CITAR_TEST_LMSTUDIO=http://localhost:1234`, skipped otherwise; run in this package's gates): it reads LM Studio's model list (`/api/v0/models`) and checks `google/gemma-4-e2b` is loaded before and after, never loading, unloading or swapping anything; the helper, driven by the transcript server, answers a request with tools with a raw message; a long generation cancelled at 2 s. Docs: none (4-05 rewrites `WORKERS.md`'s model part).
- **Gates:**
  1. The completion and cancel transcripts pass from the helper's side: streamed and not, tool-call and reasoning deltas, usage present and absent, `busy`, `closed`, `unknown_model`, `timeout`, an endpoint error, cancel before the first token, mid-stream and after `done`; the lost connection (the fake endpoint sees its client gone within 1 s of the socket's close, and after the reconnect the next requests are not refused `busy`).
  2. **Cancel at the endpoint:** the fake endpoint's log shows the client gone within 1 s of the `cancel` and no token sent after it; `cancelled` carries the tokens received so far.
  3. Parameters outside the whitelist are dropped (logged once per name); nothing in a request changes the endpoint, the key or streaming.
  4. **The real model** (the opt-in test): `google/gemma-4-e2b` loaded before and after; a request with tools gets a `done` whose raw message holds a tool call or text; a 1,500-token generation cancelled at 2 s is acknowledged within 1 s, and three short requests after it take no longer than 1.5 x the median of three before it plus 1 s.
  5. S7, S9, S10.

### 4-05: The hub on v2 only; the Python worker retired

- **Depends on:** 4-04
- **Estimated size:** ~1,400 Python (the hub 450, `WorkerConversation` on v2 150, the scheduler 100, routes and records 150, the refusal 20, the CLI's and install scripts' pointers 80, `qa_phase3.py`'s switch 50, `qa_phase4.py` with `helper` and `llm-real` 250, the scripted endpoint's streaming 150) + ~100 JS + ~900 tests (`FakeHelper` 250, conformance 200, end to end 300, hub 150); deletes ~1,600 lines. At the upper bound: if it runs long, the key-reset and rescan routes and the card's helper display move to the start of 4-06 (nothing before 4-06 needs them: a fresh machine registers its own key); the hub's switch and the worker's deletion never split.
- **Scope:** the hub on v2 only (P4.5.1): P4.2.8's refusal as its first check, the rate limit before lookup over distinct failing token hashes with `ratelimit.client_ip`, `refused` frames, key registration and its refusal, `welcome` with settings, `hub.push_settings` from the settings route, `settings_ack` into the scheduler's capacity, `catalog`, `hardware` (3-18's `record_report`), `status` (quiet hours into the scheduler), `request`/`done`/`error`/`cancel`/`cancelled`, `last_ip` and `last_seen_at`. `WorkerConversation` on v2 (OpenAI-format tools, the JSON protocol in `json` mode, usage mapping) calling `parse_completion`. The scheduler's acknowledged cancel and `flags.cancel_unacked` (P4.5.3). `POST /api/machines/{id}/key/reset` (leaving the trust as it is) and `/rescan`; the card shows protocol, helper version and key fingerprint. **Retirement:** `citar/worker/` and `installer/helper_entry.py` deleted; the `worker` extra and `citar-worker` script removed from `pyproject.toml`; `citar worker` and `citar setup --worker` print where to download the helper and exit 2; `install.sh --worker` and `install.ps1 -Worker` the same (downloading it is Phase 5's); `tests/test_worker.py` split into `tests/test_hub.py` (what remains true of the hub) and the end-to-end tests. `tests/helperfake.py` (`FakeHelper`: plays transcripts against the real hub over a real socket) and `tests/test_helper_conformance.py` for the handshake, settings, catalog, hardware, status, completion and cancel transcripts; `tests/test_helper_e2e.py` (the real binary from `citar-ext/` against the real hub), with test.yml's `build-ext` job building the helper so they run on CI's three OSes (CI-only). The scripted endpoint streams and records disconnects (P4.7.6); `qa_phase3.py`'s helpers become the Rust binary with `--home` in the QA directory; `qa_phase4.py` with its parts `helper` and `llm-real`. KNOWN_ISSUES: "A cancelled request still runs on a helper" goes. Docs: `WORKERS.md` (the helper replaces the worker; a 0.1.5 system unit retries the refusal every 15 s until disabled; P4.2.8's runbook), `API.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md` (the hub on v2), `CONFIGURATION.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Gates:**
  1. The transcripts for the handshake (every `refused` code, the v1 refusal), settings, catalog, hardware, status, completions and cancel pass from the hub's side.
  2. **Parsing parity:** 4-02's corpus through `WorkerConversation` (a `FakeHelper`'s raw message) gives the same `StepResult`s and stored assistant entries as through `OpenAIConversation`; a `json`-mode seat's request through a helper carries `JSON_PROTOCOL` and the tool listing (the plan's defect; the test fails on 4-04's head).
  3. **Cancel:** a `FakeHelper` acknowledging within 50 ms frees the slot without draining (a ticket waiting behind it starts within 200 ms; the scheduler's `draining` counter stays 0); one that never acknowledges drains as in P3.3.3 and sets `flags.cancel_unacked`.
  4. **Keys:** the first hello registers its key; the same key reconnects; another key is `refused key` until the owner's reset (one audit row, the trust unchanged), after which it registers; the routes' access tests (stranger 404, visitor 401, grantee 403, moderator 404, owner and admin 200).
  5. **The rate limit:** 20 distinct bad tokens from one address, then the 21st attempt with a good token is `refused rate` and `authenticate` is not called (patched); one revoked token retried 40 times counts once, and another machine's good token from the same address connects; with `behind_proxy` off, a forged `X-Forwarded-For` does not change the address the limit counts.
  6. **The refusal:** a 0.1.5 hello (a fixture played by a `FakeHelper`), a hello with `protocol: 3` and one with neither key each get P4.2.8's message in a v1 `error` frame and close 4426, with no token lookup and no audit row; 100 of them from one address log one line.
  7. **End to end through the scheduler** (Windows, then WSL), the real binary: it registers its key (the card's fingerprint equals `--version`'s); a lease's steps and a ticket's are parsed on the server (a seat plays three turns with tool calls from the scripted endpoint); a cancelled ticket's slot is free within 1 s with no draining, and the endpoint's log shows the client gone within 1 s of the cancel and no token after it; a revoke drops it within a second (exit 78, no reconnect); it reconnects by itself after a server restart.
  8. `qa_phase3.py queue machines exit` pass with the Rust helper; `qa_phase4.py helper` passes; `qa_phase4.py llm-real` passes (4-04 gate 4's checks through the hub and the scheduler, with the tool call parsed on the server).
  9. One protocol: no `citar.worker`, `citar-worker` or `PROTOCOL_VERSION = 1` left in the tree outside the archive notes, and no v1 frame name handled in `citar/server` beyond the refusal (grep gates).
  10. The hub's overhead (P4.7.9) under 3 ms; the session's overhead under 2.87; S1, S1b, S2-S7, S9, S10.
