### 4-07: Jobs in Rust: the vocabulary, the lab game, reference games, the jobs build, the chain and the result hash

- **Depends on:** 4-06
- **Estimated size:** ~1,400 Rust (specs and validation 300, the lab game 450, reference games 150, hashes and the chain 150, the jobs build with the two build scripts 150, bindings 200) + ~300 Python + ~600 tests
- **Scope:** the `citar-jobs` crate (P4.4.1, P4.4.2, P4.4.4 without signing, P4.4.5): `LabSpec`, the typed `ReferenceSpec` and `ReferenceResult`, validation and bounds, canonical bytes and `spec_hash`; `lab::play` with the chain on and no budget; reference games as single baseline games without a budget; crash outcomes as messages; `errors` normalised; `result_hash`, `chain_check`; **the jobs build** (build scripts for citar-sim and citar-jobs with the shared `content_code.rs` machinery, the flavour); the canary (both checks) and the self-check function the job host will call; the `jobs` golden set in citar-jobs with its own test binary, run in both profiles, and determinism.yml running it on five targets in both; bindings in citar-py (`citar._engine.jobs`: `check_spec`, `run`, `result_hash`, `chain_check`, `jobs_build`) and `engine_api` wrappers; `lab.play()` on `engine_api.run_job` (first recording the Python results of six seeds as `tests/data/lab_results_*.json`, then deleting the Python result building). Docs: `ARCHITECTURE.md` (jobs, the jobs build), `BOTS.md` (what a lab result records; `errors` as messages).
- **Gates:**
  1. **Parity:** for six seeds (duels and small games, 40 rounds), the Rust `LabResult` equals the recorded Python result on every deterministic key (`errors` compared as messages).
  2. **Determinism:** one job twice in one process, in two processes, and in the `ci` and `release` profiles gives one chain head and one result hash; the `jobs` golden set passes in both profiles (blessed once, the bless commit saying why); WSL gives the same (S9); CI-only: determinism.yml on five targets, both profiles.
  3. **No clock in a result:** a lab job and a reference job played with a test hook sleeping between seat steps give the same result hashes as without it; no hashed result holds `seconds`, `cpu_s`, `started_ts`, `finished` or a trace (a schema test); a `ReferenceSpec` with a path, budget or workers key is refused.
  4. **The jobs build** changes when a citar-sim or citar-jobs source changes and between the `ci` and `release` flavours (tests over the build scripts' inputs), and is equal on Windows and in WSL for one source and profile (S9 compares).
  5. Specs with an unknown key, a value out of bounds, an unknown bot version or a hash that does not match are refused with their reasons, through Rust and through the binding.
  6. A result altered in one score, or a digest list altered in one round, fails `result_hash`/`chain_check` through the binding.
  7. The canary passes here, and each of its two checks fails on an injected value (a test switch).
  8. Speed (P4.7.9): a lab duel through `run_job` no slower than through the old `lab.play`, same run.
  9. S1, S1b, S2, S4, S5, S7, S9.

### 4-08: The job host and CPU limits per OS

- **Depends on:** 4-07
- **Estimated size:** ~1,100 Rust (the host 450, limits 350 with the `sys` additions about 150, the counting allocator 100, the parent side 200) + ~250 Python + ~550 tests
- **Scope:** P4.4.3 and the limits table of P4.3.6: `citar_jobs::host` (the JSON-lines loop, threads, abort, pause and resume, deadlines not counting paused time, progress with `paused_s` and `peak_mb`, heartbeat, `ready` with `jobs_build`, the refusal of another jobs build, the canary and the self-check deciding `verifiable`, stdin EOF), the counting `#[global_allocator]`, the classification of memory deaths, `limits::apply` per OS in the `sys` module with the xtask rule confining `unsafe` to it, `host::spawn` (with the `systemd-run --user --scope` wrapper where available), `citar/jobs/host.py` and `python -m citar.jobs host`, the helper's `job-host` subcommand and `--self-test`, the `test-jobs` kinds (`burn`: spin for N seconds; `alloc`: allocate in steps up to N MB). Docs: `WORKERS.md` (limits per OS, hard and soft).
- **Gates:**
  1. **Windows** (local): a host with `cpu_percent` set to 10% of the machine and two `burn` jobs for 8 s uses, by its own process CPU time, at most that share x 1.15 + 0.05 core-seconds per second, and at least 40% of the share; with `memory_mb` 200, an `alloc` job to 400 ends as `job_failed memory`, classified from the allocation-failure line, the parent restarts the host and the next job runs; `ready` reports hard, hard.
  2. **WSL:** under the scope the host reports hard, hard and the same two checks pass (the memory death classified from the cgroup's `oom_kill`); with the wrapper turned off (a test switch) it reports soft CPU and hard memory (`RLIMIT_DATA`), and the `alloc` job still ends as `memory`.
  3. **Results do not depend on limits:** the shortest golden lab job and a reference job run in a host capped at 10% and paused twice give the same result hashes as uncapped (Windows and WSL).
  4. **Pause and ending:** a paused run's round does not move for 2 s, then resumes, with `paused_s` reported and the run's deadline extended by it; abort ends a run within 1 s; a host whose parent dies exits within 2 s, leaving no process (checked by pid).
  5. **Memory counted:** an `alloc` job of 100 MB reports a peak within 10% of 100 MB, and two concurrent runs' peaks are kept apart.
  6. **Builds:** a host told another `jobs_build` refuses to start; a parent refuses a `ready` with another one; a host whose canary is forced off (a test switch) reports `verifiable: false` and refuses every job `unverifiable`; `citar-helper --self-test` prints its version, build, jobs build and target and passes.
  7. Every limit test stays within P4.7.1's bounds (at most two cores' worth, 10 s, 512 MB).
  8. CI-only: macOS reports soft, soft and its background priority (rust.yml's macOS leg).
  9. S1, S1b, S2, S4, S5, S7, S9, S10.

### 4-09: The dispatcher and the local executor; results into the lab, the ratings and the ledger

- **Depends on:** 4-08
- **Estimated size:** ~1,500 Python (store 250, dispatcher 550, local executor 200, lab changes 150 and deletions, ledger 100, the cost table 50, routes 100, QA part 100) + ~200 JS + ~850 tests. At the upper bound: if it runs long, split after the store, the dispatcher and the local executor with results and the ledger (4-09a) and before the routes, pages and the QA part (4-09b).
- **Scope:** P4.5.5, P4.5.6 (local), P4.5.7: `citar/jobs/{store,dispatcher,local}.py`; jobs from the lab's queue with the experiment `kind` (`lab`, `ranking`, `reference`; the last two admins' and `collection`); the host's CPU defaults (server mode min(1,024 MB, 25% of the RAM)); quiet hours; benchmark leases (no offer, and `pause` to the local host); deadlines from the cost table scaled by the executor's share, extended by paused time; retries; memory failures raising `mem_mb`, and matching by memory per thread; the cost table seeded from a committed measurement of the golden and benchmark jobs (`citar/jobs/costs.json`) and updated from runs; crashes; results committed first and appended idempotently, with the reconciliation at start; reference lines; experiment completion; ledger events per run (P4.5.7, `cpu_core_s` bounded by observation and by the reference cost) and `ingest_lab` removed; the old runner deleted (P4.5.6) and `citar lab run` retired; `stop`/`resume`; `lab.overview()` from the dispatcher; `GET /api/lab/jobs`, `GET /api/admin/jobs`; the Lab page's per-game executor; the Queue page's CPU jobs for the host; `qa_phase4.py lab`. Docs: `BOTS.md`, `BOT_TUNING.md` (how to run the lab now), `API.md`, `CONFIGURATION.md` (the host's CPU settings), `ARCHITECTURE.md`.
- **Gates:**
  1. An experiment of six 30-round duels runs to its end on the local executor: six result lines, each with `executor.machine` the host and `verification: reference`; six `cpu` events with the experiment's owner as consumer and no provider; the ratings include its games; the experiment moves to `done/`.
  2. A ranking and a reference experiment (an administrator's) run too: `collection` (no consumer), the reference lines in `saves/lab/reference/` read by `summarize.py`.
  3. **Restart and the order of writing:** the server killed with two runs in flight comes back and re-runs exactly those two, with no duplicate line; a crash injected between the commit and the append (a test hook) leaves exactly one line after the restart; a line whose job is not accepted is removed at start.
  4. Priority, round-robin across owners, `stop` (running runs finish, nothing new starts) and `resume`; a benchmark lease going live pauses the local host's games, and its end resumes them.
  5. **Memory:** a job whose `mem_mb` exceeds the executor's memory per thread is not offered to it (it stays queued, its reason shown to administrators); a `memory` failure raises the job's `mem_mb` to the peak reached.
  6. **Deadlines** from the cost table and the executor's share (a table of cases), extended by paused time up to twice, and a pause-caused expiry not counted as an abandon.
  7. The old runner is gone: no `taskkill`, `Get-CimInstance`, `_Adopted` or `lab/jobs` paths left (a grep gate); the lab's tests updated.
  8. Access tests for the two routes; the dispatcher's tick under 50 ms (P4.7.9).
  9. `qa_phase4.py lab` passes; browser H3 (the host part) and H4 (executors).
  10. S1, S1b, S2-S6, S10.

### 4-10: Remote executors: CPU jobs on helpers

- **Depends on:** 4-09
- **Estimated size:** ~800 Rust + ~900 Python + ~600 tests
- **Scope:** P4.3.6 on the helper (the job host as its child under the effective limits and the local switch; relaying job frames; `yield_to_llm`; `jobs_pause` and `jobs_resume`; deadlines with paused time; results kept in `helper/results/` until acknowledged, `hello.results` and `jobs.resend`; rejects; offers' time to live), the hello's `cpu` block; the hub's job frames to the dispatcher; the dispatcher's remote executor adapter and eligibility (P4.5.5: jobs build, kinds, the local switch, memory, quiet hours, benchmark leases with `jobs_pause`, grants listing `lab`, the administrators' bypass not applying, owners' own machines, `collection` opt-in), acknowledgements and duplicates, offer expiry, silent-run expiry, re-offer backoff, abandons; `admission._purpose_ok`'s change and the grants dialog's "Lab (CPU work)". **Until 4-11, remote work is offered only to machines an administrator set `trusted`** (the verification machinery lands next), and their results are accepted `unverified`. Ledger events with the machine's owner as provider. The card's and dialog's CPU in effect, hard and soft; "not lent by this machine's owner"; the grants that include `lab`; the dialog's note goes; `yield_to_llm`; `GET /api/machines/{id}/jobs`. `qa_phase4.py remote` (and `--wsl`). Docs: `WORKERS.md` (lending CPU: off until the owner turns it on, grants), `API.md`, KNOWN_ISSUES ("CPU sharing waits" goes; macOS's soft limits added).
- **Gates:**
  1. **The job transcripts pass from both sides:** offer, accept, progress, result, acknowledgement; reject for build, kind, quiet, busy, memory and a bad spec hash; abort; a deadline; a reconnect that keeps a running run and one that drops it; **reconnect after send** (the connection dropped right after a `job_result` and before its ack: the result is resent after the welcome's `jobs.resend`, accepted once, one line); a duplicate (identical: acknowledged again; different: a claim failure recorded); **a lost offer** (no accept within the time to live: expired and offered elsewhere; a late accept gets `job_abort`); a silent run expired; re-offers backing off.
  2. **End to end on Windows:** a trusted helper machine whose owner lent CPU locally, with 2 threads, 20% CPU and 512 MB, runs eight 30-round games of another member's experiment (that member given `run_lab` by an administrator, and a grant listing `lab` on the machine's group): eight lines with `executor.machine` that machine, eight events given by its owner and used by the experiment's owner; the dialog shows the effective limits, hard; the job host's CPU over the run stays under its cap plus slack, and every run's round advances at least every 30 s.
  3. **Yield and benchmarks:** while a request streams at the scripted endpoint, the running game's round stops moving, and moves again within 2 s of the `done`, with `paused_s` reported and the deadline extended; with `yield_to_llm` off, a benchmark lease going live on the machine sends `jobs_pause` and the games stop, then resume at its end.
  4. **Eligibility:** one test per refusal reason (offline, CPU off on the site, CPU off on the machine, kind not opted in, another jobs build, not enough memory per thread, quarantined owner, quiet hours, a live benchmark lease, no grant, a grant without `lab`, a grant with an empty purposes list, an administrator's own experiment on a member's machine without a grant), each leaving the job queued for another executor.
  5. `qa_phase4.py remote --wsl`: the same experiment with the server in WSL and the Windows helper (topology of P4.7.6), or the fallback, recorded.
  6. S1, S1b, S2-S7, S9, S10.

### 4-11: Proof of work: signed claims, verification, trust tiers, quarantine, withdrawal

- **Depends on:** 4-10
- **Estimated size:** ~500 Rust (signing, `verify_claim`, the faults) + ~1,200 Python (trust 450, the dispatcher's verification 350, withdrawal and re-opening 250, ledger 150) + ~900 tests
- **Scope:** P4.3.7 (signing), P4.4.4 (claims, `verify_claim`), P4.4.6 (`test-faults`, every mode), P4.5.8 rules 1-10: replication decided after the primary's result, keyed sampling, verifiers (the local executor and administrator-marked machines), the caps on `new` machines, settling, mismatches with evidence and the tiebreak, promotion, quarantine of the machine and, on tampering, of its owner, withdrawal back to `trust.since`, a quarantined verifier's matches re-opened and promotions undone, the ledger's `pending`/`verified`/`unverified`/`rejected` with rejected credits at 0, rollups recomputed and budget settlements reversed; the visibility rule in the existing routes and pages; the end of 4-10's "trusted only" rule; `PUT /api/admin/machines/{id}/trust` with `withdraw` and `verifier`; `POST /api/admin/machines/{id}/replicate`. Docs: `WORKERS.md` (verification, what a lender's tier means), `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `API.md`.
- **Gates:**
  1. **Unit:** the tier machine's table (each transition, each threshold, release, over-reporting blocking promotion, a match against a non-verifier not counted, a key reset keeping the tier); **sampling** over 10,000 job ids, for each rate p of 0.02 and 0.10 and two secrets: |observed − p| ≤ 4·sqrt(p(1−p)/n), and the two secrets sample different jobs; claims verify, and a changed byte in any claim field, the result, a digest or the signature fails with its reason.
  2. **Caught while new:** a helper with `--test-fault result:1` is quarantined at its first job with the verdict "same chain, different result: tampering or an incompatible build"; its result never reaches the results file; its event is `rejected` with no credits; its owner's `trust_tier` is `quarantined`, and a second machine of that owner is refused CPU work. `digest:12`'s evidence names round 12 and quarantines the machine only; `sig`'s is a bad signature; `abandon:1` re-queues the job and counts an abandon.
  3. **Caught by sampling, everything withdrawn:** with `provisional_rate` 0, a helper with `result-at:6,9` reaches `provisional` after five matches; job 6 (altered) is accepted `unverified`; an administrator's `replicate 1` makes job 7 (honest) a verified match; job 8 is accepted `unverified`; with the rate set to 1.0, job 9 is replicated and mismatches. The machine is quarantined, and jobs 6 and 8 (6 before the verified match included) leave the results file, run again elsewhere and are accepted, their old events are `rejected` with their budget settlements reversed (the experiment runs under a budgeted grant), and the ratings no longer count them.
  4. **Visibility:** while a primary is in flight, its job's listing for its owner (as the machine's owner and as an experiment viewer) through `GET /api/lab/jobs`, `GET /api/machines/{id}/jobs` and the Queue page's data is identical whether or not it will be sampled; a replica's creation follows its primary's committed result (timestamps and a test hook).
  5. **Collusion and load:** a trusted machine of another owner not marked `verifier` never gets a replica, and no match counts against it; a third `new` machine of one owner waits for work; with one local thread and both `new` replicas and other work waiting, the host alternates; quarantining a `verifier` returns the jobs it verified to `unverified` and re-replicates them, and a machine promoted on its matches returns to `new`.
  6. **Promotion:** an honest helper with the thresholds lowered to 3 and 6 goes `new` → `provisional` → `trusted`, with an audit row each.
  7. Quarantine refuses the machine's work for other members (a pooled ticket from another member refused `quarantined`; its owner's own still served); release returns it to `new` with `since` reset.
  8. Access tests for the changed and new routes; `qa_phase4.py verify` (its tier, quarantine and withdrawal checks); S1, S1b, S2-S7, S9, S10.

### 4-12: Proof of work, continued: credits by reference cost, model results by tier, the admin UI

- **Depends on:** 4-11
- **Estimated size:** ~700 Python (credits and the class check 200, model results by tier 200, routes 150, the evidence views 150) + ~400 JS + ~500 tests
- **Scope:** P4.5.7's credit bound by the reference cost, `flags.capped_cpu`, `flags.overreported_cpu` blocking promotion, `trust.speed` and `flags.class_suspect`; P4.5.8 rule 11 (model results from helper machines on the global leaderboards by tier, with the "unverified machine" badge); the verification routes of P4.5.10 (`GET`/`PUT /api/admin/verification`, `GET /api/admin/jobs` with verifiers and the caps); `#/admin/verification`; the card's trust, evidence, measured speed, `verifier` mark and admin controls; the Lab page's badges and "waiting for a verifier". Docs: `SECURITY.md` (P4.6), `WORKERS.md` (credits for CPU work), `API.md`, KNOWN_ISSUES.
- **Gates:**
  1. **Credits:** `cpu:3` earns at most 1.5 x the reference cost, sets `overreported_cpu` and blocks promotion; `hold:S` earns at most 1.5 x the reference cost and sets `capped_cpu` only; an honest machine capped to a quarter of a core sets `capped_cpu` only and is still promoted; a class whose machines' measured speeds differ by more than 2x flags the slow one `class_suspect`.
  2. **Model results by tier:** a benchmark run on a `provisional` member's helper machine is badged and left out of the global board; one on an administrator's machine or a `trusted` one counts; trusting the machine includes its earlier results.
  3. Access tests for every new route (admin 200, member's "not found", visitor 401, moderator); `PUT /api/admin/verification`'s bounds.
  4. `qa_phase4.py verify` complete; browser H5, H1 (trust and key) and H4 (badges).
  5. S1, S1b, S2-S6, S10.

### 4-13: Signed self-update

- **Depends on:** 4-12
- **Estimated size:** ~900 Rust (the updater 450, the supervisor and rollback 150, xtask commands 300) + ~300 Python (the relay) + ~450 tests
- **Scope:** P4.3.8 and P4.5.9: the manifest and its signature, `update-keys.pub` (empty), `test-update-key` (`--update-key`, `CITAR_HELPER_VERSION`), the helper's update steps with `--self-test`, redirects over `https` only (and loopback `http` in test builds), the hand-off (`restarting`, the socket closed first, the `pending` and `healthy` markers, the console supervisor, exit 75 in service mode, rollback by the supervisor and by the start count), the job host stopped and build-checked, the `packaged` feature (`notify`, `auto` refused), `update rollback`, `update_status`; `cargo xtask keygen`, `helper-manifest`, `sign-manifest` with their refusals; the server's relay with `helper_manifest_url` and `helper_manifest_file`, `engine_api.verify_manifest` and the test-ops key; welcome's and `update` frames; the card's update state; `qa_phase4.py update`. Docs: `WORKERS.md` (updates and how to turn them off), `SECURITY.md` (the update channel), `CONFIGURATION.md`.
- **Gates:**
  1. **An update with a throwaway key** generated in the test's temporary directory: a helper built as `0.1.6-test.1` and copied into a temporary install directory (never the build output) is offered `0.1.6-test.2` (a second build) by a local server whose manifest file was signed with that key and whose asset is served on loopback behind a redirect; it downloads, checks, self-tests, hands off and reconnects reporting the new version and `applied`; the server never sends `replaced` (the old socket closed first).
  2. **Refusals,** each leaving the installed binary's sha256 unchanged and reported `refused` with its reason: a bad signature, an unknown key, a wrong sha256, a wrong size, a downgrade, the same version, a non-loopback `http` URL, a redirect to one, a missing target, policy `off`, `auto` in a `packaged` build; `notify` reports `available` and downloads nothing; a build with no trusted key reports `unavailable`.
  3. **Rollback:** a staged binary that fails `--self-test` is not swapped; in a console run, a swapped-in binary that exits before `healthy` (a test build made to exit at start) is swapped back by the supervisor, and the old one serves and reports `failed`; in service mode, with a test supervisor standing in for the service manager, a new binary that dies three starts in a row before `healthy` restores `.old`; `update rollback` restores `.old` by hand.
  4. **No stale job host:** a job host left running across a swap (a test hook) has its `ready` refused by the new parent (another jobs build), which starts its own; no result from the old host is relayed after the swap.
  5. The relay: fetched at most hourly, a failed fetch keeps the last good one, an unverifiable manifest is never relayed; welcomes carry it.
  6. `xtask keygen` and `sign-manifest` refuse paths inside the repository; after the gates, `git ls-files` and a scan of the tree find no private key (no `*.key`, no ed25519 secret JSON, no `PRIVATE KEY` header).
  7. S1, S1b, S2-S5, S7, S9, S10.

### 4-14: Service installation, the release names, helper.yml, the helper docs

- **Depends on:** 4-13
- **Estimated size:** ~800 Rust (definitions 300, the Windows Run supervisor 150, the Windows service with its account and DACL 200, service mode 150) + ~250 workflow YAML + ~100 JS and Python + docs
- **Scope:** P4.3.9 (`service install|uninstall|status|run` with `--user`, `--system` and `--dry-run` per OS; the exit codes; service mode idling on a permanent refusal; systemd's `Restart=on-failure` with `RestartPreventExitStatus=78`; launchd's `KeepAlive` with `SuccessfulExit` false; `run --detach` and its supervisor; the Windows service as `NT SERVICE\citar-helper`, the binary copied into `%ProgramData%\CITAR\helper\bin`, the home's DACL, failure actions with the non-crash flag); release.yml's `build-helper` rewritten as P4.10's cargo matrix with the five unversioned names and their `.sha256` (it stays behind the Phase 5 gate); `helper.js` with five entries, both macOS downloads offered without an architecture hint, and no pipx sentence; `citar/web/tests/helper.test.mjs` holding `helper.js`'s names equal to the `asset:` entries of release.yml's helper matrix (read by pattern: the project has no YAML dependency, and the matrix lines are one per target) and a Safari user agent on a Mac offered both; `.github/workflows/helper.yml` (build and run the helper's and citar-jobs' tests, the conformance transcripts and the `jobs` golden set in both profiles on the five targets; Windows service install, start, connect to a loopback fake server, stop, uninstall; Linux `systemd --user` after linger; macOS launchd); `packaging/README.md` (what Phase 5 does for the helper, R4, the `packaged` feature); KNOWN_ISSUES (the Intel Mac item goes); `WORKERS.md` assembled into its final shape (the helper, CPU sharing, limits, verification, updates, services, troubleshooting, the 0.1.5 unit's retries, the Phase 6 runbook). Docs: `INSTALL.md` and `QUICKSTART.md` where they name the worker.
- **Gates:**
  1. `--dry-run` on each OS prints exactly the committed expected definitions (the unit file with its restart rules, the plist with `KeepAlive`, the `sc.exe` arguments with the virtual account and the failure actions with the non-crash flag, the home's DACL, the Run value with `--detach`), checked by unit tests that run on every OS.
  2. **WSL, in one `wsl` invocation under a `trap` that uninstalls:** `service install --user` starts the helper against a WSL dev server; it connects; `service status` says running; with its token revoked it idles (the unit's `NRestarts` stays 0 and the server sees one attempt); `uninstall` stops and removes it; `systemctl --user list-unit-files` shows no citar unit after.
  3. **The Run supervisor** (run in the foreground by the test through a test switch): it restarts its child at once after exit 75 and after a crash with backoff, stops on 0, and a revoked helper under it idles rather than exiting (one attempt an hour on a fake clock).
  4. **Windows, nothing installed:** the test first asserts the process is not elevated and aborts if it is; `service install --system` unelevated prints the elevated command and exits 2; `--user` only ever with `--dry-run`; S10's service, Run-key and task checks clean.
  5. The names test passes; `helper.yml` and `release.yml` parse; no feature on any build line of either (xtask).
  6. CI-only: `helper.yml` green on the five targets, its service legs included (the Windows service under its virtual account, launchd, `systemd --user`), at the merge push.
  7. Browser H6; S1, S1b, S2-S7, S9, S10.

### 4-15: Phase 4 exit: conformance, the remote experiment verified, the faulty helper, the real model, the evidence

- **Depends on:** 4-14
- **Estimated size:** ~850 Python (the `exit` and `real` parts, `all`) + docs
- **Scope:** `qa_phase4.py all` with `exit` and `real` (P4.7.6); the browser checklist; the P4.1.4 table measured; the docs pass: `CHANGELOG.md` (Compatibility: protocol v2 and the v1 refusal, the Python worker removed, the lab inside the server, CPU lending off by default, grants lending CPU only with `lab`, new routes; Added and Fixed by area, including P4.0's defects and the review's), `KNOWN_ISSUES.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md` (Phase 4 in one section), `CONTRIBUTING.md` (`qa_phase4.py`, WSL, the helper's dev loop), `API.md`, the wiki regenerated; "As built in 4-15" with the evidence.
- **Gates:**
  1. **Conformance on Windows x64 and Linux x64:** S7 and S9 green at the head; the transcripts from both sides; `tests/test_helper_e2e.py` on Windows and in WSL; CI-only: `helper.yml` and determinism.yml on all five targets at the merge push.
  2. **A remote lab experiment verified end to end** (`exit`, P4.7.6's topology, the extension and the helpers built in one profile, recorded): two members, bob given `run_lab` by an administrator and alice granting him `lab` (listed explicitly) on her Windows helper machine (CPU lent at setup; 2 threads, 25%, 512 MB) and her WSL helper machine (2 threads, 50%, 512 MB); bob's experiment of 24 games of 40 rounds, an administrator's ranking experiment of 6 and reference run of 4, all dispatched across the two helpers and the local executor. Every accepted line is signed by its machine (or the host's), its chain consistent, replicated on the local executor while its machine was new and sampled after; the replicated games' Windows and Linux results equal byte for byte; each game is in the results file once; the ratings include exactly the accepted lab and ranking games; the ledger's events by machine and verification equal the runs, alice's given and bob's used equal their sums (each bounded by the reference cost), replicas used by nobody; the limits held (hard on both) and were shown so.
  3. **A faulty helper quarantined:** a third member's helper (WSL, `--test-fault result:3`) joins as new and is quarantined at its third job with the evidence shown on `#/admin/verification`, and that member's second machine is refused CPU work; in a second pass, a fourth member's helper with `result-at:` faults on either side of an administrator-requested replica reaches `provisional`, is caught by sampling, and every unverified result it gave is withdrawn (one from before its verified match included), re-run and accepted elsewhere, with the ledger, the budget and the ratings following.
  4. **The real model** (`real`): LM Studio's `google/gemma-4-e2b` (loaded before and after; never loaded, unloaded or swapped) through the Windows helper: a game of 6 rounds with alice's person seat, an LLM seat and a hybrid seat (prompted every 2 turns) and two bots; the LLM seat's turns end by its own `end_turn`; at least two chats answered by the models' own calls, parsed on the server; the ledger's generation seconds equal the scheduler's; a long generation cancelled at 2 s acknowledged within 1 s, the next requests within the same-run baseline (4-04's rule), no slot drained.
  5. **Self-update** with a throwaway key (`update`) and **the service** in WSL (`service`) pass inside `all`.
  6. `qa_phase4.py all` exits 0; `qa_phase3.py all --skip real` exits 0 with the Rust helper (Phase 3's regression).
  7. The browser checklist H1-H7, every item with its screenshots.
  8. The P4.1.4 table, each row measured, in the as-built note; S1, S1b, S2-S7, S9, S10 at the head; the session's overhead; CI-only: test.yml, rust.yml, determinism.yml, helper.yml at the merge push.
